DECENNIUM DESCENT DIRECTOR REVIEW PACKET

Exact text from approved V6 DOCX, not the former stale extract. No new rules are asserted by this packet. Line numbers refer to the extraction saved as docs/reference/Player_Rulebook_v6_source_text.txt.


Chapter 1 advancement context
125: "Zeetes packed everything he thought might be useful," Shawn explains, "including several half-finished inventions that he's sure will come in handy... if he can just get them working properly. His equipment is meticulously organized but includes many experimental items that don't always function as intended."
126: 
127: 6. Come Together
128: Shawn and the other players discuss how their characters met and why they've decided to work together. They might decide that the more experienced adventurers found Zeetes after one of his inventions malfunctioned spectacularly, or perhaps they sought him out specifically because they needed someone who could disable a complex mechanical trap.
129: 
130: "I think it would be fun if the other characters found Zeetes trying to test one of his inventions, a mechanical monster detector, which was going haywire and actually attracting monsters instead of detecting them," Shawn suggests. "He's eager to join them because he realizes his inventions need real-world testing, and they might see value in having someone who can create useful gadgets, even if he is completely inexperienced."
131: 
132: Beyond 1st Level
133: 
134: As your character goes on adventures and overcomes challenges, they gain experience. Opening campaigns use milestone advancement (see Advancement in Opening Campaigns below). Experience points and the Character Advancement table remain available when your Director opts into XP tracking: a character who reaches a specified experience point total advances in capability. This advancement is called gaining a level.
135: 
136: When your character gains a level, their class often grants additional features, as detailed in the class description. Some of these features allow you to increase your ability scores, either increasing two scores by 1 each or increasing one score by 2. You can't increase an ability score above 20. In addition, every character's proficiency bonus increases at certain levels.
137: 
138: Each time you gain a level, you gain 1 additional Hit Die. Roll that Hit Die, add your Constitution modifier to the roll, and add the total to your hit point maximum. Alternatively, you can use the fixed value shown in your class entry, which is the average result of the die roll (rounded up).
139: 
140: When your Constitution modifier increases by 1, your hit points maximum increases by 1 for each level you have attained. For example, if your 7th-level Tinker has a Constitution score of 18, when they reach 8th level, they increase their Constitution score from 17 to 18, thus increasing their Constitution modifier from +3 to +4. Their hit point maximum then increases by 8.
141: 
142: The Character Advancement table summarizes the XP you need to advance in levels from level 1 through level 20, and the proficiency bonus for a character of that level. Consult the information in your character's class description to see what other improvements you gain at each level.
143:

153: | Charisma | Confidence, eloquence, leadership | Bard, Paladin, Oracle, Crimson Agent | Tiefling (+2), Imperial human (+2), Drakari (+1) |

192: Advancement in Opening Campaigns
193: For Opening campaigns (the full Decennial Opening — including Expeditions that push past the Bar of Last Refuge and continue toward deeper Gates), milestone advancement is the official method: the Director awards a level when the Expedition hits a Gate seal, completes a major arc beat, or reaches another announced milestone. Tell the table which milestones grant levels before the Opening begins. Deeper floors do not change this method.
194: The Character Advancement table remains canon for total character level, proficiency bonus, and for tables that opt into XP tracking. This book does not publish creature or encounter XP awards at any depth. Any companion Session CR→XP helper is optional and non-canon — it never replaces Director milestones for Opening campaigns and does not write XP to your character sheet automatically.
195: 


Complete Chapter 3 classes
1642: Chapter 3: Classes
1643: 
1644: Adventurers are extraordinary people, driven by a thirst for excitement into a life that others would never dare lead. They are heroes, compelled to explore the dark places of the world and take on the challenges that lesser women and men can't stand against.
1645: 
1646: Class is the primary definition of what your character can do. It's more than a profession; it's your character's calling. Class shapes the way you think about the world and interact with it and your relationship with other people and powers in Aethel. A soldier, for example, might view the world in pragmatic terms of strategy and maneuvering, and see herself as just a pawn in a much larger game. A cleric, by contrast, might see himself as a willing servant in a god's unfolding plan or a conflict brewing among various deities. While the soldier has contacts in a mercenary company or army, the cleric might know a number of priests, paladins, and devotees who share his faith.
1647: 
1648: Your class gives you a variety of special features, such as a soldier's mastery of weapons and armor, and a wizard's spells. At low levels, your class gives you only two or three features, but as you advance in level you gain more and your existing features often improve. Each class entry in this chapter includes a table summarizing the benefits you gain at every level, and a detailed explanation of each one.
1649: 
1650: Adventurers sometimes advance in more than one class. A thief might switch direction in life and swear the oath of a paladin. A barbarian might discover latent magical ability and dabble in the wizard class while continuing to advance as a barbarian. Elves are known to combine martial mastery with magical training and advance as warriors and wizards simultaneously. Optional rules for combining classes in this way, called multiclassing, appear in chapter 6.
1651: 
1652: Seventeen classes, listed in the Classes table, are found throughout Aethel and define the spectrum of typical adventurers.
1653: 
1654: Classes Table
1655: 
1656: | Class | Description | Hit Die | Primary Ability | Saving Throw Proficiencies | Armor and Weapon Proficiencies |
1657: |-------|-------------|--------|-----------------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|
1658: | Bard | An inspiring magician whose power echoes the music of creation | d8 | Charisma | Dexterity & Charisma | Light armor, simple weapons, hand crossbows, longswords, rapiers, shortswords |
1659: | Cleric | A priestly champion who wields divine magic in service of a higher power | d8 | Wisdom | Wisdom & Charisma | Light and medium armor, shields, simple weapons |
1660: | Crimson Agent | A versatile combatant who blends martial prowess with arcane techniques | d8 | Dexterity & Intelligence | Dexterity & Intelligence | Light/medium armor, simple and martial weapons |
1661: | Dragoon | A master of aerial combat who harnesses momentum and gravity | d10 | Strength & Dexterity | Strength & Dexterity | All armor, shields, simple and martial weapons |
1662: | Druid | A priest of the Old Faith, wielding the powers of nature and adopting animal forms | d8 | Wisdom | Intelligence & Wisdom | Light and medium armor (nonmetal), shields (nonmetal), clubs, daggers, darts, javelins, maces, quarterstaffs, scimitars, sickles, slings, spears |
1663: | Mage | A versatile arcane spellcaster with a broad range of magical abilities | d6 | Intelligence | Intelligence & Wisdom | Daggers, darts, slings, quarterstaffs, light crossbows |
1664: | Monk | A master of martial arts, harnessing the power of the body in pursuit of physical and spiritual perfection | d8 | Dexterity & Wisdom | Strength & Dexterity | Simple weapons, shortswords |
1665: | Mirrorsage | A specialist in reflection magic who manipulates light, images, and duplicates | d6 | Intelligence & Charisma | Intelligence & Charisma | Light armor, simple weapons, shortswords |
1666: | Oracle | A divinely inspired seer whose prophecies and visions guide their magic | d8 | Wisdom & Charisma | Wisdom & Charisma | Light armor, simple weapons |
1667: | Ranger | A warrior who uses martial prowess and nature magic to combat threats on the edges of civilization | d10 | Dexterity & Wisdom | Strength & Dexterity | Light and medium armor, shields, simple and martial weapons |
1668: | Shaman | A spiritual guide who communes with ancestral and elemental spirits | d8 | Wisdom | Wisdom & Constitution | Light/medium armor, shields, simple weapons |
1669: | Soldier | A disciplined warrior trained in military tactics and weapon mastery | d10 | Strength or Dexterity | Strength & Constitution | All armor, shields, simple and martial weapons |
1670: | Thief | A skilled infiltrator who uses stealth and trickery to overcome obstacles and enemies | d8 | Dexterity | Dexterity & Intelligence | Light armor, simple weapons, hand crossbows, longswords, rapiers, shortswords |
1671: | Tinker | An innovative creator who combines mechanical ingenuity with magical enhancement | d8 | Intelligence | Intelligence & Dexterity | Light armor, simple weapons, hand crossbows, light crossbows |
1672: | Warrior | A fierce combatant who relies on raw power and combat instinct | d12 | Strength | Strength & Constitution | All armor, shields, simple and martial weapons |
1673: | Wizard | A scholarly magic-user capable of manipulating the structures of reality | d6 | Intelligence | Intelligence & Wisdom | Daggers, darts, slings, quarterstaffs, light crossbows |
1674: | Paladin | A holy warrior bound to a sacred oath | d10 | Strength & Charisma | Wisdom & Charisma | All armor, shields, simple and martial weapons |
1675: 
1676: Building Zeetes, Continued
1677: 
1678: As we saw in chapter 1, Shawn is creating Zeetes, a gnome tinkerer who has recently left his workshop to seek adventure and inspiration. Now that she's chosen his race, he needs to select a class that fits her character concept. Given Zeetes' background as an inventor and his natural curiosity about mechanical devices, Shawn decides that the Tinker class is the perfect fit. This class will allow Zeetes to create useful gadgets and infuse them with magical properties, reflecting his lifelong passion for invention. As a 1st-level Tinker, Zeetes gains several class features, including the ability to create simple mechanical devices and the Tinker's Intuition feature that helps him understand and repair complex mechanisms. Shawn notes these features on her character sheet, along with Zeetes' proficiencies in light armor, simple weapons, hand crossbows, light crossbows, and tinker's tools.
1679: 
1680: "I think Zeetes will approach adventuring like an experiment," Shawn says. "He'll be constantly taking notes on everything he encounters and thinking about how he could build devices to solve the problems the party faces. He might not be the strongest fighter, but his gadgets will give the group options they wouldn't otherwise have."
1681: 
1682: Class Features
1683: 
1684: As a member of a class, you gain a variety of features that define your capabilities. When you gain a new level in a class, you get additional features from your class as shown in the class description. Some features indicate a level at which you gain an enhanced version of the feature. For example, a monk gains Unarmored Movement at 2nd level and then gains an enhancement to that feature at 9th level.
1685: 
1686: Hit Points
1687: 
1688: Your hit points define how tough your character is in combat and other dangerous situations. Your hit points are determined by your Hit Dice (short for Hit Point Dice).
1689: 
1690: Hit Dice: Each class has a Hit Die, a die of a specific size that determines how many hit points you gain when you level up. For example, a warrior uses a d12, while a wizard uses a d6. When you gain a level, you roll your class's Hit Die, add your Constitution modifier, and add the total to your hit point maximum. Alternatively, you can use the fixed value shown in your class entry, which is the average result of the die roll (rounded up).
1691: 
1692: Hit Points at 1st Level: When you create your character, you determine your hit points by adding your Constitution modifier to the highest possible total of your class's Hit Die. For example, if you're a warrior with a Constitution score of 16 (modifier of +3), your starting hit points would be 15 (12 + 3).
1693: 
1694: Hit Points at Higher Levels: Each time you gain a level, you roll your class's Hit Die (or take the average value). Add your Constitution modifier to the roll and add the total to your hit point maximum.
1695: 
1696: Proficiency Bonus
1697: 
1698: The table that appears in your class description shows your proficiency bonus, which is +2 for a 1st-level character. Your proficiency bonus applies to many of the numbers you'll be using for your character:
1699: 
1700: Attack rolls using weapons you're proficient with
1701: Attack rolls with spells you cast
1702: Ability checks using skills you're proficient in
1703: Ability checks using tools you're proficient with
1704: Saving throws you're proficient in
1705: Saving throw DCs for spells you cast
1706: 
1707: Your class determines your weapon and armor proficiencies, your saving throw proficiencies, and some of your skill and tool proficiencies. Your background gives you additional skill and tool proficiencies, and some races give you more proficiency. Be sure to note all of your proficiencies on your character sheet. Your proficiency bonus can't be added to a single die roll or other number more than once. Occasionally, your proficiency bonus might be modified (doubled or halved, for example) before you apply it. If a circumstance suggests that your proficiency bonus applies more than once to the same roll or that it should be multiplied more than once, you nevertheless add it only once, multiply it only once, and halve it only once.
1708: 
1709: Skill Trees and Specialization
1710: 
1711: One of the defining features of Decennium Descent's class system is the skill tree. Beginning at 1st level, each class has access to three distinct specialization paths, represented by skill trees. As you advance in level, you can invest skill points into these trees, unlocking new abilities and enhancing existing ones and if you plan correctly, you might be able to discover a Prestige Class!!!
1712: 
1713: Each skill tree represents a different approach to your class's core concept. For example, a Soldier might choose between skill trees focusing on defense, two-handed weapons, or battlefield control. This system allows for tremendous customization within each class, ensuring that no two characters of the same class need be alike.
1714: 
1715: The skill tree system works as follows:
1716: 
1717: You gain skill points as you level up, which can be invested in your class skill trees
1718: Each skill tree contains multiple tiers of abilities, with higher tiers requiring investment in lower tiers first
1719: You can invest in multiple skill trees, creating hybrid specializations
1720: Some abilities have prerequisites beyond tier placement, such as minimum ability scores or other specific abilities
1721: At higher levels, you can access prestige classes by meeting specific requirements within your skill trees
1722: 
1723: This system allows you to customize your character to match your preferred playstyle while maintaining the core identity of your chosen class.
1724: 
1725: Multiclassing
1726: 
1727: Decennium Descent allows characters to gain levels in multiple classes, representing the diversification of their training and abilities. Multiclassing enables the creation of unique character concepts that blend the features of different classes.
1728: 
1729: To multiclass, you must meet minimum ability score requirements for both your current class and the new class you wish to adopt. When you gain a level, you must choose which of your classes to advance, gaining all the benefits provided by a level in that class.
1730: 
1731: Multiclassing comes with both advantages and challenges:
1732: 
1733: Advantages
1734: Access to a wider variety of abilities and features
1735: Unique combinations that can create specialized character concepts
1736: Greater versatility in addressing different situations
1737: 
1738: Challenges
1739: Slower access to high-level abilities in any single class
1740: More complex character management
1741: Potential MAD (Multiple Ability Dependency) requiring good scores in several abilities
1742: 
1743: Specific rules for multiclassing, including how features like spellcasting and skill trees interact across classes, are detailed in the Multiclassing section at the end of this chapter.
1744: 
1745: Prestige Classes
1746: 
1747: As you advance in your base class and develop your character's abilities, you may qualify for prestige classes, elite specializations that represent the pinnacle of certain paths or combinations of skills. Prestige classes offer powerful abilities and unique features that aren't available to base classes. Unlike multiclassing, which allows you to diversify your character's abilities, prestige classes represent focused mastery and specialization. Each prestige class has specific prerequisites that must be met before you can take levels in it, such as minimum ability scores, skill tree investments, or specific accomplishments in the game world. Prestige classes are detailed in a separate chapter, but they're mentioned here because your choice of base class and how you develop your skill trees will determine which prestige classes become available to your character.
1748: 
1749: Classes and Society
1750: 
1751: Your class often influences how you are perceived in the world of Aethel. Some classes, like Soldiers and Clerics, are common and widely accepted across most societies. Others, like certain specializations of Wizards or Thieves, may face suspicion or prejudice in certain regions.
1752: 
1753: Class Organizations
1754: Many classes have associated organizations, guilds, or orders that train new members and provide community for established practitioners:
1755: 
1756: Military Academies and Mercenary Companies: Train Soldiers, Warriors, and Dragoons
1757: Arcane Colleges: Centers of magical research and education for Wizards, Mages, and Mirrorsages
1758: Clerical Hierarchies: Religious organizations structured around specific deities
1759: Druidic Circles: Secretive groups dedicated to preserving natural balance
1760: Thieves' Guilds: Underground networks for Thieves and similar classes
1761: Bardic Colleges: Institutions preserving lore and training performers
1762: Tinkers' Workshops: Collaborative spaces where inventors share ideas and resources
1763: 
1764: Class and Culture
1765: Different cultures may favor certain classes based on their values and environment:
1766: 
1767: Imperial human societies often value Wizards, Soldiers, and Clerics
1768: Nomadic human tribes commonly produce Warriors, Rangers, and Shamans
1769: Elven communities typically respect Mages, Druids, and Rangers
1770: Dwarven societies honor Soldiers, Clerics, and Tinkers
1771: Orcish tribes revere Warriors, Shamans, and Rangers
1772: 
1773: Hero Paths Are Philosophies, Not Moral Verdicts
1774: At 10th level, a Hero Path describes what a character has learned to do with power—not an objective cosmic judgment of whether that character is Good, Neutral, or Evil. A Saint can become authoritarian, a Cultist can make a terrible sacrifice to save others, and a path associated with fear or blood can still be used in defense of the living. Reputation follows deeds, witnesses, institutions, and consequences. The world may judge you; the rules do not pre-judge you.
1775: Using This Chapter
1776: 
1777: The following sections detail each of the seventeen base classes. For each class, you'll find:
1778: 
1779: Class Overview: A general description of the class, its role, and its place in the world
1780: Class Features: The mechanical abilities gained as the character advances in level
1781: Skill Trees: The three specialization paths available to the class
1782: Playing the Class: Advice on effective tactics, roleplaying, and character development
1783: Class Relationships: How the class typically interacts with other classes
1784: Notable NPCs: Famous examples of the class in the world of Aethel
1785: 
1786: When creating a character, read through the class descriptions to find one that matches your preferred playstyle and character concept. Consider not just the mechanical benefits but also how the class will influence your character's personality, background, and goals.
1787: 
1788: Remember that while classes provide structure and abilities, they don't dictate every aspect of your character. A Soldier might be a noble knight, a grizzled mercenary, or a tribal defender. Use the class as a foundation for your character, then build upon it with unique traits, goals, and quirks that make the character truly yours.# Bard
1789: 
1790: "The half-elf's fingers danced across her lute strings, weaving a melody that seemed to shimmer in the air. As she sang of ancient heroes and forgotten battles, her companions felt their weariness fade and their spirits lift. The wounds of their recent battle seemed less painful, their resolve strengthened by the power of her song."
1791: 
1792: Bards are versatile performers whose magic flows through music, poetry, and other artistic expressions. In the world of Aethel, bards serve as entertainers, historians, messengers, and magical practitioners whose power comes from the emotional resonance of their performances. Their unique blend of performance skills, arcane knowledge, and social prowess makes them adaptable adventurers capable of supporting allies, hindering enemies, and solving complex problems.
1793: 
1794: Class Overview
1795: 
1796: As a bard, you harness the fundamental power of artistic expression to create magical effects. Your performances can inspire courage, heal wounds, manipulate emotions, and even harm your enemies. Beyond your magical abilities, you serve as a repository of knowledge, stories, and cultural traditions, making you valuable in both social and practical situations.
1797: 
1798: In Aethel, bards can be found in virtually every society, from royal courts to frontier taverns. They might be wandering minstrels carrying news between isolated settlements, court performers entertaining nobility, lore keepers preserving cultural histories, or battle singers inspiring troops on the battlefield.
1799: 
1800: Core Mechanics
1801: 
1802: Crescendo – Each Bard ability played in rhythm without interruption adds 1 Crescendo stack (max 5). At 5 stacks, the next ability consumes them all for a bonus effect. Maintaining rhythm and building Crescendo is central to mastering the Bard class.
1803: 
1804: Primary Attributes
1805: Main Attribute: Charisma
1806: Secondary Attributes: Dexterity, Intelligence
1807: Hit Points: Medium (8 + Constitution modifier at 1st level, 1d8 per level thereafter)
1808: Armor Proficiency: Light armor
1809: Weapon Proficiency: Simple weapons, musical instruments as weapons
1810: Tool Proficiency: Three musical instruments of your choice
1811: 
1812: Progression Path
1813: 
1814: Level 1-2 (Base Bard Skills)
1815: Foundational skills before Arch class selection.
1816: 
1817: Passive Skills
1818: Verse Mastery: +5% damage on all Verses.
1819: Lingering Notes: Chants last 1 extra turn after being interrupted.
1820: Melodic Instinct: +5% initiative and movement speed.
1821: Echo Harmonization: Echo effects apply +1 turn duration.
1822: Performance Focus: Gain 10% reduced cooldown on Bard skills when at max Crescendo.
1823: 
1824: Active Skills
1825: Verse of Courage: A quick musical phrase that deals minor damage to a single target and grants +5% damage to an ally for 2 turns.
1826: Chant of Renewal: A sustained melody that heals nearby allies for a small amount each turn while maintained.
1827: Echo of Inspiration: Amplifies your last performed ability, causing its effects to repeat at 50% potency next turn.
1828: Dissonant Note: A jarring sound that interrupts enemy concentration and has a chance to briefly stun.
1829: Bardic Knowledge: Passive ability that grants insight into lore, history, and cultural information encountered during adventures.
1830: 
1831: Level 3-9 (Arch Class: Virtuoso)
1832: At level 3, bards specialize into the Virtuoso arch class, refining their musical talents and developing more powerful performances.
1833: 
1834: Passive Skills
1835: Fluid Tempo: Crescendo stacks no longer drop when hit.
1836: Verses Unbound: Verses can now crit, dealing x1.5 damage.
1837: Chorus Shield: Using a Chant grants a minor damage shield to self and nearest ally.
1838: Signature Sound: Choose a single element (Fire/Ice/Lightning). Your Verses deal +10% of that type.
1839: Perfect Pitch (Capstone): At 5 Crescendo, Verses also apply a debuff: -10% enemy resistances for 2 turns.
1840: 
1841: Active Skills
1842: Virtuoso's Verse: An enhanced musical phrase that deals moderate damage and has a chance to apply a short debuff based on your Signature Sound element.
1843: Resonant Chant: A powerful sustained melody that provides both healing and a small defensive bonus to allies in range.
1844: Cascading Echo: Creates multiple echoes of your previous ability, each at diminishing potency.
1845: Tempo Control: Manipulates the flow of battle, slightly increasing allies' action speed while decreasing enemies' speed.
1846: Harmonic Convergence: Consumes all Crescendo stacks to create a powerful area effect that combines damage to enemies and support for allies.
1847: 
1848: Level 10+ (Hero Path Selection)
1849: At level 10, bards choose a Hero Path that reflects their philosophy and methods, further specializing their musical abilities.
1850: 
1851: Harmony (Hero Path)
1852: Supportive songsmith focused on healing, cleansing, and morale.
1853: 
1854: Passive Skills
1855: Song of Serenity: Nearby allies regenerate minor HP over 2 turns after a Chant.
1856: Cleansing Verse: Crescendo 5 grants group-wide status cleanse.
1857: Echo of Light: Echoes apply +10% healing received.
1858: Divine Chorus: Party gains +1 action every 3 turns while Crescendo is active.
1859: Finale: Crescendo of Hope: On death, performs one last healing Chant automatically. (1/day)
1860: 
1861: Active Skills
1862: Healing Harmony: A powerful restorative melody that heals allies and has a chance to remove negative status effects.
1863: Protective Verse: Creates a barrier around an ally that absorbs damage and grants resistance to the next status effect.
1864: Chorus of Renewal: Consumes all Crescendo stacks to provide a significant healing effect to all allies in range.
1865: Inspiring Performance: Boosts allies' morale, granting bonuses to attack, defense, and saving throws.
1866: Symphony of Serenity: Ultimate ability that creates a zone of peace, healing allies over time and preventing enemies from using certain aggressive abilities.
1867: 
1868: Virtuoso (Hero Path)
1869: Absolute mastery of tempo, combos, and battlefield manipulation.
1870: 
1871: Passive Skills
1872: Echo Reverb: Echo effects trigger a second time for 50% potency.
1873: Chant Cascade: Using a Chant resets cooldown on your last Verse.
1874: Encore: 20% chance a Bard skill refunds its cooldown.
1875: Tempo Theft: Gain +5% action speed for each enemy affected by your Chants.
1876: Finale: Grand Performance: Spend 5 Crescendo to grant an extra action to all allies in range. (1/day)
1877: 
1878: Active Skills
1879: Masterful Verse: A complex musical phrase that deals damage to multiple targets and builds Crescendo faster.
1880: Tempo Manipulation: Alters the flow of time in a small area, speeding up allies or slowing enemies.
1881: Virtuoso's Medley: Combines effects of your previously used abilities into a powerful sequence.
1882: Harmonic Resonance: Creates zones of beneficial or harmful energy based on musical notes played.
1883: Symphony of Mastery: Ultimate ability that maximizes your Crescendo generation and allows rapid casting of multiple bard abilities in sequence.
1884: 
1885: Discord (Hero Path)
1886: A twisted songweaver who manipulates madness, fear, and ruin.
1887: 
1888: Passive Skills
1889: Dissonant Pulse: Chants deal minor psychic damage each round.
1890: Cacophony: Verses apply a stacking -5% Accuracy debuff.
1891: Maddening Echo: Echoes may confuse enemies for 1 turn (25% chance).
1892: Twisted Chorus: Crescendo 5 causes enemies to suffer -10% to all saves for 2 turns.
1893: Finale: Dirge of Madness: Once per long rest, enemies in a wide area are stunned and take psychic damage based on your Crescendo stacks.
1894: 
1895: Active Skills
1896: Verse of Agony: A painful melody that deals damage over time and reduces healing received.
1897: Chant of Fear: Causes enemies to flee or fight at reduced effectiveness.
1898: Discordant Echo: Corrupts the effects of your previous ability to create harmful distortions.
1899: Mind-Rending Performance: Deals psychic damage and has a chance to confuse or stun enemies.
1900: Symphony of Madness: Ultimate ability that creates a zone of chaotic energy, causing enemies to attack each other and suffer from hallucinations.
1901: 
1902: Playing a Bard
1903: 
1904: As a bard in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on rhythm, positioning, and strategic use of your Crescendo mechanic. Unlike other classes that might focus on raw damage or defense, you excel at controlling the flow of battle through tempo manipulation and well-timed performances.
1905: 
1906: Combat Strategy
1907: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path:
1908: 
1909: Harmony Bards should position themselves to maximize the number of allies affected by their supportive abilities, using Verses strategically to build Crescendo for powerful group healing effects.
1910: 
1911: Virtuoso Bards thrive on maintaining perfect rhythm, using their abilities in carefully planned sequences to maximize Encore procs and Echo effects. They are the most technically demanding bard path but offer unparalleled versatility.
1912: 
1913: Discord Bards should focus on disrupting enemy formations and targeting key opponents with debilitating effects, using their damage-over-time abilities to pressure multiple enemies simultaneously.
1914: 
1915: All bards should remember that maintaining Crescendo is crucial, being interrupted or missing beats can reset your momentum and significantly reduce your effectiveness.
1916: 
1917: Reputation and Social Consequences
1918: Your choice of Hero path affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
1919: 
1920: Harmony Bards are generally welcomed in civilized settlements and may receive discounts or special treatment from those who value their healing and protective abilities.
1921: 
1922: Virtuoso Bards are respected for their technical skill but may be viewed with some suspicion due to their unpredictable nature. They typically have neutral reputation modifiers in most regions.
1923: 
1924: Discord Bards often face prejudice and suspicion, particularly in lawful communities. They may need to disguise their true nature or find alternative paths to achieve their goals in certain regions.
1925: 
1926: Notable Bard NPCs in Aethel
1927: 
1928: Lyria Silverharp
1929: A high elf Harmony bard who serves as the Royal Chronicler in the Imperial Court. She has documented the reign of three emperors and is known for her healing symphonies that can cure even the most grievous wounds.
1930: 
1931: Thorne Battleson
1932: A human Virtuoso bard who travels with mercenary companies, using his perfect mastery of tempo to coordinate attacks and provide tactical advantages. His performances are studied by military strategists throughout Aethel.
1933: 
1934: Whisper
1935: A mysterious feykin Discord bard who performs in high-society gatherings throughout Aethel. Those who hear Whisper's haunting melodies often find themselves revealing secrets they intended to keep or acting against their own interests days after the performance has ended.
1936: 
1937: Cleric
1938: 
1939: "The dwarven cleric raised her holy symbol, and a wave of golden light emanated from it, washing over her wounded companions. As the light touched them, their wounds began to close, and the weariness faded from their eyes. 'The divine favors those who act in its name,' she proclaimed, turning to face the approaching darkness with renewed determination."
1940: 
1941: Clerics are divine conduits who channel the power of their deity to heal allies, smite enemies, and perform sacred rites. In the world of Aethel, clerics serve as intermediaries between mortals and the divine, interpreting the will of their gods and manifesting divine power through faith and devotion. Their connection to higher powers grants them abilities that can turn the tide of battle, restore life to the fallen, and banish unholy threats.
1942: 
1943: Class Overview
1944: 
1945: As a cleric, you draw power directly from your deity through prayer, ritual, and unwavering faith. Your divine connection allows you to heal wounds, protect allies, smite enemies, and perform sacred ceremonies that invoke your deity's power. Beyond your magical abilities, you serve as a spiritual guide, offering counsel and representing your faith's tenets in your actions.
1946: 
1947: In Aethel, clerics can be found in temples, on battlefields, in remote shrines, or wandering the land in service to their deity. They might be devoted healers tending to the sick, zealous warriors fighting against heretics, or scholarly priests preserving ancient religious knowledge.
1948: 
1949: Core Mechanics
1950: 
1951: Divine Favor – Clerics accumulate Conviction points through prayer, ritual actions, and fulfilling their deity's will. These points can be spent to empower divine abilities, with more powerful effects requiring more Conviction. Managing this resource effectively is key to maximizing a cleric's potential.
1952: 
1953: Primary Attributes
1954: Main Attribute: Wisdom
1955: Secondary Attributes: Intelligence, Constitution
1956: Hit Points: Medium (8 + Constitution modifier at 1st level, 1d8 per level thereafter)
1957: Armor Proficiency: Light and medium armor, shields
1958: Weapon Proficiency: Simple weapons, deity-favored weapons
1959: Tool Proficiency: Religious implements
1960: 
1961: Progression Path
1962: 
1963: Level 1-2 (Base Cleric Skills)
1964: Foundational skills before Arch class selection.
1965: 
1966: Passive Skills
1967: Conviction Pool: +2 max Conviction cap.
1968: Consecrated Ground: Healing allies on terrain you sanctify (via Rite) heals for +10%.
1969: Judging Light: Smite deals +20% bonus damage to enemies afflicted by Cursed or Weakened effects.
1970: Fervent Ritualist: +10% faster channel speed on all Rites.
1971: Invocation Attunement: Choose one Invocation Path (see below); gain a permanent passive tied to it.
1972: 
1973: Invocation Path Bonuses (Apply at Level 2)
1974: Radiance: +10% healing power and heals cleanse minor conditions.
1975: Judgment: Smites mark enemies with Judged for 2 rounds, reducing their AC by 1.
1976: Renewal: Healing Rites restore 5 stamina to the target.
1977: Flame: Conviction can be consumed to ignite targets for 2 turns (fire DoT).
1978: Veil: Gain passive True Sight vs invisible or ethereal creatures.
1979: Echoes: 10% of healing or damage is repeated 1 turn later (Echo Effect).
1980: 
1981: Active Skills
1982: Divine Smite: Channel divine energy into a powerful strike against a single enemy, dealing damage based on your Wisdom and consuming 1 Conviction.
1983: Healing Rite: A channeled ability that restores health to an ally, with increased effectiveness on consecrated ground.
1984: Sacred Chant: A sustained prayer that generates 1 Conviction every 3 turns while maintained.
1985: Consecrate: Sanctify the ground in a 15-foot radius, creating consecrated terrain that enhances your healing abilities and weakens undead enemies.
1986: Divine Insight: Commune briefly with your deity to gain insight about a religious matter, sacred location, or divine presence.
1987: 
1988: Level 3-9 (Arch Class: Bishop)
1989: At level 3, clerics specialize into the Bishop arch class, refining their connection to the divine and developing more powerful prayers and rites.
1990: 
1991: Passive Skills
1992: Aura of Faith: Allies in range gain +5% healing received and +1 to saves.
1993: Sermon of Purpose: Chants now generate 1 Conviction every 2 turns.
1994: Liturgical Flow: Chaining 2 Rites reduces cooldown of your next Invocation by 1 turn.
1995: Anointed Guardian: First time an ally drops below 20% HP per combat, grant them a 30% damage reduction shield for 2 rounds.
1996: Sanctified Reprisal: Whenever you heal, next Smite deals 20% more damage and stuns Undead.
1997: Miraculous Channel: Sacrificing 3 Conviction causes your next Rite to affect the full party.
1998: Capstone: Bishop's Mandate: Activating a Sacrament automatically casts a free Invocation without cost or cooldown.
1999: 
2000: Active Skills
2001: Divine Invocation: Channel a powerful prayer based on your chosen Invocation Path, with effects ranging from mass healing to divine fire or protective barriers.
2002: Sacrament: Perform a sacred ceremony that consumes Conviction to create a powerful, sustained effect in an area.
2003: Banish Unholy: Channel divine energy to damage and potentially banish undead, demons, or other unholy entities.
2004: Blessing of Protection:  Bestow a divine blessing on an ally, granting them resistance to a specific damage type or condition.
2005: Commune:  Enter a deep meditative state to receive guidance directly from your deity, gaining insight on important matters or divine intervention.
2006: 
2007: Level 10+ (Hero Path Selection)
2008: At level 10, clerics choose a Hero Path that reflects their philosophy and methods, further specializing their divine abilities.
2009: 
2010: Saint (Hero Path)
2011: Master of mass healing and divine immunity.
2012: 
2013: Passive Skills
2014: Aura of Grace: Allies in range resist critical hits and gain minor HP regen.
2015: Divine Rebirth: On revive, ally returns with 50% HP and a temporary resistance shield.
2016: Merciful Sacrament: Sacraments cost no Conviction if used on an ally under 25% HP.
2017: Unyielding Light: Once per day, prevents party wipe and restores 30% HP.
2018: Final Miracle: Celestial Resurgence: Revive all allies who died within the last 3 turns.
2019: 
2020: Active Skills
2021: Mass Healing Light: Channel divine energy to heal all allies within 30 feet, with increased effectiveness on those most wounded.
2022: Divine Intervention: Call upon your deity to intervene directly, potentially averting disaster or creating a miraculous effect.
2023: Sanctify Being: Bestow temporary divine protection on an ally, granting them immunity to specific conditions or damage types.
2024: Radiant Burst: Release a powerful wave of divine energy that heals allies and damages unholy creatures.
2025: Resurrection: Return a fallen ally to life with renewed vigor and temporary divine blessing.
2026: 
2027: Priest (Hero Path)
2028: Pragmatic defender of faith and balance.
2029: 
2030: Passive Skills
2031: Scriptural Echo: Casting a Rite repeats the Invocation used previously for 50% effect.
2032: Words of Equilibrium: Heal and Damage dealt scale off the higher of INT or WIS.
2033: Balanced Scales: Enemies take divine damage if they attack healed targets.
2034: Doctrine Shield: Gain 1 Conviction anytime a condition is removed.
2035: Final Miracle: Word of Binding: Enemies in a large AoE are silenced, snared, and take +25% divine damage for 3 turns.
2036: 
2037: Active Skills
2038: Balance Restoration: Channel divine energy that both heals allies and damages enemies in an area, maintaining cosmic balance.
2039: Divine Judgment: Mark an enemy for judgment, causing them to take damage proportional to the harm they inflict.
2040: Ritual of Cleansing: Perform a ritual that removes multiple negative conditions from allies while potentially imposing them on enemies.
2041: Word of Power: Speak a divine word that has different effects based on the target's nature and actions.
2042: Cosmic Equilibrium: Create a zone where healing and harm are balanced, converting excess damage to healing and vice versa.
2043: 
2044: Cultist (Hero Path)
2045: Twisted zealot using death, sacrifice, and dark rites.
2046: 
2047: Passive Skills
2048: Sanguine Rite:  Rites damage enemies and heal allies for a portion of the same amount.
2049: Blasphemous Tongue: Sacraments inflict Doomed, reducing healing received and buff duration.
2050: Dark Conviction: Killing an enemy grants 1 Conviction instantly.
2051: Zealot's Touch: Healing over time effects become life drain if cast on enemies.
2052: Final Miracle: Rite of the Crimson Choir: Summon 4 fanatics for 3 turns. Each heals you for 10% of their damage dealt and explodes in death.
2053: 
2054: Active Skills
2055: Blood Sacrifice: Sacrifice a portion of your health or an ally's to generate Conviction and empower your next divine ability.
2056: Dark Sacrament: Perform a twisted ritual that drains life from enemies to heal allies or create other beneficial effects.
2057: Corrupt Divinity: Channel corrupted divine energy to weaken enemies and potentially convert them to your cause.
2058: Zealot's Fervor: Enter a state of religious frenzy, increasing your damage output and healing power at the cost of defense.
2059: Ritual of Undeath: Perform a forbidden ritual that can temporarily raise fallen enemies as servants or prevent allies from dying by transforming them into undead-like entities.
2060: 
2061: Playing a Cleric
2062: 
2063: As a cleric in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on Conviction management, positioning, and strategic use of your divine abilities. Unlike other classes that might focus on raw damage or defense, you excel at supporting allies, controlling the battlefield through divine effects, and adapting to the needs of your party.
2064: 
2065: Combat Strategy
2066: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path:
2067: 
2068: Saint Clerics should position themselves to maximize the number of allies affected by their healing and protective abilities, using Conviction strategically for powerful group healing effects when the party is most threatened.
2069: 
2070: Priest Clerics balance offensive and supportive abilities, adapting to the flow of battle and maintaining equilibrium between healing allies and smiting enemies. They excel at removing conditions and turning enemies' actions against them.
2071: 
2072: Cultist Clerics use more aggressive tactics, often sacrificing health or weaker enemies to fuel powerful abilities. They should focus on creating situations where their life-draining effects can be maximized.
2073: 
2074: All clerics should remember that Conviction management is crucial, spending it too quickly can leave you without resources when most needed, while being too conservative might prevent you from turning the tide of battle at critical moments.
2075: 
2076: Reputation and Social Consequences
2077: Your choice of Hero path affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
2078: 
2079: Saint Clerics are generally welcomed in civilized settlements and may receive special treatment from those who share their faith or benefit from their healing abilities.
2080: 
2081: Priest Clerics are respected for their balanced approach to divine matters but may face scrutiny from zealots of both good and evil alignments. They typically have neutral reputation modifiers in most regions.
2082: 
2083: Cultist Clerics often face prejudice and persecution, particularly in lawful good communities. They may need to disguise their true nature or find alternative paths to achieve their goals in certain regions.
2084: 
2085: Notable Cleric NPCs in Aethel
2086: 
2087: High Priestess Elara
2088: A human Saint cleric who leads the Cathedral of Dawn in the Imperial Capital. Her miraculous healing abilities have saved countless lives, including the Emperor himself during an assassination attempt. She is known for her mercy even toward enemies of the faith.
2089: 
2090: Brother Thorne
2091: A dwarf Priest cleric who maintains a shrine at a crossroads between three warring nations. He offers healing and guidance to travelers of all backgrounds, maintaining strict neutrality in political matters while ensuring the cosmic balance is preserved.
2092: 
2093: Cultist Morrigan
2094: A feykin Cultist cleric who leads a hidden sect in the shadow of Mount Dread. Her blood rituals are feared throughout the region, but desperate people still seek her out when conventional healing fails, willing to pay her dark price for miraculous cures.
2095: 
2096: Crimson Agent
2097: 
2098: "The half-elf moved like a shadow through the moonlight, twin daggers gleaming with a crimson sheen. As she struck, her blades left more than mere wounds, they left a lingering connection, a magical tether of blood that bound her to her target. With each heartbeat, the connection grew stronger, until she whispered a single word that caused her enemy to collapse as if drained from within."
2099: 
2100: Crimson Agents are masters of blood magic and stealth, combining precise strikes with hemomantic abilities that manipulate the vital essence of their targets. In the world of Aethel, these specialized operatives serve as elite assassins, spies, and saboteurs who use their unique connection to blood to track, weaken, and eliminate their targets with terrifying efficiency.
2101: 
2102: Class Overview
2103: 
2104: As a Crimson Agent, you harness the power of blood to enhance your combat abilities and create magical effects. Your specialized training allows you to mark targets with your blades, establish magical connections through blood, and manipulate these connections to devastating effect. Beyond your combat abilities, you excel at infiltration, tracking, and gathering intelligence through your heightened senses and stealth capabilities.
2105: 
2106: In Aethel, Crimson Agents might serve noble houses as specialized bodyguards, work for intelligence networks as field operatives, or operate independently as mercenaries for those who can afford their unique services. Some are viewed with suspicion due to their use of blood magic, while others are highly sought after for their unparalleled effectiveness in eliminating threats.
2107: 
2108: Core Mechanics
2109: 
2110: Blood Mark – Crimson Agents accumulate Blood Gauge points (initially max 3, later 6) through successful attacks and special abilities. These points can be spent to create and detonate Blood Marks on enemies or to power Veinweaves (self-targeted buffs). Managing this resource effectively is key to maximizing a Crimson Agent's potential.
2111: 
2112: Primary Attributes
2113: Main Attribute: Dexterity
2114: Secondary Attributes: Intelligence, Constitution
2115: Hit Points: Medium (8 + Constitution modifier at 1st level, 1d8 per level thereafter)
2116: Armor Proficiency: Light armor
2117: Weapon Proficiency: Simple weapons, finesse weapons, thrown weapons
2118: Tool Proficiency: Poisoner's kit, thieves' tools
2119: 
2120: Progression Path
2121: 
2122: Level 1-2 (Base Crimson Agent Skills)
2123: Foundational skills before Arch class selection.
2124: 
2125: Passive Skills
2126: Arterial Cut: Critical hits apply Bleed for 3 turns; Bleed duration +2 turns.
2127: Blood Tithe: Killing a bleeding target restores 10% of max HP.
2128: Veinweaver's Core: Unlocks use of Veinweaves (1 active at a time).
2129: Vital Targeting: +15% damage to flanked or unaware enemies.
2130: Shroudstep: After using stealth movement, gain +10% dodge for 1 turn.
2131: 
2132: Active Skills
2133: Hemorrhage Slash: A precise strike that deals moderate damage and applies Bleed status to the target, generating 1 Blood Gauge point.
2134: Veinweave: Quickblood: Consume 1 Blood Gauge point to increase your movement and attack speed by 15% for 3 turns.
2135: Blood Mark: Apply a magical mark to a bleeding target that lasts for 5 turns. The mark can be detonated for additional effects.
2136: Shroud: Enter stealth mode, becoming invisible to enemies until you attack or move into bright light. Limited uses per encounter.
2137: Vital Sense: Heighten your senses for 1 minute, allowing you to detect the presence of living creatures within 60 feet and see their vital points more clearly (+10% critical hit chance).
2138: 
2139: Level 3-9 (Arch Class: Hemomancer)
2140: At level 3, Crimson Agents specialize into the Hemomancer arch class, refining their blood magic and developing more powerful hemomantic techniques.
2141: 
2142: Passive Skills
2143: Blood Echo: Bleeds now stack twice and deal +10% more damage.
2144: Bio-Alchemical Flow: Veinweaves do not break stealth.
2145: Spill Control: Max Blood Gauge increased to 6.
2146: Rupture Trigger: Detonating a Blood Mark causes AoE Bleed in a small radius.
2147: Crimson Flow: Using Blood Gauge grants +10% movement and attack speed for 2 turns.
2148: Crimson Harmony: Maintain 2 Veinweaves simultaneously.
2149: Capstone – Bloodbound Edge: Once per rest, convert all Bleeds on enemies to instant True Damage.
2150: 
2151: Active Skills
2152: Veinweave: Bloodshield: Consume 2 Blood Gauge points to create a protective barrier that absorbs damage equal to 20% of your maximum health.
2153: Rupture: Detonate a Blood Mark on a target, dealing significant damage and consuming the mark.
2154: Bloodtrail: Mark a bleeding target, allowing you to track them perfectly for 1 hour regardless of distance or obstacles.
2155: Veinweave: Sensory Theft: Consume 2 Blood Gauge points to briefly experience what a bleeding target sees and hears.
2156: Hemostasis: Stop all bleeding effects on yourself or an ally, converting each remaining turn of Bleed into a small amount of healing.
2157: 
2158: Level 10+ (Hero Path Selection)
2159: At level 10, Crimson Agents choose a Hero Path that reflects their philosophy and methods, further specializing their hemomantic abilities.
2160: 
2161: Bloodweaver (Hero Path)
2162: A blood-magic defender who uses hemomancy to heal and shield others.
2163: 
2164: Passive Skills
2165: Bloodbond: Link with an ally; share 25% incoming damage and transfer 10% healing to them.
2166: Hemostasis: Bleeds applied to allies now heal them instead.
2167: Protective Flow: Allies affected by your Veinweaves gain 10% DR and +1 to all saves.
2168: Veinlock Tether: Form a damage-sharing link with an ally for 3 turns.
2169: Final Cut – Crimson Pact: Detonate all enemy Bleeds to heal nearby allies and grant them barriers. (1/day)
2170:

2174: Blood Ward: Create a protective circle using blood magic that shields allies within from certain types of damage or effects.
2175: Purify Blood: Remove poison, disease, or other blood-borne afflictions from a target.
2176: Lifewell: Ultimate ability that converts all your remaining Blood Gauge into a powerful healing aura for allies.
2177: 
2178: Shadow Operative (Hero Path)

2192: Blood Cipher: Extract information directly from a bleeding target's blood, potentially learning secrets or weaknesses.
2193: Blackout: Ultimate ability that creates a zone of magical darkness where only you can see clearly, granting significant advantages.
2194: 
2195: Thirstborn (Hero Path)
2196: A blood-crazed executioner who turns suffering into strength.
2197: 
2198: Passive Skills
2199: Blood Frenzy: +5% damage per enemy actively bleeding (max +25%).
2200: Hemorrhagic Plague: Bleeds automatically spread to nearby enemies every 3 turns.
2201: Pain-to-Power Conduction: Veinweaves explode when struck, dealing AoE Bleed and minor Fear.
2202: Carrion Conductor: Corpses leave behind blood pools that explode for AoE damage.
2203: Final Cut – Veinburst Requiem: Detonates all Bleeds and Blood Gauge effects in range. Enemies under 25% HP are instantly executed. (1/day)
2204:

2207: Veinweave: Blood Frenzy: Consume 3 Blood Gauge points to enter a frenzied state, increasing damage and attack speed but reducing defense.
2208: Blood Boil: Cause a target's blood to heat rapidly, dealing internal damage and potentially stunning them.
2209: Crimson Harvest: Execute a heavily wounded target, restoring health and Blood Gauge based on the target's missing health.
2210: Bloodstorm: Ultimate ability that creates a swirling vortex of blood, dealing continuous damage to enemies and empowering your abilities.
2211: 
2212: Playing a Crimson Agent
2213: 
2214: As a Crimson Agent in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on stealth, positioning, and strategic use of your Blood Gauge. Unlike other classes that might focus on direct confrontation, you excel at selecting high-value targets, establishing Blood Marks, and choosing the perfect moment to strike.
2215: 
2216: Combat Strategy
2217: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path:
2218: 
2219: Bloodweaver Agents should focus on establishing beneficial links with allies, using their hemomantic abilities to support the party while still maintaining offensive pressure on key targets.
2220: 
2221: Shadow Operative Agents excel at reconnaissance and ambush tactics, using stealth to position themselves advantageously and disable high-threat enemies before they can act effectively.
2222: 
2223: Thirstborn Agents thrive in prolonged combat, building up multiple Bleed effects across the battlefield and then detonating them for devastating area damage.
2224: 
2225: All Crimson Agents should remember that Blood Gauge management is crucial, spending it too quickly can leave you without resources when most needed, while being too conservative might prevent you from capitalizing on perfect opportunities.
2226: 
2227: Reputation and Social Consequences

2230: Bloodweaver Agents are often viewed with cautious respect, as their use of blood magic for healing and protection challenges common prejudices against hemomancy.
2231: 
2232: Shadow Operative Agents typically maintain low profiles, with their true capabilities known only to their employers and targets. They may have neutral or ambiguous reputations in most regions.
2233: 
2234: Thirstborn Agents are feared and reviled in most civilized areas, their blood-harvesting practices considered abhorrent. They often operate in the shadows or in regions where such practices are tolerated.
2235:

2246: 
2247: Dragoon
2248: 
2249: "The Drakari warrior leapt impossibly high, her spear gleaming in the sunlight as she reached the apex of her jump. For a moment, she seemed suspended against the sky before plummeting downward with devastating force. 2250: 
2251: Dragoons are elite warriors who combine exceptional mobility with devastating polearm attacks, specializing in aerial combat techniques inspired by the hunting methods of dragons. In the world of Aethel, these specialized fighters serve as elite shock troops, monster hunters, and battlefield controllers who use their signature leap attacks to strike from unexpected angles and control the flow of combat.
2252: 
2253: Class Overview
2254: 
2255: As a Dragoon, you harness momentum and gravity to enhance your combat abilities, using powerful leap attacks to strike enemies from above with devastating force. Your specialized training allows you to build and maintain momentum throughout battle, converting it into powerful attacks or defensive maneuvers. Beyond your combat abilities, you excel at vertical mobility, anti-air tactics, and polearm mastery.
2256: 
2257: In Aethel, Dragoons might serve in elite military units, work as specialized monster hunters focusing on flying creatures, or act as guardians of mountain passes and other strategic high ground. Their distinctive fighting style makes them instantly recognizable on the battlefield, and their ability to control vertical space gives them unique tactical advantages.
2258: 
2259: Core Mechanics
2260: 
2261: Momentum Gauge – Dragoons build Momentum through movement and successful attacks. This resource (initially max 5, later 7) can be spent to power special attacks or defensive abilities. Managing this resource effectively is key to maximizing a Dragoon's potential in extended engagements.

2271: Progression Path
2272: 
2273: Level 1-2 (Base Dragoon Skills)

2275: 
2276: Passive Skills
2277: Skybound Assault: Leap to a target and deal +25% damage. Grants 1 Momentum.
2278: Draconic Discipline: Gain +10% resistance to knockback and crowd control.
2279: Momentum Engine: Movement of 3+ tiles in a turn grants 1 Momentum (Max 5).
2280: Dragoon's Reach: Polearm attacks strike 1 tile further and have +5% crit chance.
2281: Aerial Poise: While airborne, take 25% reduced damage from ranged attacks.
2282: 
2283: Active Skills
2284: Dragon Leap: Launch yourself into the air and come crashing down on a target up to 30 feet away, dealing damage based on your Strength and height.
2285: Piercing Thrust: A powerful spear attack that partially ignores enemy armor and has increased critical hit chance.
2286: Wyvern's Guard: Enter a defensive stance that increases your resistance to damage from the next attack.
2287: Momentum Surge: Consume 2 Momentum to gain a burst of speed and +1 to your next attack roll.
2288: Draconic Roar: Release a intimidating battle cry that can frighten weaker enemies and rally allies.
2289: 
2290: Level 3-9 (Arch Class: Dragon Lancer)
2291: At level 3, Dragoons specialize into the Dragon Lancer arch class, refining their aerial combat techniques and developing more powerful momentum-based abilities.
2292: 
2293: Passive Skills
2294: Crater Strike: Leap attack that applies knockdown in a small AoE.
2295: Momentum Overflow: Exceed Momentum cap (Max 7), bonus damage scales with overflow.
2296: Piercing Howl: Next polearm strike ignores 50% armor.
2297: Lance Tempo: If you moved this turn, gain +10% evasion and +10% crit damage.
2298: Wyvern's Flow: Successful leaps refund 1 Momentum and reduce next cooldown by 1.
2299: Apex Assault: Ultimate leap: high damage finisher that consumes all Momentum.
2300: Capstone – Lancer's Apex: Leap attacks now cost no action if performed at max Momentum (1/encounter).
2301: 
2302: Active Skills
2303: Dragonflight: An enhanced leap ability that allows you to jump twice as high and far, with increased damage based on distance traveled.
2304: Spear Cyclone: Spin your polearm in a wide arc, striking all enemies within range and building Momentum for each hit.
2305: Momentum Burst: Consume all Momentum to gain a significant boost to your next attack's damage and critical hit chance.
2306: Aerial Recovery: After being knocked down or back, instantly regain your footing and gain 1 Momentum.
2307: Dragon's Eye: Briefly gain the keen vision of a dragon, allowing you to spot weaknesses in enemy defenses and increase your critical hit range.
2308: 
2309: Level 10+ (Hero Path Selection)
2310: At level 10, Dragoons choose a Hero Path that reflects their philosophy and methods, further specializing their aerial combat techniques.
2311: 
2312: Dragon Guardian (Hero Path)
2313: A protector who uses leaps to intercept danger and shield allies with wyrm-forged resilience.
2314: 
2315: Passive Skills
2316: Aegis Leap: Leap to an ally and intercept the next attack made against them.
2317: Wyrmhide: Gain +10% DR and immunity to Bleed/Burn/Poison while above 50% HP.
2318: Momentum Shield: Spending Momentum grants temporary HP.
2319: Spiral Defense: Automatically counter melee attacks with a polearm when at max Momentum.
2320: Final Flight – Bastion Descent: Slam down between allies, cleansing effects and shielding the group.
2321: 
2322: Active Skills
2323: Guardian's Intercept: Leap to an ally's position and take the next attack targeted at them, with reduced damage to yourself.
2324: Draconic Ward: Channel draconic energy to create a protective aura around yourself and nearby allies, reducing incoming damage.
2325: Wyrmscale Defense: Temporarily harden your skin like dragon scales, gaining significant damage reduction and status effect resistance.
2326: Momentum Barrier: Convert your Momentum into a protective shield that absorbs damage for you and nearby allies.
2327: Dragon's Sanctuary: Ultimate ability that creates a zone of protection, significantly reducing damage to all allies within range and cleansing negative effects.
2328: 
2329: Drake Rider (Hero Path)
2330: Balanced rider of power and discipline, favors fast, reactive strikes and elemental tactics.
2331: 
2332: Passive Skills
2333: Drake Mark: Each leap attack marks enemies. Marked enemies take +10% damage from you.
2334: Elemental Spearcraft: Infuse polearms with Fire, Ice, or Lightning for 3 turns (switchable).
2335: Air Mastery: Gain +20% jump range and 10% reduced cooldown on leap skills.
2336: Momentum Tempo: After spending 3+ Momentum in a round, gain an extra action next turn.
2337: Final Flight – Drakestorm Dive: Choose an element and leap into a group, triggering a large AoE blast.
2338: 
2339: Active Skills
2340: Elemental Lance: Infuse your weapon with elemental energy (fire, ice, or lightning), adding additional damage and effects to your attacks.
2341: Drake's Agility: Channel the speed of a drake, significantly increasing your movement speed and allowing an additional leap this turn.
2342: Aerial Combo: Perform a rapid series of attacks while airborne, building and consuming Momentum with each strike.
2343: Elemental Adaptation: Temporarily gain resistance to a chosen element and infuse your attacks with that element.
2344: Drakestorm: Ultimate ability that summons a swirling storm of elemental energy around you, dealing damage to nearby enemies and enhancing your mobility.
2345: 
2346: Dragon Tyrant (Hero Path)
2347: Harnesses draconic wrath, devastating bursts, tyrannical pressure, and terrain-breaking leaps.
2348: 
2349: Passive Skills
2350: Tyrant's Crash: Leap attack applies Fear and Sunder Armor in an AoE.
2351: Momentum Feeder: Killing or critting grants +2 Momentum.
2352: Wyrmblood Surge: Each Momentum spent grants +5% damage for the next 2 turns.
2353: Skypiercer Stance: Gain passive +15% crit and ignore height restrictions on leaps.
2354: Final Flight – Sovereign Descent: Break the ground in a large radius, stunning enemies and applying Bleed and Burn. (1/rest)
2355: 
2356: Active Skills
2357: Tyrant's Impale: A devastating attack that can pin weaker enemies to the ground, preventing movement and causing bleeding damage.
2358: Draconic Dominance: Channel the terrifying presence of a dragon, causing fear in nearby enemies and potentially causing them to flee.
2359: Momentum Rampage: Enter a state of draconic fury, gaining increased damage and attack speed based on your current Momentum.
2360: Terrain Shatter: A powerful ground strike that damages enemies in an area and creates difficult terrain.
2361: Wyrmking's Wrath: Ultimate ability that channels pure draconic power, greatly enhancing your leap attacks and allowing rapid successive jumps for a short duration.
2362: 
2363: Playing a Dragoon
2364: 
2365: As a Dragoon in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on mobility, positioning, and strategic use of your Momentum Gauge. Unlike other classes that might focus on sustained damage or defense, you excel at burst damage, battlefield control through positioning, and devastating single-target attacks.
2366: 
2367: Combat Strategy
2368: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path:
2369: 
2370: Dragon Guardian Dragoons should focus on positioning to protect vulnerable allies, using their leap abilities to intercept threats and their defensive skills to absorb damage that would otherwise harm their companions.
2371: 
2372: Drake Rider Dragoons excel at adaptability, switching between elemental effects to exploit enemy weaknesses and using their superior mobility to control the battlefield from multiple angles.
2373: 
2374: Dragon Tyrant Dragoons thrive on aggressive tactics, building and spending Momentum rapidly to maintain a constant offensive pressure and using fear effects to disrupt enemy formations.
2375: 
2376: All Dragoons should remember that Momentum management is crucial, building it through movement and spending it at optimal moments can dramatically increase your effectiveness in combat.
2377: 
2378: Reputation and Social Consequences
2379: Your choice of Hero path affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
2380: 
2381: Dragon Guardian Dragoons are often respected and welcomed in civilized areas, their protective abilities and discipline making them valued defenders of communities and allies in battle.
2382: 
2383: Drake Rider Dragoons typically maintain neutral reputations, their balanced approach to combat and pragmatic use of elemental powers making them effective mercenaries and monster hunters.
2384: 
2385: Dragon Tyrant Dragoons may face suspicion or fear in peaceful settlements due to their aggressive tactics and intimidating presence, though they are often sought after by military forces for their devastating effectiveness in battle.
2386: 
2387: Notable Dragoon NPCs in Aethel
2388: 
2389: Commander Thorne Skyleap
2390: A human Dragon Guardian who leads the Imperial Dragoon Corps, an elite unit responsible for defending the capital from aerial threats. His ability to intercept flying attackers has saved countless lives during wyvern raids.
2391: 
2392: Aria Stormspear
2393: A Drakari Drake Rider who works as an independent monster hunter, specializing in taking down dangerous flying creatures. Her mastery of elemental spearcraft allows her to adapt her tactics to any prey, earning her the nickname "The Elemental Lance."
2394: 
2395: Krag the Earthbreaker
2396: An orc Dragon Tyrant whose devastating leap attacks have become legendary on the battlefield. His signature move, a leap so powerful it creates a small crater and shockwave, has broken enemy formations in numerous conflicts, earning him both fear and respect among military leaders.
2397: 
2398: The Druid
2399: "The balance of nature is not a metaphor, it is the fundamental truth of existence. As a druid, I am both its guardian and its instrument." , Thorna Oakenheart, Circle of the Land Archdruid
2400: Druids are the voice of nature, channeling its primal power and defending the balance of life. In Aethel, they stand as guardians of wild places, mediators between civilization and wilderness, and masters of transformation. Drawing power from the natural world itself, druids can call upon the elements, commune with beasts, and even take on animal forms through their unique shapeshifting abilities.
2401: Class Features
2402: As a druid, you gain the following class features.
2403: Hit Points
2404: Hit Dice: 1d8 per druid levelHit Points at 1st Level: 8 + your Constitution modifierHit Points at Higher Levels: 1d8 (or 5) + your Constitution modifier per druid level after 1st
2405: Proficiencies
2406: Armor: Light armor, medium armor, shields (druids will not wear armor or use shields made of metal)Weapons: Clubs, daggers, darts, javelins, maces, quarterstaffs, scimitars, sickles, slings, spearsTools: Herbalism kitSaving Throws: Intelligence, WisdomSkills: Choose two from Arcana, Animal Handling, Insight, Medicine, Nature, Perception, Religion, and Survival
2407: Equipment
2408: You start with the following equipment, in addition to the equipment granted by your background:
2409: (a) a wooden shield or (b) any simple weapon
2410: (a) a scimitar or (b) any simple melee weapon
2411: Leather armor, an explorer's pack, and a druidic focus
2412: Druidic
2413: You know Druidic, the secret language of druids. You can speak the language and use it to leave hidden messages. You and others who know this language automatically spot such a message. Others spot the message's presence with a successful DC 15 Wisdom (Perception) check but can't decipher it without magic.
2414: Spellcasting
2415: Drawing on the essence of nature itself, you can cast spells to shape that essence to your will. See chapter 10 for the general rules of spellcasting and chapter 11 for the druid spell list.
2416: Cantrips
2417: At 1st level, you know two cantrips of your choice from the druid spell list. You learn additional druid cantrips of your choice at higher levels, as shown in the Cantrips Known column of the Druid table.
2418: Preparing and Casting Spells
2419: The Druid table shows how many Resonance Points you have to cast your druid spells. A spell's base RP cost is twice its Resonance Rank (Rank 0 cantrips cost 0), modified by explicit class features and current Dissonance. You regain expended RP according to the Resonance recovery rules.
2420: You prepare the list of druid spells that are available for you to cast, choosing from the druid spell list. When you do so, choose a number of druid spells equal to your Wisdom modifier + your druid level (minimum of one spell). The spells must be of a level for which you have Resonance Points.
2421: For example, if you are a 3rd-level druid, you have 7 Resonance Points. With a Wisdom of 16, your list of prepared spells can include six spells, and they can be of 1st or 2nd level in any combination. If you prepare the Rank 1 spell cure wounds, its base cost is 2 Resonance Points before explicit specialization or Dissonance modifiers. Casting the spell doesn't remove it from your list of prepared spells.
2422: You can change your list of prepared spells when you finish a long rest. Preparing a new list of druid spells requires time spent in prayer and meditation: at least 1 minute per spell level for each spell on your list.
2423: Spellcasting Ability
2424: Wisdom is your spellcasting ability for your druid spells, since your magic draws upon your devotion and attunement to nature. You use your Wisdom whenever a spell refers to your spellcasting ability. In addition, you use your Wisdom modifier when setting the saving throw DC for a druid spell you cast and when making an attack roll with one.
2425: Spell save DC = 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Wisdom modifier
2426: Spell attack modifier = your proficiency bonus + your Wisdom modifier
2427: Ritual Casting
2428: You can cast a druid spell as a ritual if that spell has the ritual tag and you have the spell prepared.
2429: 
2430: Spellcasting Focus
2431: You can use a druidic focus as a spellcasting focus for your druid spells.
2432: 
2433: Shapeshifting
2434: At 1st level, you gain the ability to transform into various animal forms. Shapeshifting is Aethel's native druidic transformation discipline and functions according to the following rules:
2435: 
2436: Activation and Duration
2437: You can use a bonus action to assume a beast form. You can remain in beast form for 10 minutes per use. You then revert to your normal form unless you expend another use of this feature. You can revert to your normal form earlier by using a bonus action on your turn.
2438: 
2439: Uses per Day
2440: At 1st level, you can use your Shapeshifting ability twice per long rest. You gain additional uses as you reach higher levels, as shown in the Shapeshifting Uses column of the Druid table.
2441: 
2442: Available Forms
2443: At 1st level, you can transform into any of the Base Druid Forms listed below. At 11th level, you gain access to Arch Druid Forms. At 10th level, you gain access to Hero Druid Forms specific to your chosen Circle.
2444: Limitations While Transformed
2445: While in beast form:
2446: You cannot cast spells unless specified by your Hero Class
2447: Your ability to speak is limited (only preserved in arch/hero forms)
2448: You retain your mental ability scores (Intelligence, Wisdom, and Charisma)
2449: You gain the physical traits of the chosen form as specified
2450: Base Druid Forms (Levels 1-10)
2451: Wolf Form (Striker)
2452: +10 ft. movement speed
2453: Keen Senses: Advantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks that rely on hearing or smell
2454: Bite: 1d8 piercing damage + DC 11 Strength save or target is knocked prone
2455: Bear Form (Tank)
2456: +20 HP temporary buffer
2457: AC set to 14 (unless your normal AC is higher)
2458: Claw: 2d6 slashing damage
2459: Rage-like trait: Deal +1 damage per attack
2460: Hawk Form (Scout)
2461: Flight speed of 60 ft.
2462: Keen Sight: Advantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks that rely on sight
2463: Dive Attack: 1d4 slashing damage + advantage if striking from above
2464: Boar Form (Brawler)
2465: Tough Hide: Resistance to slashing damage
2466: Charge: If you move at least 10 ft. straight toward a target, you deal an additional 1d6 damage and push the target 5 ft.
2467: Panther Form (Stealth)
2468: +4 bonus to Dexterity (Stealth) checks
2469: 60 ft. darkvision
2470: Pounce: If you move at least 20 ft. straight toward a creature and hit with a claw attack (2d4 damage), the target must succeed on a DC 12 Strength saving throw or be grappled
2471: Druid Circle
2472: At 2nd level, you choose to identify with a circle of druids: the Circle of the Land, the Circle of the Moon, or the Circle of the Storm, all detailed at the end of the class description. Your choice grants you features at 2nd level and again at 6th, 10th, and 14th level.
2473: Ability Score Improvement
2474: When you reach 4th level, and again at 8th, 12th, 16th, and 19th level, you can increase one ability score of your choice by 2, or you can increase two ability scores of your choice by 1. As normal, you can't increase an ability score above 20 using this feature.
2475: Timeless Body
2476: Starting at 18th level, the primal magic that you wield causes you to age more slowly. For every 10 years that pass, your body ages only 1 year.
2477: Beast Spells
2478: Beginning at 18th level, you can cast many of your druid spells in any shape you assume using Shapeshifting. You can perform the somatic and verbal components of a druid spell while in a beast shape, but you aren't able to provide material components.
2479: Archdruid
2480: At 20th level, you can use your Shapeshifting feature an unlimited number of times.
2481: Additionally, you can ignore the verbal and somatic components of your druid spells, as well as any material components that lack a cost and aren't consumed by a spell. You gain this benefit in both your normal shape and your beast shapes from Shapeshifting.

2630: Arch Druid Forms (Levels 11-20)
2631: At 11th level, you gain access to more powerful shapeshifting forms:
2632: Dire Wolf
2633: Pack Tactics: You have advantage on attack rolls against a creature if at least one of your allies is within 5 ft. of the creature and not incapacitated
2634: Bite: 2d6 piercing damage
2635: Howl: As an action, you can emit a frightening howl. Each hostile creature within 30 ft. must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw or be frightened for 1 minute. The creature can repeat the saving throw at the end of each of its turns.
2636: Earth Warden (Dire Bear)
2637: 30 temporary hit points while in this form
2638: Earth Slam: As an action, you can slam the ground, causing a shockwave in a 10-ft. radius. Each creature in that area must make a Dexterity saving throw or take 2d6 bludgeoning damage and be knocked prone.
2639: Stone Resistance: You have resistance to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from nonmagical attacks
2640: Sky Serpent
2641: Flight speed of 80 ft.
2642: Lightning Bite: Your bite deals 2d8 lightning damage
2643: Glide Reflexes: You gain a +2 bonus to Dexterity saving throws
2644: Ironhorn Ram
2645: AC set to 17 (unless your normal AC is higher)
2646: Charge: If you move at least 20 ft. straight toward a target and hit with a ram attack, the target takes an extra 3d6 bludgeoning damage and must succeed on a DC 14 Strength saving throw or be pushed 10 ft. away
2647: Stability: You cannot be knocked prone
2648: Shadow Lynx
2649: Blink: As a reaction when you would be hit by an attack, you can teleport up to 30 ft. to an unoccupied space you can see
2650: Magical Attacks: Your attacks count as magical for the purpose of overcoming resistance and immunity to nonmagical attacks
2651: Druid Circles
2652: Circle of the Land
2653: The Circle of the Land is made up of mystics and sages who safeguard ancient knowledge and rites through a vast oral tradition. These druids meet within sacred circles of trees or standing stones to whisper primal secrets in Druidic. The circle's wisest members preside as the chief priests of communities that hold to the Old Faith and serve as advisors to the rulers of those folk. As a member of this circle, your magic is influenced by the land where you were initiated into the circle's mysterious rites.
2654: Bonus Cantrip
2655: When you choose this circle at 2nd level, you learn one additional druid cantrip of your choice.
2656: Natural Recovery
2657: Starting at 2nd level, you can regain some of your magical energy by sitting in meditation and communing with nature. During a short rest, you choose expended Resonance Points to recover. The Resonance Points can have a combined level that is equal to or less than half your druid level (rounded up), and none of the points can be used to cast spells of 6th level or higher. You can't use this feature again until you finish a long rest.
2658: For example, when you are a 4th-level druid, you can recover up to 2 Resonance Points during a short rest.
2659: Circle Spells
2660: Your mystical connection to the land infuses you with the ability to cast certain spells. At 3rd, 5th, 7th, and 9th level you gain access to circle spells connected to the land where you became a druid. Choose that land, arctic, coast, desert, forest, grassland, mountain, swamp, or Murk, and consult the associated list of spells.
2661: Once you gain access to a circle spell, you always have it prepared, and it doesn't count against the number of spells you can prepare each day. If you gain access to a spell that doesn't appear on the druid spell list, the spell is nonetheless a druid spell for you.
2662: Arctic 3rd level: hold person, spike growth 5th level: sleet storm, slow 7th level: freedom of movement, ice storm 9th level: commune with nature, cone of cold
2663: Coast 3rd level: mirror image, misty step 5th level: water breathing, water walk 7th level: control water, freedom of movement 9th level: conjure elemental, scrying
2664: Desert 3rd level: blur, silence 5th level: create food and water, protection from energy 7th level: blight, hallucinatory terrain 9th level: insect plague, wall of stone
