2665: Forest 3rd level: barkskin, spider climb 5th level: call lightning, plant growth 7th level: divination, freedom of movement 9th level: commune with nature, tree stride
2666: Grassland 3rd level: invisibility, pass without trace 5th level: daylight, haste 7th level: divination, freedom of movement 9th level: dream, insect plague
2667: Mountain 3rd level: spider climb, spike growth 5th level: lightning bolt, meld into stone 7th level: stone shape, stoneskin 9th level: passwall, wall of stone
2668: Swamp 3rd level: darkness, acid arrow 5th level: water walk, stinking cloud 7th level: freedom of movement, locate creature 9th level: insect plague, scrying
2669: Murk 3rd level: spider climb, web 5th level: gaseous form, stinking cloud 7th level: greater invisibility, stone shape 9th level: cloudkill, insect plague
2670: Land's Stride
2671: Starting at 6th level, moving through nonmagical difficult terrain costs you no extra movement. You can also pass through nonmagical plants without being slowed by them and without taking damage from them if they have thorns, spines, or a similar hazard.
2672: In addition, you have advantage on saving throws against plants that are magically created or manipulated to impede movement, such as those created by the entangle spell.
2673: Nature's Ward
2674: When you reach 10th level, you can't be charmed or frightened by elementals or fey, and you are immune to poison and disease.
2675: Hero Forms: Circle of Thorns
2676: At 10th level, you gain access to the following Hero Druid Forms:
2677: Blight Elk
2678: Aura of Decay: Enemies that start their turn within 5 ft. of you take 1d4 poison damage
2679: Antlers: Your antler attack deals 2d8 piercing damage
2680: Toxic Presence: Plants wither in your presence, and water becomes tainted
2681: Tanglemaw
2682: Bite: Your bite attack deals 2d6 piercing damage and causes the target to be restrained on a failed DC 14 Strength saving throw
2683: Thorny Body: Creatures that touch you or hit you with a melee attack take 1d4 piercing damage
2684: Root Network: You can sense the presence of creatures touching the ground within 30 ft. of you
2685: Nature's Sanctuary
2686: When you reach 14th level, creatures of the natural world sense your connection to nature and become hesitant to attack you. When a beast or plant creature attacks you, that creature must make a Wisdom saving throw against your druid spell save DC. On a failed save, the creature must choose a different target, or the attack automatically misses. On a successful save, the creature is immune to this effect for 24 hours.
2687: The creature is aware of this effect before it makes its attack against you.
2688: Circle of the Moon
2689: Druids of the Circle of the Moon are fierce guardians of the wilds. Their order gathers under the full moon to share news and trade warnings. They haunt the deepest parts of the wilderness, where they might go for weeks on end before crossing paths with another humanoid creature, let alone another druid.
2690: Changeable as the moon, a druid of this circle might prowl as a great cat one night, soar over the treetops as an eagle the next day, and crash through the undergrowth in bear form to drive off a trespassing monster. The wild is in the druid's blood.
2691: Combat Shapeshifting
2692: When you choose this circle at 2nd level, you gain Combat Shapeshifting. While transformed by Shapeshifting, you can use a bonus action to expend one or more Resonance Points, regaining 1d8 hit points for each RP expended. Activating Shapeshifting itself still spends a Shapeshifting use, not RP.
2693: Circle Forms
2694: Starting at 2nd level, you can use your Shapeshifting to transform into more powerful beast forms than a typical druid. Your level as a druid determines the maximum CR of the form you can take, as shown in the Druid table.
2695: Primal Strike
2696: Starting at 6th level, your attacks in beast form count as magical for the purpose of overcoming resistance and immunity to nonmagical attacks and damage.
2697: Hero Forms: Circle of the Moon
2698: At 10th level, you gain access to the following Hero Druid Forms:
2699: Beastlord (Alpha Animal Hybrid)
2700: Chimera Cub: Your attacks deal 1d4 each of fire, cold, and lightning damage in addition to their normal damage
2701: Alpha Dire Wolf: You can intimidate all creatures within 15 ft. As an action, each creature in range must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw or become frightened for 1 minute
2702: Pack Leader: Beast allies within 30 ft. of you gain advantage on attack rolls
2703: Mooncursed
2704: Lycanthropic Resilience: You have resistance to all damage except silver
2705: Lunar Rage: During a full moon, your attacks deal an additional 1d8 damage
2706: Howl of the Wild: As an action, you can emit a howl that inspires allies or terrifies enemies. Choose one effect: allies within 30 ft. gain 2d8 temporary hit points, or enemies within 30 ft. must make a Wisdom saving throw or be frightened for 1 minute.
2707: Elemental Shapeshifting
2708: At 14th level, you gain the ability to transform into an elemental. You can transform into an air elemental, an earth elemental, a fire elemental, or a water elemental. You can use this feature twice. You regain expended uses when you finish a long rest.
2709: Circle of the Storm
2710: The Circle of the Storm consists of druids who have forged a special bond with the elemental spirits of storms, lightning, and thunder. These druids are often found in coastal regions, mountain peaks, or anywhere the fury of storms manifests most powerfully. They serve as both harbingers of coming tempests and protectors against their most destructive aspects.
2711: Storm Magic
2712: When you choose this circle at 2nd level, you gain the ability to subtly control the weather around you. If it is raining, you can make it rain harder or stop. If it is windy, you can increase or decrease the wind's intensity. These minor changes last for 1 hour after you stop actively maintaining them.
2713: Additionally, you learn the thunderwave and fog cloud spells, which don't count against your number of druid spells known.
2714: Thunderous Strike
2715: At 2nd level, when you deal lightning or thunder damage to a Large or smaller creature, you can push it up to 10 feet away from you.
2716: Storm's Embrace
2717: Starting at 6th level, you gain resistance to lightning and thunder damage. Additionally, whenever you cast a spell that deals lightning or thunder damage, add your Wisdom modifier to the damage it deals.
2718: Hero Forms: Stormcaller
2719: At 10th level, you gain access to the following Hero Druid Forms:
2720: Tempest Raptor
2721: Lightning Claw: Your claw attacks deal 2d6 lightning damage
2722: Flight: You have a flying speed of 60 ft.
2723: Storm Shroud: You are surrounded by crackling electricity. Creatures that touch you or hit you with a melee attack take 1d6 lightning damage.
2724: Thunderhorn Stag
2725: Charge: If you move at least 20 ft. straight toward a target and hit with a horn attack, the target takes 2d8 thunder damage and must succeed on a Constitution saving throw or be deafened until the end of its next turn
2726: Thunder Step: Once per transformation, you can teleport up to 30 ft. in a crack of thunder. Each creature within 10 ft. of the space you left must make a Constitution saving throw or take 2d8 thunder damage and be deafened until the end of its next turn.
2727: Weather Sense: You can sense approaching storms up to 24 hours in advance
2728: Eye of the Storm
2729: At 14th level, you gain the ability to create a zone of relative calm within a storm. As an action, you can create a 20-foot-radius sphere centered on you. Within the sphere, winds are reduced to gentle breezes, precipitation stops, and temperature becomes moderate. This effect lasts for 10 minutes and doesn't require concentration.
2730: Additionally, when you cast a spell that deals lightning or thunder damage, you can choose a number of creatures equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of one) to automatically succeed on their saving throws against the spell, taking no damage if they would normally take half damage on a successful save.
2731: Additional Druid Circles
2732: Circle of Thorns
2733: Druids of the Circle of Thorns embrace the darker aspects of nature, decay, poison, and the inevitable cycle of death and rebirth. Often misunderstood and feared, these druids understand that rot and decay are as essential to the natural order as growth and bloom. They tend blighted areas, ensuring that decay serves its purpose without spreading unchecked.
2734: Toxic Affinity
2735: When you choose this circle at 2nd level, you gain resistance to poison damage and advantage on saving throws against being poisoned. Additionally, you learn the poison spray cantrip if you don't already know it.
2736: Blighted Touch
2737: Starting at 2nd level, you can channel decay through your touch. As an action, you can touch a creature and force it to make a Constitution saving throw against your spell save DC. On a failed save, the creature takes 2d6 poison damage and is poisoned until the end of your next turn. On a successful save, the creature takes half damage and isn't poisoned.
2738: You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of once). You regain all expended uses when you finish a long rest.
2739: Withering Aura
2740: Beginning at 6th level, you emanate an aura of decay. As a bonus action, you can activate this aura, causing nonmagical plants within 10 feet of you to wither and die. Additionally, each creature of your choice that starts its turn within 10 feet of you must make a Constitution saving throw against your spell save DC. On a failed save, the creature takes 1d6 poison damage.
2741: This aura lasts for 1 minute or until you dismiss it as a bonus action. Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a short or long rest.
2742: Hero Forms: Circle of Thorns
2743: At 10th level, you gain access to the following Hero Druid Forms:
2744: Blight Elk
2745: Aura of Decay: Enemies that start their turn within 5 ft. of you take 1d4 poison damage
2746: Antlers: Your antler attack deals 2d8 piercing damage
2747: Toxic Presence: Plants wither in your presence, and water becomes tainted
2748: Tanglemaw
2749: Bite: Your bite attack deals 2d6 piercing damage and causes the target to be restrained on a failed DC 14 Strength saving throw
2750: Thorny Body: Creatures that touch you or hit you with a melee attack take 1d4 piercing damage
2751: Root Network: You can sense the presence of creatures touching the ground within 30 ft. of you
2752: Master of Decay
2753: At 14th level, you gain immunity to poison damage and the poisoned condition. Additionally, when you cast a spell that deals poison damage, you can choose a number of creatures equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of one) to automatically fail their saving throws against the spell.
2754: Circle of Winterfang
2755: Druids of the Circle of Winterfang have forged a special bond with the harsh beauty and unforgiving nature of winter. These druids often dwell in arctic regions or high mountains, where they serve as guardians of the delicate balance of life that exists in these extreme environments.
2756: Frost Magic
2757: When you choose this circle at 2nd level, you learn the ray of frost cantrip if you don't already know it. Additionally, you learn the ice knife spell, which doesn't count against your number of druid spells known.
2758: Cold Resistance
2759: Starting at 2nd level, you gain resistance to cold damage. Additionally, you are naturally adapted to cold climates, as described in the environmental rules in Chapter 5.
2760: Winter's Embrace
2761: Beginning at 6th level, you can surround yourself with an aura of biting cold. As a bonus action, you can activate this aura, causing each creature of your choice that starts its turn within 10 feet of you to make a Constitution saving throw against your spell save DC. On a failed save, the creature takes 1d6 cold damage and its speed is reduced by 10 feet until the start of its next turn.
2762: This aura lasts for 1 minute or until you dismiss it as a bonus action. Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a short or long rest.
2763: Hero Forms: Winterfang
2764: At 10th level, you gain access to the following Hero Druid Forms:
2765: Snow Leopard
2766: Claw: Your claw attacks deal 2d6 cold damage
2767: Stealth: You gain a +10 bonus to Dexterity (Stealth) checks made in snowy or icy terrain
2768: Frost Walk: You can move across icy surfaces without making ability checks and through snow without penalty
2769: Frost Wyrmling
2770: Breath Weapon: As an action, you can exhale a 30-foot cone of freezing air. Each creature in the area must make a Dexterity saving throw against your spell save DC, taking 4d6 cold damage on a failed save, or half as much on a successful one. Once you use this breath weapon, you can't use it again until you complete a short or long rest.
2771: Scales of Ice: You gain a +2 bonus to AC
2772: Cold Immunity: You are immune to cold damage
2773: Master of Winter
2774: At 14th level, you gain immunity to cold damage. Additionally, you can cast the control weather spell once without expending Resonance Points, but when you do so, you can only create weather conditions related to winter (such as snow, sleet, or freezing rain). Once you cast the spell in this way, you can't do so again until you finish a long rest.
2775: Circle of the Rotshaper
2776: Druids of the Circle of the Rotshaper have formed a unique bond with the often-overlooked creatures of nature, vermin, insects, and other small beings that most consider pests. These druids understand that even the smallest creatures play vital roles in the ecosystem, breaking down dead matter and returning it to the soil.
2777: Vermin Affinity
2778: When you choose this circle at 2nd level, you can communicate simple ideas with tiny beasts such as rats, spiders, insects, and other vermin. Additionally, such creatures are indifferent to you by default rather than hostile.
2779: Swarm Summoning
2780: Starting at 2nd level, you can summon a swarm of tiny creatures to aid you. As an action, you can summon a swarm of insects, rats, or spiders (your choice) in an unoccupied space within 30 feet of you. The swarm acts on your initiative count and obeys your verbal commands. It lasts for 10 minutes, until it drops to 0 hit points, or until you dismiss it as a bonus action.
2781: Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a short or long rest.
2782: Plague Bearer
2783: Beginning at 6th level, you can infect creatures with debilitating diseases. When you hit a creature with a melee attack, you can force it to make a Constitution saving throw against your spell save DC. On a failed save, the creature is poisoned for 1 minute. While poisoned in this way, the creature takes 1d6 poison damage at the start of each of its turns.
2784: The creature can repeat the saving throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success. Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a short or long rest.
2785: Hero Forms: Rotshaper
2786: At 10th level, you gain access to the following Hero Druid Forms:
2787: Carrion Rat King
2788: Poison Bite: Your bite attack deals 1d8 piercing damage plus 2d6 poison damage. The target must succeed on a DC 13 Constitution saving throw or be poisoned for 1 minute.
2789: Summon Swarm: As an action, you can summon a swarm of rats that acts on your initiative and obeys your mental commands. The swarm lasts for 1 minute or until reduced to 0 hit points.
2790: Disease Immunity: You are immune to disease and the poisoned condition.
2791: Insect Broodmother
2792: Spawn Swarms: Each time you transform into this form, you can spawn 1d4 swarms of insects (CR 1/4 each) that act on your initiative and obey your mental commands. The swarms last until they are reduced to 0 hit points or until you revert to your normal form.
2793: Chitinous Armor: You gain a +3 bonus to AC
2794: Wall Crawling: You can climb difficult surfaces, including upside down on ceilings, without making an ability check.
2795: Master of Vermin
2796: At 14th level, you gain the ability to transform into a swarm of tiny beasts (insects, rats, bats, or spiders) as if using your Shapeshifting feature. While in swarm form, you have resistance to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage, and you can move through any space large enough for a Tiny creature without squeezing.
2797: Additionally, when you cast a spell that summons or creates beasts, you can choose to summon a swarm instead of individual creatures.
2798: Circle of the Ashen Grove
2799: Druids of the Circle of the Ashen Grove understand that fire, while destructive, is also a natural force of renewal. These druids often tend to areas recovering from wildfires, helping to guide the process of regeneration. They believe that from ash and cinder, new life will always emerge stronger.
2800: Fire Affinity
2801: When you choose this circle at 2nd level, you gain resistance to fire damage. Additionally, you learn the produce flame cantrip if you don't already know it.
2802: Blazing Rebirth
2803: Starting at 2nd level, you can channel the regenerative power of fire. When you or an ally within 30 feet of you takes fire damage, you can use your reaction to grant temporary hit points equal to your druid level + your Wisdom modifier to that creature.
2804: You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of once). You regain all expended uses when you finish a long rest.
2805: Flame Stride
2806: Beginning at 6th level, you can move through fire without taking damage. Additionally, when you move through a space containing fire, you can choose to leave a trail of fire behind you. Each creature that enters this fire for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there takes 1d6 fire damage.
2807: The fire lasts for 1 minute or until extinguished. Once you use this feature, you can't use it again until you finish a short or long rest.
2808: Hero Forms: Ashen Grove
2809: At 10th level, you gain access to the following Hero Druid Forms:
2810: Charbear
2811: Flame Aura: Creatures within 5 ft. of you take 1d6 fire damage at the start of their turn
2812: Fire Claw: Your claw attacks deal 2d8 fire damage
2813: Smoke Cloud: As an action, you can create a 20-foot-radius sphere of smoke centered on yourself. The area is heavily obscured, and creatures other than you that start their turn in the smoke must succeed on a Constitution saving throw or be poisoned until the start of their next turn.
2814: Blazewood Stag
2815: Flaming Trail: You leave a trail of fire behind you as you move. Each creature that enters this fire for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there takes 1d8 fire damage.
2816: Burning Charge: If you move at least 20 ft. straight toward a target and hit with a horn attack, the target takes an additional 2d6 fire damage.
2817: Conflagration: Once per long rest, you can explode in a burst of flame. Each creature within 10 ft. of you must make a Dexterity saving throw against your spell save DC, taking 6d6 fire damage on a failed save, or half as much on a successful one.
2818: Master of Ash
2819: At 14th level, you gain immunity to fire damage. Additionally, when you cast a spell that deals fire damage, you can choose a number of creatures equal to your Wisdom modifier (minimum of one) to automatically fail their saving throws against the spell.
2820: Furthermore, you can cast the plant growth spell to instantly restore vegetation to an area devastated by fire, causing plants to grow to full maturity within minutes rather than over the course of a year.
2821: Playing a Druid
2822: As a druid, you gain power through a profound connection to the natural world and its cycles. Your shapeshifting abilities allow you to literally walk in the paws, hooves, or claws of nature's creatures, gaining not just their physical capabilities but also their perspectives. This intimate understanding of the natural order gives you a unique outlook on the world.
2823: Roleplaying a Druid
2824: Consider these questions when creating your druid character:
2825: What aspect of nature do you feel most connected to? Forests, mountains, storms, or something else?
2826: How did you first discover your ability to shapeshift? Was it a gradual development or a sudden transformation in a moment of need?
2827: What is your attitude toward civilization? Do you see it as a threat to nature or as part of the natural order?
2828: How do you view your role in maintaining the balance of nature? Are you a guardian, a mediator, or something else?
2829: What natural disaster or environmental threat most concerns you, and how do you work to prevent or mitigate it?
2830: Druid Circles in Aethel
2831: In Aethel, druid circles are more than just philosophical groupings, they are actual communities with territories, traditions, and hierarchies. Some notable circles include:
2832: The Verdant Conclave: A powerful circle in the Greenhaven Forest that maintains the ancient groves and protects them from encroachment.
2833: The Stormcallers of Mistral Plains: Druids who ride the great thunderstorms across the plains, bringing rain where needed and diverting destructive weather.
2834: The Frostbound: A secretive circle in the northern reaches who guard ancient secrets frozen in the ice since the Dawn Era.
2835: The Ashen Renewal: Druids who specialize in helping lands recover from devastation, whether natural or magical in origin.
2836: The Deeproot Network: A circle that spans the continent through underground connections, sharing information through fungal networks and root systems.
2837: Druids and Reputation
2838: Your actions as a druid will significantly impact your reputation with various factions:
2839: Nature-Focused Groups: Your fellow druids and other nature protectors will judge you based on how well you maintain the balance. Allowing unnecessary destruction of natural areas will harm your standing.
2840: Settled Communities: Villages and towns near wild areas often have complex relationships with druids, respecting their power but sometimes fearing their priorities.
2841: Arcane Organizations: Many magical academies seek druidic knowledge but approach nature from a more analytical perspective, creating tension.
2842: Religious Orders: Some religious groups view druids as competitors for spiritual authority, while others see them as allies in maintaining divine order.
2843: Druids and Alignment
2844: A druid's alignment often reflects their view of natural order:
2845: Good-aligned druids typically focus on nature's nurturing aspects, protecting life and helping communities live in harmony with their environment.
2846: Neutral druids often embody the balance of nature itself, understanding that both creation and destruction are necessary parts of the cycle.
2847: Evil-aligned druids might emphasize nature's ruthlessness, believing that the strong should dominate the weak as predators do prey.
2848: At 10th level, your Circle and the choices you have made determine which Hero Forms you can pursue. These forms express a philosophy of nature and power rather than an objective moral alignment.
2849: Notable Druid NPCs in Aethel
2850: Thorna Oakenheart - An ancient elf of the Circle of the Land who has protected the Greenhaven Forest for centuries. She appears as a wizened woman with bark-like skin and leaves growing from her hair.
2851: Gale Stormrider - A human Circle of the Storm druid who travels the Mistral Plains, helping communities predict and prepare for the massive thunderstorms that sweep across the region.
2852: Frost Whisperer Narak - A Drakari of the Circle of Winterfang who guards the frozen northern reaches against those who would disturb ancient evils trapped beneath the ice.
2853: Mossbeard - A dwarf of the Circle of the Land who has devoted his life to reclaiming lands corrupted by dark magic, slowly cleansing them and restoring natural growth.
2854: Vex Thornheart - A tiefling of the Circle of Thorns who studies the natural cycle of decay and rebirth, often misunderstood due to her focus on death as part of life's cycle.
2855: 
2856: Mage
2857: 
2858: "The elven mage's fingers traced complex patterns in the air, leaving trails of arcane energy that coalesced into a sphere of crackling lightning. With a word of power and a gesture of command, she hurled the sphere toward the approaching enemies. It detonated in a brilliant flash, arcs of electricity chaining between foes as they fell, their armor offering no protection against the raw elemental fury she had unleashed."
2859: 
2860: Mages are the purest embodiment of destructive arcane will, fragile but overwhelming spellcasters who shape elemental forces into lethal expressions of dominance. In the world of Aethel, these scholars of devastation stand apart from ritual-bound clerics or nature-attuned druids, relying instead on their intellectual mastery of the fundamental forces that compose reality itself.
2861: 
2862: Class Overview
2863: 
2864: As a mage, you harness raw elemental power through rigorous study and practice, focusing primarily on evocation, conjuration, and abjuration magic. Your specialty lies in devastating spell combinations, precise area effects, and arcane defenses that compensate for your physical vulnerability. Beyond your combat abilities, you represent the pinnacle of arcane scholarship, capable of bending reality to your will through the application of magical theory and elemental manipulation.
2865: 
2866: In Aethel, mages might serve as royal advisors, battlefield artillery, arcane researchers, or independent scholars seeking to push the boundaries of magical understanding. Their power makes them both respected and feared, with many common folk viewing them with a mixture of awe and suspicion.
2867: 
2868: Core Mechanics
2869: 
2870: Elemental Focus – At level 2, you choose one elemental specialization (Fire, Cold, or Lightning) that enhances your spells of that type and unlocks powerful finisher spells when you chain three consecutive spells of the same element.
2871: 
2872: Nuke Chains – After casting three consecutive spells of your chosen element, you can trigger an enhanced finisher spell with devastating effects.
2873: 
2874: Resonance Surge – A resource-based casting system that enables burst-damage rotations, allowing you to sacrifice sustainability for moments of overwhelming power.
2875: 
2876: Primary Attributes
2877: Main Attribute: Intelligence
2878: Secondary Attributes: Wisdom, Dexterity
2879: Hit Points: Low (6 + Constitution modifier at 1st level, 1d6 per level thereafter)
2880: Armor Proficiency: None (cloth only)
2881: Weapon Proficiency: Wands, staves, spell foci
2882: Tool Proficiency: Arcana tools, scribe's supplies
2883: 
2884: Progression Path
2885: 
2886: Level 1-2 (Base Mage Skills)
2887: Foundational skills before Arch class selection.
2888: 
2889: Passive Skills
2890: Spellcasting: Access to Evocation Tier I spells, focusing on direct damage.
2891: Resonance Pool: A reservoir of arcane energy that powers your spells and recovers during rest.
2892: Arcane Insight: Identify magical effects and items with greater accuracy than non-mages.
2893: Elemental Affinity: Gain a minor resistance to your chosen element once selected.
2894: Spell Precision: Reduce the chance of friendly fire damage from your area effect spells.
2895: 
2896: Active Skills
2897: Arcane Missile: Launch bolts of pure arcane energy that unerringly strike your target.
2898: Elemental Bolt: Cast a bolt of your chosen element (once selected) that deals additional effects based on the element.
2899: Resonance Shield: Convert a portion of your Resonance Points into a temporary protective barrier.
2900: Blink: Teleport a short distance to avoid danger or reposition.
2901: Detect Magic: Sense and identify magical auras and effects in your vicinity.
2902: 
2903: Level 3-9 (Arch Class: Arcanist)
2904: At level 3, mages specialize into the Arcanist arch class, refining their destructive capabilities and spell efficiency.
2905: 
2906: Elemental Focus (Choose One at Level 2)
2907: Fire: +10% damage, adds Burn DoT effect. Finisher: Inferno Lance – 4-tile line, DoT over time
2908: Cold: +5% crit chance, slows targets. Finisher: Glacier Spike – Cone AoE, 50% slow
2909: Lightning: +15% jump chance, arc disruption. Finisher: Storm Arc – Jumps to 3 enemies, stuns on crit
2910: 
2911: Passive Skills
2912: Arcane Stability: Reduce spell fizzle chance by 25%.
2913: Overload Crits: Critical strikes deal +50% splash damage to nearby enemies.
2914: Arcane Bypass: Once per rest, ignore all enemy resistances for one spell.
2915: Metaspell Access: Choose one Universal Metamagic effect to apply to a spell once per day.
2916: Elemental Mastery: Increase the effectiveness of your chosen element's secondary effects.
2917: Spell Efficiency: Reduce the Resonance Point cost of spells cast in sequence with the same element.
2918: 
2919: Active Skills
2920: Elemental Surge: Channel a concentrated burst of your chosen element, dealing high damage to a single target.
2921: Arcane Barrage: Launch multiple arcane projectiles that seek out different targets.
2922: Conjure Elemental Familiar: Summon a minor elemental of your chosen type to assist you in combat.
2923: Displacement: Create illusory duplicates of yourself while teleporting to safety.
2924: Counterspell: Disrupt an enemy's spellcasting, potentially negating their spell entirely.
2925: 
2926: Level 10+ (Hero Path Selection)
2927: At level 10, mages choose a Hero Path that reflects their philosophy and methods, further specializing their arcane abilities.
2928: 
2929: Archmage (Hero Path)
2930: "True mastery means knowing when not to destroy."
2931: 
2932: Passive Skills
2933: Ward of Purity: Create an AoE shield that reflects 25% of incoming spells.
2934: Celestial Comet: Cast a Radiant/Arcane AoE that blinds enemies.
2935: Empowered Link: Share buffs with allies in a 5-tile radius.
2936: Harmonized Core: Spells heal allies for 10% of base damage as temporary HP.
2937: 
2938: Active Skills
2939: Arcane Sanctuary: Create a zone of protection that shields allies and enhances their magical abilities.
2940: Purifying Beam: Channel a beam of radiant arcane energy that damages enemies and cleanses negative effects from allies.
2941: Spell Sharing: Temporarily grant an ally access to one of your spells.
2942: Resonance Transfusion: Transfer a portion of your Resonance Points to restore an ally's magical reserves.
2943: Arcane Convergence: Combine your magic with nearby allies for a coordinated, enhanced spell effect.
2944: 
2945: Elementalist (Hero Path)
2946: "I master not one element, but the storm that binds them all."
2947: 
2948: Passive Skills
2949: Elemental Pivot: Switch focus element mid-combat as a bonus action.
2950: Voltaic Pulse: Create an AoE jump-stun effect.
2951: Elemental Overload: Deal dual-element damage in a 3x3 blast.
2952: Elemental Supremacy: Deal +10% damage against elemental vulnerabilities; reset chain cooldown on kill.
2953: 
2954: Active Skills
2955: Elemental Fusion: Combine two elements to create a hybrid spell with unique effects.
2956: Cascade Storm: Trigger a sequence of elemental effects that build upon each other.
2957: Elemental Absorption: Temporarily absorb incoming elemental damage to enhance your next spell.
2958: Primal Channeling: Draw raw elemental energy from the environment to empower your spells.
2959: Elemental Mastery: Temporarily gain mastery over all elements, allowing you to use any elemental finisher regardless of your focus.
2960: 
2961: Lich (Hero Path)
2962: "I bled the world dry for this power. Now I bleed nothing."
2963: 
2964: To become a Lich, you must complete the multi-phase phylactery ritual at Level 10:
2965: 
2966: Phylactery Requirements:
2967: A Soul Coin or two participants (gains a bonus if they are willing)
2968: Defeat and bind 2-3 named mini-bosses as guardians
2969: Conduct the ritual in a planar nexus (e.g., Veil of Shadows)
2970: Survive a divine echo battle and pass a DC 20 INT + CON save
2971: 
2972: Failure Consequences:
2973: You become a melee undead NPC
2974: Permanently lose spellcasting
2975: Marked as a cursed being hostile to most factions
2976: Wear only cursed gear, gain no XP
2977: 
2978: Passive Skills
2979: Undying Spellform: When reduced to 0 HP, continue casting for 1 full round before death.
2980: Soul Reservoir: All DoT damage you deal feeds Resonance recovery.
2981: Necromantic Mastery: Gain access to enhanced necromancy spells and abilities.
2982: Undead Immunities: Become immune to poison, disease, fear, sleep, and mind control.
2983: 
2984: Active Skills
2985: Lingering Malice: Create a necrotic zone for 3 rounds that applies Fear or Drain effects.
2986: Resonance to Flesh: Convert Resonance into HP (or vice versa) once per rest.
2987: Flesh to Ash: Create an AoE burst that doubles damage if target is below 50% HP.
2988: Corpse Gateway: Teleport to a corpse within line of sight.
2989: Soulbound Ritual: Revive a fallen undead pet with bonus stats.
2990: 
2991: Undead Pet System
2992: Liches can maintain up to 3 elite undead companions:
2993: 
2994: | Pet Name | Role | Abilities |
2995: |----------|------|-----------|
2996: | Boneflayer | Ranged caster | Casts Bone Shards, Fear Pulse, AoE DoT |
2997: | Dreadblade | Melee disruptor | Crits apply Weakened, interrupts casting |
2998: | Wraithbound | Ethereal assassin | Invisible unless attacking, 20% stun chance |
2999: 
3000: Playing a Mage
3001: 
3002: As a mage in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on positioning, resource management, and strategic spell selection. Unlike more durable classes, you excel at dealing massive damage from a safe distance while controlling the battlefield through elemental effects.
3003: 
3004: Combat Strategy
3005: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path:
3006: 
3007: Archmage Mages should focus on supporting allies while still dealing significant damage, using their protective abilities to enhance the party's survivability and their radiant spells to control the battlefield.
3008: 
3009: Elementalist Mages excel at adapting to different combat situations, switching elements as needed to exploit enemy weaknesses and chain together devastating combinations of spells.
3010: 
3011: Lich Mages command the battlefield through necromantic power, using their undead minions to distract and disrupt enemies while they unleash devastating spells and drain life force to sustain themselves.
3012: 
3013: All mages should remember that positioning is crucial, your low health and armor make you vulnerable to direct attacks, so maintaining distance from enemies while still having line of sight to your targets is essential.
3014: 
3015: Reputation and Social Consequences
3016: Your choice of Hero path significantly affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
3017: 
3018: Archmage Mages are generally respected in civilized areas, their focus on controlled and beneficial magic making them valuable advisors and protectors.
3019: 
3020: Elementalist Mages often face a mixture of awe and wariness, their raw destructive potential balanced by their typically neutral stance on political matters.
3021: 
3022: Lich Mages are universally feared and hunted in most regions, their necromantic powers and undead nature making them anathema to the living. They often must hide their true nature or dwell in remote locations, interacting with society through intermediaries or disguises.
3023: 
3024: Notable Mage NPCs in Aethel
3025: 
3026: Archmage Elyndra Starweaver
3027: A high elf Archmage who serves as the head of the Imperial Arcane Academy. Her mastery of protective magic has saved the capital city multiple times from magical disasters, and her research into harmonic spell theory has revolutionized magical healing techniques.
3028: 
3029: Stormcaller Krazak
3030: An orc Elementalist who wanders the Storm Plains, studying the perpetual lightning storms that rage there. His ability to harness and redirect lightning has earned him the respect of even the most xenophobic human settlements, who seek his aid during the worst of the seasonal tempests.
3031: 
3032: The Bone Collector
3033: A human Lich who dwells in a tower at the edge of the Blighted Marsh. Few know his true name or history, but rumors speak of a once-noble mage who sought immortality to continue his research into curing a magical plague. Now, he sends his undead servants to collect rare components and magical artifacts, occasionally trading valuable arcane knowledge to those brave or desperate enough to seek him out.
3034: 
3035: Mirrorsage
3036: 
3037: "The half-elf's eyes gleamed with an otherworldly light as she faced the venomous serpent. As the creature lunged, spraying toxic venom, she made a complex gesture with her hands, capturing the essence of the attack in a shimmering mirror of arcane energy. With a fluid motion, she redirected the captured venom at the approaching bandits, who scattered in terror as their own ally's poison rained down upon them."
3038: 
3039: Mirrorsages are unique spellcasters who specialize in copying and reflecting the abilities of creatures they encounter. In the world of Aethel, these adaptive mages study the fundamental patterns of magic and life, learning to capture, store, and replicate the innate powers of beasts and monsters. Their versatility makes them unpredictable opponents and valuable allies, able to adapt their arsenal to meet any challenge.
3040: 
3041: Class Overview
3042: 
3043: As a Mirrorsage, you harness the power of reflection and mimicry to build a constantly evolving repertoire of abilities. Your specialized training allows you to observe and capture monster abilities, store them in Echo Slots, and either cast them directly or manifest them through summoned mirror beasts. Beyond your mimicry abilities, you excel at adapting to new threats, charming creatures fighting alongside you, and turning enemies' powers against them. In Aethel, mirrorsages might serve as monster hunters, collecting abilities from dangerous creatures; researchers studying the fundamental nature of magical abilities; or explorers venturing into unknown territories to discover and catalog new powers. Their unique approach to magic often makes them objects of curiosity and sometimes suspicion among more traditional spellcasters.
3044: 
3045: Core Mechanics
3046: 
3047: Echo Slots – Mirrorsages store monster abilities ("Echoes") in Echo Slots (initially 3, later 5). Each slot holds one captured Echo; using that Echo empties the slot until another ability is captured with Mirrorbind.
3048: 
3049: Mirrorbind – Once per encounter, attempt to copy an ability witnessed in the current battle. If successful, the ability is stored in an Echo Slot. Bosses and creatures with INT ≥ 7 cannot be mirrored unless specifically scripted to allow it.
3050: 
3051: Charm Bind – Once per long rest, charm a creature with INT ≤ 6 for 3 rounds. The charmed creature uses its own stats and abilities and counts as your Reflective Companion (replacing Mirror Beast while active).
3052: 
3053: Primary Attributes
3054: Main Attribute: Intelligence
3055: Secondary Attributes: Wisdom, Charisma
3056: Hit Points: Medium (8 + Constitution modifier at 1st level, 1d8 per level thereafter)
3057: Armor Proficiency: Light armor
3058: Weapon Proficiency: Simple weapons, short swords, hand crossbows
3059: Tool Proficiency: Calligrapher's supplies
3060: 
3061: Progression Path
3062: 
3063: Level 1-2 (Base Mirrorsage Skills)
3064: Foundational skills before Arch class selection.
3065: 
3066: Passive Skills
3067: Echo Channeling: Cast a stored Echo ability. Consumes a slot.
3068: Mirrorcall: Summon a Mirror Beast that mimics one stored Echo ability.
3069: Echo Slot Expansion I: Increase slots from 2 → 3.
3070: Reflective Pulse: First hit you take each round returns 10% damage.
3071: Beast Instinct: +10% chance to capture Echoes from INT ≤ 6 creatures.
3072: 
3073: Active Skills
3074: Mirrorbind: Attempt to capture an ability you've just witnessed, storing it in an Echo Slot if successful.
3075: Echo Release: Cast a stored Echo ability, consuming the slot.
3076: Mirror Beast Summon: Call forth a Mirror Beast that can use one of your stored Echo abilities.
3077: Charm Creature: Attempt to temporarily charm a low-intelligence creature to fight alongside you.
3078: Reflective Shield: Create a brief defensive barrier that has a chance to reflect the next attack back at the attacker.
3079: 
3080: Level 3-9 (Arch Class: Reflectionist)
3081: At level 3, mirrorsages specialize into the Reflectionist arch class, refining their mimicry abilities and developing more powerful reflection techniques.
3082: 
3083: Passive Skills
3084: Advanced Mirrorcall: Mirror Beast can now shift once per fight into a new form based on a second Echo.
3085: Echo Slot Expansion II: Max Echo Slots: 5
3086: Dual Echo Cast: Spend 2 Echoes in one turn; must be from different creatures.
3087: Mimicry Focus: Gain +1 Focus Point per battle. Can spend Focus to empower Echoes (double duration/damage).
3088: Mirror Shell: Once per long rest, reflect the first ability cast at you.
3089: Capstone – Perfect Reflection: Reflect any observed ability (spell, breath, strike) back at the caster for full effect (1/day).
3090: 
3091: Active Skills
3092: Echo Fusion: Temporarily combine two stored Echoes to create a hybrid ability with properties of both.
3093: Mirror Shift: Command your Mirror Beast to change form, adopting a different stored Echo ability.
3094: Focus Channel: Spend a Focus Point to significantly enhance your next Echo cast or Mirror Beast ability.
3095: Reflective Analysis: Study a creature to gain insight into its abilities and weaknesses, increasing your chance to successfully bind its Echoes.
3096: Mirror Dimension: Create a small pocket dimension of reflective surfaces that confuses enemies and enhances your mimicry abilities.
3097: 
3098: Level 10+ (Hero Path Selection)
3099: At level 10, mirrorsages choose a Hero Path that reflects their philosophy and methods, further specializing their reflection abilities.
3100: 
3101: Light Weaver (Hero Path)
3102: Copies divine and beneficial abilities. Summons radiant beasts and support Echoes.
3103: 
3104: Passive Skills
3105: Blessed Copy: Echoed heals or buffs also affect a second ally.
3106: Luminant Bond: Charmed/Beast emits aura that reveals invisible and grants +5% saves.
3107: Sacred Echo Slot: +1 additional slot for healing/support abilities.
3108: Final – Radiant Archive: Cast 3 stored divine Echoes at once (once/day).
3109: 
3110: Active Skills
3111: Divine Reflection: Capture and enhance beneficial abilities, increasing their healing or protective power.
3112: Radiant Mirror Beast: Summon a Mirror Beast infused with divine energy, capable of healing allies and revealing hidden threats.
3113: Blessing Echo: Transform a captured offensive Echo into a beneficial effect that can heal or protect allies.
3114: Light Binding: Create bonds of radiant energy that can restrain enemies or create protective barriers for allies.
3115: Purifying Reflection: Reflect and purify harmful effects, potentially converting them into healing or beneficial effects.
3116: 
3117: Mirror Mage (Hero Path)
3118: Pure mimicry and fusion-focused. Tactical duplicator and Echo manipulator.
3119: 
3120: Passive Skills
3121: Mirror Double: Create a copy of yourself for 1 turn. It casts your last Echo at 50% power.
3122: Fused Echo: Combine two Echoes to create a custom hybrid. Once per long rest.
3123: Floating Memory Slot: Temporarily store a 6th Echo. Auto-erases after combat.
3124: Final – Reflected Repertoire: Reset all Echo slots. Next 3 Echoes cast without consuming slots.
3125: 
3126: Active Skills
3127: Echo Manipulation: Modify a stored Echo, changing its element, range, or effect.
3128: Mirror Image: Create multiple illusory duplicates of yourself that can confuse enemies and potentially cast Echo abilities.
3129: Prismatic Reflection: Split a single-target Echo into multiple weaker versions that can affect several targets.
3130: Echo Preservation: Prevent an Echo from being consumed when cast, allowing it to be used again.
3131: Reality Fracture: Create a zone where Echo abilities are enhanced and have a chance to trigger additional effects.
3132: 
3133: Shattered One (Hero Path)
3134: Twists copied powers into destructive perversions. Uses glitched beastforms and corrupted abilities.
3135: 
3136: Passive Skills
3137: Corrupted Mirrorcall: Mirror Beast gains a random mutation (e.g., Burn Aura, Bleed Burst).
3138: Shatterbind: Echoes from undead/demons/aberrations gain necrotic or psychic damage tags.
3139: Fractured Echo: 25% chance each Echo triggers again randomly the next round.
3140: Final – Fractured Dominion: For 2 turns, every Echo cast also generates a Glitched Clone Echo that strikes a nearby enemy.
3141: 
3142: Active Skills
3143: Corrupt Echo: Twist a captured ability into a more destructive or debilitating version.
3144: Shattered Mirror Beast: Summon a corrupted Mirror Beast with unstable, mutated abilities that can harm both enemies and allies.
3145: Echo Leech: Drain life force from a target when casting certain Echo abilities, healing yourself.
3146: Fractured Reality: Create a zone of broken reflections that confuses and damages creatures within it.
3147: Mind Shatter: Use corrupted Echo energy to attack a creature's mind, potentially causing confusion or temporary insanity.
3148: 
3149: Echo Bestiary System
3150: All Echo-eligible creatures must have INT 6 or lower unless explicitly scripted as reflectable.
3151: 
3152: | Creature | INT | Bindable | Echo Example |
3153: |----------|-----|----------|--------------|
3154: | Venommaw Serpent | 2 | ✔️ | Venom Spray (Poison Cone) |
3155: | Thunder Roc | 4 | ✔️ | Storm Dive (Lightning AoE) |
3156: | Stonehide Troll | 6 | ✔️ | Regenerate Hide (+DR) |
3157: | Lich Priest | 10 | ❌ | ❌ Cannot be bound or echoed |
3158: 
3159: Playing a Mirrorsage
3160: 
3161: As a mirrorsage in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on adaptability, strategic Echo collection, and creative use of your mimicked abilities. Unlike other classes that have fixed spell lists or abilities, you build your arsenal dynamically based on the creatures you encounter.
3162: 
3163: Combat Strategy
3164: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path:
3165: 
3166: Light Weaver Mirrorsages should focus on capturing beneficial and protective abilities, using them to support allies while their Mirror Beasts provide additional utility and protection.
3167: 
3168: Mirror Mage Mirrorsages excel at tactical flexibility, using their ability to manipulate and combine Echoes to adapt to changing battlefield conditions and exploit enemy weaknesses.
3169: 
3170: Shattered One Mirrorsages thrive on chaos and destruction, corrupting captured abilities to maximize damage and debilitating effects, often at the risk of unpredictable consequences.
3171: 
3172: All mirrorsages should remember that Echo Slot management is crucial, deciding which abilities to capture and when to use them can dramatically affect your effectiveness in different situations.
3173: 
3174: Reputation and Social Consequences
3175: Your choice of Hero path affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
3176: 
3177: Light Weaver Mirrorsages are generally viewed positively, their focus on beneficial mimicry and protective abilities making them welcome allies in most communities.
3178: 
3179: Mirror Mage Mirrorsages are often regarded with curiosity and sometimes wariness, their neutral approach to mimicry and focus on pure replication making their intentions sometimes difficult to discern.
3180: 
3181: Shattered One Mirrorsages frequently face suspicion or outright hostility, their corruption of natural abilities and unpredictable nature making them unwelcome in many civilized areas.
3182: 
3183: Notable Mirrorsage NPCs in Aethel
3184: 
3185: Lumina Brightmirror
3186: A high elf Light Weaver who serves as a monster researcher for the Imperial Academy. She has cataloged hundreds of creature abilities and uses her mimicry to heal and protect explorers venturing into dangerous territories. Her radiant Mirror Beasts are often mistaken for celestial entities.
3187: 
3188: Prism
3189: A human Mirror Mage of unknown origin who travels between dimensions through reflective surfaces. His mastery of Echo manipulation allows him to combine abilities in ways thought impossible, creating entirely new effects. He appears at sites of magical disturbance, studies the phenomena, then vanishes without explanation.
3190: 
3191: The Fractured Queen
3192: A Umbrin Shattered One who rules a hidden court of corrupted mirror creatures. Her ability to twist and pervert captured abilities has created an army of nightmarish beasts that serve her whims. Those who venture into her domain find themselves facing corrupted reflections of their own abilities and worst fears made manifest.
3193: 
3194: Monk
3195: 
3196: "The human monk moved with impossible grace, her body flowing like water between the attacks of her opponents. Each stance shift was seamless, each strike precise. As three attackers converged on her, she simply smiled, centered her breathing, and in a blur of motion, left all three sprawled unconscious on the ground."
3197: 
3198: Monks are masters of martial arts who harness their inner energy, known as ki, to perform extraordinary physical feats. Through rigorous training and meditation, they achieve a harmony of body, mind, and spirit that grants them capabilities beyond ordinary mortals, allowing them to fight effectively without weapons or armor.
3199: 
3200: Class Overview
3201: 
3202: As a monk, you harness the power of ki to enhance your natural abilities and execute specialized martial techniques. Your training focuses on stance-based combat, allowing you to adapt to different situations by shifting your fighting style. Beyond your combat abilities, you excel at mobility, self-sufficiency, and maintaining inner balance in chaotic situations.
3203: 
3204: In Aethel, monks might belong to secluded mountain monasteries, serve as guardians of sacred sites, or travel the world seeking to perfect their art through practical experience. Some follow structured traditions with formal hierarchies, while others develop their abilities through intuitive self-discovery and adaptation.
3205: 
3206: Core Mechanics
3207: 
3208: Ki Flow – Monks build Ki Chain (0-5) through movement + attack combos, critical strikes, or defensive reactions. This resource can be spent to fuel advanced techniques or trigger powerful effects. Managing Ki Chain effectively is central to mastering the monk class.
3209: 
3210: Stance System – Monks can switch between specialized combat stances as a free action once per round, adapting to changing battlefield conditions.
3211: 
3212: Unarmored Flow – Monks gain bonus dodge, initiative, and stance effects while wearing no armor, emphasizing mobility and agility over heavy protection.
3213: 
3214: Primary Attributes
3215: Main Attribute: Dexterity
3216: Secondary Attributes: Wisdom, Constitution
3217: Hit Points: Medium (8 + Constitution modifier at 1st level, 1d8 per level thereafter)
3218: Armor Proficiency: None (benefits from Unarmored Flow)
3219: Weapon Proficiency: Simple weapons, monk weapons (kama, nunchaku, staff)
3220: Tool Proficiency: One artisan's tool of your choice
3221: 
3222: Progression Path
3223: 
3224: Level 1-2 (Base Monk Skills)
3225: Foundational skills before Arch class selection.
3226: 
3227: Monk Stances
3228: Tiger Stance (Offense): +15% attack speed, +10% crit chance on unarmed strikes. Gain Ki Chain on crits.
3229: Tortoise Stance (Off-Tank): +20% DR, soft taunt aura (enemies within 2 tiles targeting allies get -20% accuracy). Blocks/dodges grant Ki Chain.
3230: Crane Stance (Support): +10% healing received. Moving 3+ tiles grants +15% dodge. At 5 Ki, next strike heals self and ally.
3231: 
3232: Passive Skills
3233: Flowing Form: Alternating move + strike each round grants +1 Ki Chain.
3234: Striking Palm: Crits with unarmed attacks deal +50% armor-piercing internal damage.
3235: Unarmored Speed: +10% movement and initiative while unarmored.
3236: Stance Shift: Switch stance freely once per round.
3237: Discipline Initiate: Choose a discipline (offense, tank, support) and gain 1 technique based on it.
3238: 
3239: Active Skills
3240: Flurry of Blows: Deliver a rapid sequence of unarmed strikes, with each hit having a chance to generate Ki Chain.
3241: Deflect Missile: React to a ranged attack by reducing its damage and potentially catching the projectile.
3242: Step of the Wind: Harness your ki to dramatically increase your movement speed and jumping distance for one turn.
3243: Patient Defense: Enter a defensive state that increases your dodge chance and generates Ki Chain when successfully avoiding attacks.
3244: Stunning Strike: Channel ki into a precise strike that can temporarily paralyze an opponent if they fail to resist.
3245: 
3246: Level 3-9 (Arch Class: Master)
3247: At level 3, monks specialize into the Master arch class, refining their ki manipulation and developing more advanced martial techniques.
3248: 
3249: Passive Skills
3250: Combo Step: Use three different unarmed attacks in one round to stun the target.
3251: Ki Reversal: At 4+ Ki Chain, auto-counter the next melee hit.
3252: Perfect Balance: Standing still: +15% crit. Moving: +15% dodge.
3253: Breath of Focus: Skip an attack to gain +1 Ki Chain.
3254: Rhythmic Flow: Every 3rd round, next strike auto-crits at max Ki.
3255: Martial Apex (Capstone): At 5 Ki, your next ability costs no action and deals +100% damage (1/encounter).
3256: 
3257: Tortoise Off-Tank Discipline (Integrated at Arch)
3258: Stone Shell Discipline: +15% DR and Ki Chain protection at 3+ Ki. Immune to forced movement.
3259: Redirecting Palm: Spend 1 Ki to redirect 50% of incoming melee damage to a nearby enemy.
3260: Iron Shell Pulse: Spend 3 Ki: grant nearby allies 10% DR and retaliation aura.
3261: Echoing Guard: Blocking/dodging applies Stagger (-10% damage, -1 move).
3262: Final Form – Bastion of Breath: Become immune for 1 round. Enemies who strike are stunned. On next round, release a force shockwave.
3263: 
3264: Active Skills
3265: Whirlwind Stance: Enter a flowing stance that allows you to strike multiple adjacent enemies with each attack.
3266: Focused Ki Burst: Channel your ki into a powerful energy discharge that damages enemies in a small area.
3267: Diamond Soul: Temporarily harden your body against physical and magical attacks, gaining resistance to damage.
3268: Pressure Point Strike: Target a specific vulnerability in your opponent, potentially disabling a limb or ability temporarily.
3269: Meditative Healing: Enter a brief meditative state that rapidly restores your health and ki.
3270: 
3271: Level 10+ (Hero Path Selection)
3272: At level 10, monks choose a Hero Path that reflects their philosophy and methods, further specializing their martial abilities.
3273: 
3274: Enlightened Master (Hero Path)
3275: Uses martial wisdom to heal, cleanse, and fortify the party.
3276: 
3277: Passive Skills
3278: Tranquil Lotus Step: Move to ally, absorb their debuff, and grant +15% healing received (2 Ki).
3279: Serenity Breaker Palm: Strike enemy; cleanse allies in 2 tiles (3 Ki).
3280: Balance Beyond Flesh: Allies in Crane stance range heal 5% each time they spend a resource.
3281: Final – Breath of the Thousand Petals: AoE revive and death immunity (1 round).
3282: 
3283: Active Skills
3284: Healing Touch: Channel positive ki energy through your hands to heal injuries and cure minor ailments.
3285: Purifying Palm: Strike an enemy while simultaneously cleansing negative effects from nearby allies.
3286: Circle of Harmony: Create a zone of balanced energy that gradually restores health to allies within it.
3287: Shared Breath: Temporarily link your life force with an ally, sharing damage and healing effects.
3288: Transcendent Meditation: Enter a deep meditative state that dramatically accelerates healing for yourself and nearby allies.
3289: 
3290: Iron Fist Master (Hero Path)
3291: The battlefield is your dojo. Your fists are your verdict.
3292: 
3293: Passive Skills
3294: Ribcrusher Strike: Heavy strike, reduces enemy max HP by 10% (2 Ki).
3295: Centerline Blitz: Dash in a line, striking all targets. Final one is stunned at max Ki (3 Ki).
3296: Steel Reflection Form: Reflect 15% melee damage for 3 turns.
3297: Final – Limit Break Spiral: 2 bonus actions; all strikes crit this turn (1/day).
3298: 
3299: Active Skills
3300: Iron Palm: Channel ki into your strike to ignore a portion of the target's armor and resistance.
3301: Flowing Combo: Execute a series of perfectly coordinated strikes that build upon each other, with increasing damage per hit.
3302: Focused Breathing: Enter a state of perfect concentration, significantly increasing your critical hit chance for a short duration.
3303: Bone-Breaking Strike: Deliver a powerful blow that can temporarily reduce the target's maximum health.
3304: One Hundred Fists: Ultimate technique that allows you to strike with incredible speed, delivering numerous attacks in a single moment.
3305: 
3306: Way of Shadow (Hero Path)
3307: Master of pressure-point disruption, deception, and darkened breath.
3308: 
3309: Passive Skills
3310: Veinlock Nerve Jab: Strike blocks healing/resource gain (2 turns). Silences if hidden (2 Ki).
3311: Twilight Mirage: Summon a 1-turn clone to mirror strikes and intercept attacks (3 Ki).
3312: Nerve Fade Technique: Stacking Shadow Rot debuff (-saves, psychic DoT) (1 Ki).
3313: Final – Deathless Silence: Untargetable 1 turn → teleport to 3 enemies and apply Void Palm (max HP -25%, passive disable).
3314: 
3315: Active Skills
3316: Shadow Step: Briefly step into the shadow realm, becoming untargetable and reappearing at a different location.
3317: Nerve Strike: Target critical pressure points to temporarily disable an opponent's abilities or movement.
3318: Poison Hand: Infuse your strikes with toxic ki that causes continuing damage over time.
3319: Mind Cloud: Disrupt an enemy's mental clarity, causing confusion or hallucinations.
3320: Death Touch: Legendary technique that can potentially kill a weakened target instantly by stopping their heart.
3321: 
3322: Playing a Monk
3323: 
3324: As a monk in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on stance management, positioning, and strategic use of your Ki Chain. Unlike other classes that might rely on equipment or external power sources, you excel at adapting to changing situations through stance shifts and precise application of martial techniques.
3325: 
3326: Combat Strategy
3327: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path and current stance:
3328: 
3329: Enlightened Masters should focus on maintaining Crane Stance when healing is needed, shifting to other stances to build Ki Chain for supportive techniques. They excel at removing debuffs and providing sustainable healing over time.
3330: 
3331: Iron Fist Masters typically favor Tiger Stance for its offensive bonuses, building Ki Chain through critical hits and spending it on powerful striking techniques. They should focus on positioning to maximize their damage potential.
3332: 
3333: Way of Shadow monks utilize stealth and deception, often opening combat with ambush techniques before applying debilitating pressure point strikes. They excel at disabling key targets and creating chaos among enemy ranks.
3334: 
3335: All monks should remember that stance shifting is crucial, adapting to the flow of battle by changing stances at the right moment can dramatically increase your effectiveness.
3336: 
3337: Reputation and Social Consequences
3338: Your choice of Hero path affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
3339: 
3340: Enlightened Masters are generally respected for their wisdom and healing abilities, often welcomed in communities that value balance and harmony.
3341: 
3342: Iron Fist Masters are known for their disciplined approach to combat and fair judgment, making them respected if somewhat feared in many regions.
3343: 
3344: Way of Shadow monks are often viewed with suspicion due to their association with pressure point techniques and life force manipulation, sometimes forcing them to conceal their true abilities in certain regions.
3345: 
3346: Notable Monk NPCs in Aethel
3347: 
3348: Master Wei Longfist
3349: A human Enlightened Master who leads the Monastery of the Jade Mountain. He is known for his healing touch and ability to cleanse even the most persistent curses. Despite his advanced age, his movements remain fluid and precise, a testament to his lifetime of disciplined practice.
3350: 
3351: Iron Fist Kora
3352: A Drakari Iron Fist Master who travels between fighting tournaments, testing her skills against worthy opponents. Her signature Ribcrusher Strike has ended many matches in seconds, and her fair judgment as a referee is sought after by tournament organizers throughout Aethel.
3353: 
3354: Nightshade
3355: A mysterious elf who follows the Way of Shadow, known only by whispers in the criminal underworld. Those who have survived encounters with Nightshade speak of paralyzed limbs, stolen memories, and the terrifying experience of fighting an opponent who seems to materialize from darkness itself.
3356: 
3357: Oracle
3358: 
3359: "The elven oracle's eyes rolled back, revealing only white as she spoke in a voice not her own. 'The path splits three ways before the ancient stone. Choose the shadow road, though it seems most perilous. What waits in light is death disguised.' As suddenly as it had come, the trance lifted, leaving her momentarily disoriented, the burden of prophecy etched in the lines of her face."
3360: 
3361: Oracles are living conduits of divine mystery, cursed and blessed in equal measure. In the world of Aethel, these individuals do not pray for magic, they burst with it, fractured by fate, wounded by prophecy, or chosen by powers beyond comprehension. Unlike clerics who serve deities through devotion, oracles are marked by divine forces, their bodies and minds transformed into vessels for cosmic power whether they desire it or not.
3362: 
3363: Class Overview
3364: 
3365: As an oracle, you channel divine revelations through your very being, manifesting supernatural abilities tied to your specific Mystery. Your power comes at a price, a permanent Curse that marks you as touched by forces beyond mortal understanding. This duality of blessing and affliction defines your journey, as both your Mystery and Curse grow more powerful as you advance.
3366: 
3367: In Aethel, oracles might serve as advisors to rulers, wandering prophets warning of coming dangers, reclusive seers sought out by the desperate, or reluctant vessels struggling to control the divine power flowing through them. Some embrace their fate, while others rage against it, but all are forever changed by the mysteries they embody.
3368: 
3369: Core Mechanics
3370: 
3371: Mystery – Each oracle is bound to a specific Mystery that determines their Revelation tree, passive effects, and thematic focus. This Mystery shapes how your divine power manifests and what types of Revelations you can access.
3372: 
3373: Curse – Every oracle bears a permanent divine affliction that cannot be removed. While limiting in some ways, your Curse also grants increasingly powerful boons as you advance in level, eventually becoming a source of tremendous power.
3374: 
3375: Revelation Charges – Oracles do not use Resonance Points. They use Revelation Charges (initially 3, maximum 7) to power divine abilities. Each Mystery includes a unique Prophecy Trigger that allows you to recover charges in thematic ways.
3376: 
3377: Primary Attributes
3378: Main Attribute: Charisma (determines Revelation potency, saves, aura strength)
3379: Secondary Attributes: Wisdom (for Curse triggers, insight, and resistances)
3380: Hit Points: Medium (6 + Constitution modifier at 1st level, 1d6 per level thereafter)
3381: Armor Proficiency: Light armor
3382: Weapon Proficiency: Simple weapons and symbolic weapons (dagger, staff, relics)
3383: Tool Proficiency: Divination tools
3384: 
3385: Progression Path
3386: 
3387: Level 1-2 (Base Oracle Skills)
3388: At first level, you choose your Mystery and Curse, which will define your oracle's journey.
3389: 
3390: Mysteries (Choose One)
3391: Ancestor – Connection to ancestral spirits and legacy
3392: Battle – Tactical combat, divine fury, command under fire
3393: Bones – Undeath, decay, and corpse manipulation
3394: Cataclysm – Destruction, upheaval, and instability
3395: Dream – Illusion, unreality, condition-based control
3396: Flame – Fire, passion, and wrathful healing
3397: Life – Restoration, overflow healing, and protective blooms
3398: Shadow – Stealth, concealment, and psychic strikes
3399: Stars – Radiant clarity and foresight
3400: Time – Delay, reversal, and causality control
3401: 
3402: Curses (Choose One)
3403: Spiral-Touched – Manipulates initiative, gains time-based echoes
3404: Wound Eater – Cannot heal naturally, but drains enemies to live
3405: Soul-Cracked – Random alignment/magic shifts, immune to control
3406: Haunted – Spirits constantly surround you, both helping and hindering
3407: Tongues – Speaks in multiple languages simultaneously, causing confusion
3408: Burning Blood – Your blood ignites when exposed to air, damaging you and enemies
3409: False Life – You appear dead to divination and healing magic
3410: Shattered Mind – Your thoughts fragment, granting multiple partial actions
3411: Consumed – Your body slowly transforms into your Mystery's element
3412: Fate-Bound – Your destiny is locked, granting foresight but limiting choices
3413: 
3414: Active Skills
3415: Channel Revelation – Activate a Revelation from your Mystery, consuming Revelation Charges.
3416: Prophetic Insight – Gain a brief glimpse of possible futures, providing advantage on your next action.
3417: Curse Manifestation – Temporarily intensify your Curse to gain its benefits at the cost of increased limitations.
3418: Divine Sense – Detect the presence and general nature of divine, undead, or extraplanar entities.
3419: Oracular Trance – Enter a prophetic state to receive visions related to a specific question or situation.
3420: 
3421: Level 3-9 (Revelation Tiers)
3422: As you advance, you gain access to increasingly powerful Revelations tied to your Mystery.
3423: 
3424: Tier I Revelation (Level 3)
3425: Choose one Revelation from your Mystery's Tier I options. These foundational powers cost 1 Revelation Charge and establish your basic divine abilities.
3426: 
3427: Curse Boon I (Level 3)
3428: Your Curse manifests its first significant boon, granting a minor but useful ability related to your affliction.
3429: 
3430: Curse Boon II (Level 5)
3431: Your Curse deepens, providing a moderate boon that enhances your capabilities.
3432: 
3433: Tier II Revelation (Level 6)
3434: Choose one Revelation from your Mystery's Tier II options. These intermediate powers cost 2 Revelation Charges and significantly expand your divine repertoire.
3435: 
3436: Tier III Revelation (Level 9)
3437: Gain access to your Mystery's Tier III Revelation. This powerful ability costs 3 Revelation Charges and represents mastery of your Mystery's fundamental nature.
3438: 
3439: Curse Boon III (Level 10)
3440: Your Curse evolves further, granting a strong boon that substantially enhances your capabilities.
3441: 
3442: Mystic Insight (Level 12)
3443: Choose one Minor Revelation or passive enhancement from your Mystery, representing deepening insight into your divine connection.
3444: 
3445: Revelation Mastery (Level 15)
3446: Choose one known Revelation to empower or modify, such as reducing its cost, extending its duration, or adding a bonus effect.
3447: 
3448: Curse Boon IV (Level 15)
3449: Your Curse reaches a transformative stage, fundamentally altering your relationship with your divine affliction.
3450: 
3451: Prophetic Surge (Level 18)
3452: Once per day, cast any known Revelation at no cost or extend a Revelation's effect for an additional turn.
3453: 
3454: Final Revelation (Level 20)
3455: Gain access to your Mystery's ultimate power, a legendary ability representing the full manifestation of your divine connection.
3456: 
3457: Curse Boon V (Level 20)
3458: Your Curse reaches its legendary culmination, granting an extraordinary power unique to your specific affliction.
3459: 
3460: Example Mystery: Ancestor
3461: 
3462: Patron: The Echo Court (an ancestral spirit chorus that binds lineage across time)
3463: 
3464: Theme: Memory, spirits, legacy, the wisdom of the dead
3465: 
3466: Prophecy Trigger: When you aid an ally who lands a critical hit, regain 1 Revelation Charge
3467: 
3468: Level 1 Revelation – Ancestral Guardian
3469: Passive: At all times, a spectral guardian from your lineage hovers beside you.
3470: Effect: You gain +1 AC against the first attack each round and +5% resistance to mind-affecting effects.
3471: Once per long rest, you may intercept a single-target attack against an ally within 3 tiles, your guardian takes the hit and vanishes until long rest.
3472: 
3473: Level 3 – Choose 1 Tier I Revelation
3474: Echo Voice
3475: Passive: You can communicate telepathically with allies within 5 tiles.
3476: Active: Spend 1 Revelation Charge to let an ally repeat their last failed roll (attack, save, or skill) with a +2 bonus.
3477: 
3478: Ancestral Strike
3479: Active: Spend 1 Revelation Charge to channel the strength of a warrior ancestor.
3480: Your next weapon or touch attack deals +50% bonus weapon damage and stuns on crit.
3481: 
3482: Bloodline Sight
3483: Active: You may spend 1 Revelation Charge to reveal all undead, invisible, or ethereal creatures within 6 tiles for 2 rounds.
3484: During this effect, they cannot benefit from concealment or stealth.
3485: 
3486: Level 6 – Choose 1 Tier II Revelation
3487: Spirit Walk
3488: Active: Spend 2 Revelation Charges to leave your body for 1 turn.
3489: You gain intangible form, move through walls/creatures, cannot be targeted, and may cast a Revelation through your spirit at full range.
3490: At the end of the turn, you return to your body in your original or new tile.
3491: 
3492: Echo Armor
3493: Passive: You gain +10% resistance to non-magical damage.
3494: Once per short rest, when reduced to 0 HP, an ancestral echo absorbs the blow and leaves you at 1 HP.
3495: 
3496: Ghost Chant
3497: Active: Spend 1 Revelation Charge to inflict Dread on enemies within 3 tiles (Fear-based).
3498: Affected enemies suffer -10% hit chance and -10% saves for 2 turns.
3499: 
3500: Level 9 – Tier III Revelation – Bloodline Convergence
3501: Active: Spend 3 Revelation Charges to invoke the entire ancestral line in spectral form.
3502: For 2 turns:
3503: You gain +2 actions per turn (Revelation or movement only)
3504: Allies within 5 tiles gain +10% crit chance, +5% DR, and immunity to Fear
3505: You may cast 1 Revelation per round at no cost
3506: At the end of the effect, gain Exhausted (-1 action, -10% resist for 1 round)
3507: 
3508: Level 12 – Mystic Insight – Ancestral Whispers
3509: Passive: You constantly hear the whispers of your ancestors, granting you advantage on History checks and immunity to being surprised.
3510: Once per day, you may consult your ancestors for guidance on a specific question, receiving cryptic but useful insights.
3511: 
3512: Level 15 – Revelation Mastery
3513: Choose one of your known Revelations to enhance:
3514: Enhanced Echo Voice: Now affects all allies within range simultaneously
3515: Enhanced Spirit Walk: Duration increased to 2 turns
3516: Enhanced Bloodline Convergence: No longer causes Exhaustion
3517: 
3518: Level 18 – Prophetic Surge – Ancestral Confluence
3519: Once per day, you may call upon all your ancestors simultaneously to cast any Revelation at no charge cost.
3520: Additionally, for 1 turn after using this ability, all your Revelations cost 1 fewer charge (minimum 0).
3521: 
3522: Level 20 – Final Revelation: Echo Crowned
3523: You can now maintain 3 passive ancestral spirits simultaneously (one offense, one defense, one utility)
3524: Once per day, you may cast any Revelation twice in one turn using different Echoes
3525: You may sacrifice an Echo to prevent a party member's death or restore all Revelation Charges instantly
3526: 
3527: Example Curse: Spiral-Touched
3528: 
3529: Those marked by the Spiral-Touched curse exist partially outside normal time. Their perception shifts between past, present, and future, making normal conversation difficult but granting unique insights into the flow of events.
3530: 
3531: Curse Limitations
3532: You sometimes respond to events before they happen, causing confusion in social situations
3533: You occasionally experience time differently than others, potentially missing turns in combat (5% chance)
3534: You cannot maintain concentration on mundane tasks for extended periods
3535: 
3536: Curse Boon I (Level 3)
3537: You can see 1 round into the future during initiative rolls, granting +3 to initiative
3538: Once per day, you may reroll a failed save as you briefly step outside the timestream
3539: 
3540: Curse Boon II (Level 5)
3541: You may spend 1 Revelation Charge to act out of turn once per combat
3542: You gain resistance to time-based magic and aging effects
3543: 
3544: Curse Boon III (Level 10)
3545: When you would be reduced to 0 HP, you may instead phase out of time for 1 round, becoming untargetable and regaining 25% of your maximum HP
3546: This ability can be used once per long rest
3547: 
3548: Curse Boon IV (Level 15)
3549: You exist in multiple timestreams simultaneously, allowing you to make two different actions in a single turn once per combat
3550: Enemies attacking you have a 10% chance to hit your echo instead, causing the attack to miss
3551: 
3552: Curse Boon V (Level 20)
3553: You gain the ability to create a time loop, resetting an entire combat encounter to its beginning state once per day
3554: All allies retain knowledge of the original timeline, allowing them to make different choices
3555: 
3556: Playing an Oracle
3557: 
3558: As an oracle in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on managing your Revelation Charges, leveraging your Curse's benefits while mitigating its drawbacks, and selecting Revelations that complement your party's needs and your personal playstyle.
3559: 
3560: Combat Strategy
3561: Your approach to combat will vary significantly based on your chosen Mystery:
3562: 
3563: Ancestor Oracles excel at supporting allies through spiritual guidance and protection, calling upon ancestral spirits to enhance the party's capabilities while providing tactical advantages.
3564: 
3565: Battle Oracles focus on enhancing combat effectiveness through divine fury and tactical insights, often taking a more frontline role than other oracle types.
3566: 
3567: Bones Oracles manipulate the forces of death and decay, controlling undead and draining life force to weaken enemies and strengthen allies.
3568: 
3569: Cataclysm Oracles harness destructive forces, excelling at area damage and battlefield control through earthquakes, storms, and other natural disasters.
3570: 
3571: Dream Oracles specialize in reality manipulation, creating illusions and altering perception to confuse enemies and protect allies.
3572: 
3573: Flame Oracles channel passionate fire, dealing significant damage while also providing wrathful healing to allies.
3574: 
3575: Life Oracles focus on powerful healing and protection, creating zones of restoration and shielding allies from harm.
3576: 
3577: Shadow Oracles master stealth and concealment, striking from darkness and manipulating fear to control the battlefield.
3578: 
3579: Stars Oracles harness cosmic radiance for divination and precision strikes, seeing paths to victory others cannot perceive.
3580: 
3581: Time Oracles manipulate the flow of time itself, altering initiative, reversing damage, and creating temporal anomalies to gain advantage.
3582: 
3583: All oracles should remember that Revelation Charge management is crucial, knowing when to spend charges and when to conserve them can dramatically affect your effectiveness in extended encounters.
3584: 
3585: Reputation and Social Consequences
3586: Your choice of Mystery and Curse affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
3587: 
3588: Ancestor, Life, and Stars Oracles are often welcomed in civilized areas, their connections to positive divine forces making them valued advisors and healers.
3589: 
3590: Battle, Dream, and Time Oracles typically maintain neutral reputations, their powers viewed with a mixture of respect and wariness depending on how they're employed.
3591: 
3592: Bones, Cataclysm, Flame, and Shadow Oracles frequently face suspicion or fear, their connection to destructive or unsettling forces making many uncomfortable in their presence.
3593: 
3594: Regardless of Mystery, all oracles face some degree of superstition and misunderstanding due to their Curses, which mark them as fundamentally different from ordinary mortals. Some cultures revere oracles as living prophets, while others view them with suspicion as harbingers of change or doom.
3595: 
3596: Notable Oracle NPCs in Aethel
3597: 
3598: Seer Elindra
3599: A high elf Ancestor Oracle who serves as royal advisor to the Imperial Court. Her ability to consult with the spirits of past rulers provides invaluable guidance in matters of state. Though respected for her wisdom, her tendency to address people who aren't present (yet) makes many courtiers uncomfortable.
3600: 
3601: Warlord Krazak
3602: An orc Battle Oracle whose tactical visions have led his clan to numerous victories. His Spiral-Touched curse causes him to sometimes issue commands for battles that haven't yet begun, but his warriors have learned to trust these seemingly nonsensical orders that inevitably prove prescient.
3603: 
3604: The Withered One
3605: A human Bones Oracle who dwells in a mausoleum at the edge of civilization. Those desperate enough seek her counsel, for she can speak with the recently deceased to reveal their final secrets. Her Wound Eater curse has transformed her into a gaunt, corpse-like figure who must drain life to sustain herself, making each consultation a dangerous bargain.
3606: 
3607: Paladin
3608:
