4251: Net Bolt: Fire a specialized bolt that snares the target on hit (no save).
4252: Shatterburst: Fire a cone volley that imposes a -1 attack penalty on enemies for 1 round.
4253: Repel Shot: Use your reaction to knock back a melee attacker (2 charges).
4254: Turret Deployment: Deploy up to 2 automated turrets that last 3 rounds, firing for 1d6+WIS damage and applying Snare or Disrupt effects.
4255: Bubble of Discipline: Create a 3x3 area effect that blocks 1 spell per ally for 2 rounds (once per rest).
4256: 
4257: Tyrant (Hero Path)
4258: "You move when I allow it. You die when I command it."
4259: 
4260: Passive Skills
4261: Intimidating Presence: Enemies that can see you have disadvantage on morale checks.
4262: Cruel Efficiency: Gain a stacking damage bonus when hitting the same target consecutively.
4263: Marked for Death: Enemies you designate take –1 to saves and deal –1 damage vs allies other than you.
4264: Ruthless Tactician: Gain advantage on attacks against enemies that are flanked or hindered by terrain.
4265: 
4266: Active Skills
4267: Crushing Decree: Taunt enemies in range, forcing them to target you.
4268: Impale Line: Fire a shot that travels in a 4-tile line; if it hits 2+ targets, all enemies lose their reactions.
4269: Execution Zone: Create an area where enemies take –1 to morale and cannot escape.
4270: Demoralizing Shot: Hit an enemy leader to impose disadvantage on all nearby enemy attacks.
4271: Brutal Example: Deal massive damage to a weakened target, causing nearby enemies to become frightened.
4272: 
4273: Palisade System
4274: 
4275: As a soldier, you can deploy found material barriers on the battlefield as a free action once per round (1 use per encounter, increasing to 2 at level 13+).
4276: 
4277: Palisade Properties:
4278: Size: 2x1 tiles
4279: Durability: HP = 5 + your Constitution modifier
4280: Effects: Counts as cover and terrain, blocks line of sight and movement
4281: Tier II Upgrade (level 13): Palisades resist AoE spells and provide +1 AC to adjacent allies
4282: 
4283: Palisades are essential for many of your abilities, including:
4284: Hold Position: Gain +1 AC and +1 to saves when adjacent to a palisade
4285: Brace Line: Allies behind you gain +2 DR vs ranged attacks when you're near a palisade
4286: Tactical Advantage: Some of your shots gain bonuses when fired from near a palisade
4287: 
4288: Overwatch System
4289: 
4290: During your turn, you can declare a 3x3 zone that you monitor. Until your next turn, you may use 1 reaction per round to fire on the first enemy who:
4291: Enters or moves through the zone
4292: Attacks an ally within the zone
4293: Reveals themselves (e.g., from stealth or invisibility)
4294: 
4295: Your Overwatch attack deals 1d8 + Dexterity modifier damage and applies one of the following debuffs of your choice:
4296: Snare: Reduce target's movement speed
4297: Bleed: Deal additional damage over time
4298: Pushback: Force the target back 1 tile
4299: 
4300: At level 17, you gain Overwatch Advance, allowing you to shift 1 tile in any direction after making an Overwatch attack.
4301: 
4302: Playing a Soldier
4303: 
4304: As a soldier in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on positioning, battlefield awareness, and proactive defense. Unlike traditional tanks that absorb damage directly, you excel at preventing damage to your allies through strategic control of the battlefield.
4305: 
4306: Combat Strategy
4307: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path:
4308: 
4309: Champion Soldiers should focus on protecting the most vulnerable party members, using Guardian's Rush to intercept attacks and Wall of Aegis to bolster allies' defenses.
4310: 
4311: Artillerist Soldiers excel at zone control through a combination of turrets, palisades, and specialized ammunition, creating areas where enemies struggle to operate effectively.
4312: 
4313: Tyrant Soldiers dominate through intimidation and forced movement, creating zones of control that enemies either avoid or enter at great risk.
4314: 
4315: All soldiers should remember that proper positioning is crucial, your abilities are most effective when you can create chokepoints, cover firing lanes, and maintain clear lines of sight to both enemies and allies.
4316: 
4317: Reputation and Social Consequences
4318: Your choice of Hero path significantly affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
4319: 
4320: Champion Soldiers are respected and sought after as personal guards for nobility and as elite defenders of important locations. Their selfless protection makes them welcome in most civilized areas.
4321: 
4322: Artillerist Soldiers are valued for their technical expertise and battlefield control, often finding employment with mercenary companies or as specialists in military units focused on siege warfare or defensive operations.
4323: 
4324: Tyrant Soldiers inspire fear rather than respect, their ruthless efficiency making them valuable to warlords and tyrants but mistrusted in more peaceful regions. They often serve as enforcers or commanders of occupation forces.
4325: 
4326: Notable Soldier NPCs in Aethel
4327: 
4328: Captain Elara Steelshield
4329: A human Champion who commands the Royal Guard of the Imperial Capital. Her tactical genius and selfless protection of the royal family during an assassination attempt earned her legendary status. She's known for her ability to coordinate complex defensive formations that can repel forces many times her unit's size.
4330: 
4331: Tinkerer Draz
4332: A goblin Artillerist who serves as the defensive specialist for the Silver Coin mercenary company. His intricate network of turrets and palisades once held a mountain pass against an entire orcish warband for three days until reinforcements arrived. He constantly experiments with new ammunition types and deployable defenses.
4333: 
4334: The Iron Marshal
4335: A Umbrin Tyrant who rules the border fortress of Blackwatch with an iron fist. His reputation for ruthless efficiency has deterred multiple invasion attempts, as enemy commanders fear his ability to turn the battlefield into a killing ground through precise control of movement and morale. Refugees from neighboring regions speak of his execution zones with hushed terror.
4336: 
4337: Thief
4338: 
4339: "The halfling thief moved like a shadow through the noble's mansion, her nimble fingers making quick work of the lock on the study door. Inside, she spotted the jeweled dagger displayed on the wall, her target. As she approached, her trained eye spotted the nearly invisible tripwire connected to an alarm bell. With practiced precision, she disabled the trap, applied a drop of silencer's sting to her dagger, and waited in the shadows for the guard to make his rounds. One silent strike later, the guard slumped unconscious against the wall as she vanished into the night with her prize."
4340: 
4341: Thieves are masters of ambush, poisons, and disabling strikes. In the world of Aethel, these precision-focused strikers and stealth disruptors leverage critical hits and poisoncraft to eliminate key threats, bypass defenses, and dismantle enemy cohesion.
4342: 
4343: Class Overview
4344: 
4345: As a thief, you excel at striking from the shadows, using poisons and precise attacks to disable opponents before they can react. Your specialty lies in critical damage, crowd control, and non-magical utility, making you invaluable in trap-heavy environments, high-mobility fights, and caster-heavy encounters.
4346: 
4347: In Aethel, thieves might serve as guild assassins, noble spies, treasure hunters, or specialized scouts for mercenary companies. Their combination of stealth, precision, and technical skill makes them sought after for both legitimate and illicit operations that require finesse rather than brute force.
4348: 
4349: Core Mechanics
4350: 
4351: Sneak Attack – Deal additional damage (starting at 1d6) when attacking targets that are flanked, when you're hidden, or when targets are suffering from Bleed, Poison, or Disable effects.
4352: 
4353: Poison System – Apply various poisons to your weapons as a bonus action, causing effects from damage to debuffs, with enhanced effects on critical hits.
4354: 
4355: Utility Skills – Excel at lockpicking, trap disarming, and stealth, making you the premier infiltration specialist.
4356: 
4357: Primary Attributes
4358: Main Attribute: Dexterity
4359: Secondary Attribute: Intelligence or Wisdom (player choice for traps/senses)
4360: Hit Points: Medium (8 + Constitution modifier at 1st level, 1d8 per level thereafter)
4361: Armor Proficiency: Light armor
4362: Weapon Proficiency: Daggers, short swords, thrown weapons, hand crossbows
4363: Tool Proficiency: Thieves' tools, poisoner's kit
4364: 
4365: Progression Path
4366: 
4367: Level 1-2 (Base Thief Skills)
4368: Foundational skills before Arch class selection.
4369: 
4370: Passive Skills
4371: Sneak Attack: Deal 1d6 extra damage when attacking from stealth, against flanked targets, or targets suffering from Bleed, Poison, or Disable.
4372: Lockpick Mastery: Gain +5 to open non-magical locks.
4373: Stealth Chain: Gain +2 to your next stealth check after landing a Sneak Attack.
4374: Toxin Sense: Identify resistances, immunities, or ideal poisons against enemies.
4375: Trap Intuition: Gain advantage on checks to spot hidden traps and mechanisms.
4376: 
4377: Active Skills
4378: Apply Poison: Use a bonus action to apply poison to your weapon, lasting for 2 attacks.
4379: Cunning Movement: Move through hostile-occupied tiles without provoking opportunity attacks.
4380: Disable Device: Disarm non-magical traps, pressure plates, or poison glyphs.
4381: Feint: Distract an opponent, giving you advantage on your next attack against them.
4382: Shadow Step: Move half your speed without making sound or leaving tracks.
4383: 
4384: Level 3-9 (Arch Class: Rake)
4385: At level 3, thieves specialize into the Rake arch class, enhancing their precision and ambush capabilities.
4386: 
4387: Passive Skills
4388: Combat Opener: Gain +5 to initiative rolls.
4389: Ambush Focus: Deal +2 damage against unaware or distracted targets.
4390: Evasion: Take half damage on successful saves against area effects (level 5).
4391: Trap Sense: Gain passive trap detection and disarm bonuses (level 6).
4392: Shadow Stab: On critical hit, teleport through target and apply Bleed (level 9).
4393: 
4394: Active Skills
4395: Precision Feint: Once per encounter, feint an enemy to impose -2 AC and remove their reactions until next round.
4396: Vanish: Become hidden even when observed, lasting until you attack or end your next turn.
4397: Analyze Weakness: Study a target to identify vulnerabilities and gain advantage on your next attack.
4398: Distraction Tactics: Create a diversion that allows you to disengage freely once per turn (level 13).
4399: Crippling Strike: Your Sneak Attack applies Slow effect to the target (level 15).
4400: 
4401: Level 10+ (Hero Path Selection)
4402: At level 10, thieves choose a Hero Path that reflects their philosophy and methods, further specializing their abilities.
4403: 
4404: Arcane Trickster (Hero Path)
4405: "Magic is just another angle of attack."
4406: 
4407: Passive Skills
4408: Mage Sense: Detect all magical effects and passives within 5 tiles.
4409: Arcane Resistance: Gain +2 to saves against magic.
4410: Illusory Stealth: Casting illusion or utility spells does not break stealth.
4411: Spell Disruptor: Your poisons also suppress passive magical abilities for 1 round on critical hits.
4412: 
4413: Active Skills
4414: Magebane Blade: Your Sneak Attack removes 1 magical buff or deals 1d6 psychic damage if no buff is present.
4415: Pocket Illusion: Create a clone that absorbs 1 hit, then explodes in a blinding flash.
4416: Flash Shift: Blink 3 tiles, avoid opportunity attacks, and gain +2 AC/Saves for 1 round.
4417: Arcane Theft: Steal a beneficial spell effect from a target and apply it to yourself.
4418: Spell Snare: Set a trap that triggers when a spell is cast within range, countering the spell.
4419: 
4420: Shadowblade (Hero Path)
4421: "From behind or below, it makes no difference, you'll never see it coming."
4422: 
4423: Passive Skills
4424: Ambusher's Grace: After killing a target or critically hitting one affected by Bleed or Poison, become untargetable until your next turn.
4425: Double Poison: Apply two poisons instead of one on critical hits.
4426: Persistent Bleed: Bleeds you cause refresh on hit instead of expiring.
4427: Shadow Affinity: Gain advantage on Stealth checks in dim light or darkness.
4428: 
4429: Active Skills
4430: Dark Step: Teleport behind any enemy within 5 tiles and gain advantage on your next attack.
4431: Shadow Dive: Become invisible for 1 round, gaining +1d6 Sneak Attack if used on the next turn.
4432: Vein Cutter: On critical hit, apply Bleed, Slow, and Weakened effects that can stack up to 3 times.
4433: Smoke Bomb: Create a cloud of smoke that obscures vision and grants you advantage on Stealth checks.
4434: Death Blossom: Strike all adjacent enemies, applying Sneak Attack damage to one of them (level 17).
4435: 
4436: Blackguard (Hero Path)
4437: "The kill doesn't thrill me. The suffering does."
4438: 
4439: Passive Skills
4440: Exsanguinate: Gain 1 HP per round for each bleeding target within 3 tiles.
4441: Prolonged Suffering: Bleeds you cause last twice as long.
4442: Venom Master: On critical hits, your poisons also apply -1 to all saves.
4443: Exotic Toxins: You can use monster-only venoms if harvested from bestiary bosses.
4444: 
4445: Active Skills
4446: Crippling Lash: Your Sneak Attack slows the target and reduces their damage by -1 for 2 rounds.
4447: Toxic Shiv: As a bonus action, apply any poison from your inventory with no check or delay.
4448: Vile Tactics: Deal +1d6 damage against Bleeding, Poisoned, or Frightened enemies.
4449: Terror Strike: Cause nearby enemies to become frightened, imposing disadvantage on their attacks.
4450: Unseen Hand: Once per round, take an action while invisible (attack or move) (level 20).
4451: 
4452: Poison & Disabling System
4453: 
4454: Poisons are a key component of the thief's arsenal, allowing for various debilitating effects beyond simple damage.
4455: 
4456: Poison Mechanics
4457: Poisons are applied manually to weapons or traps
4458: All poisons are single-use per application
4459: You may prepare up to 3 poisons at once (increases at levels 10, 15, and 20)
4460: Critical hits automatically trigger enhanced poison effects, often bypassing saves
4461: 
4462: Poison Tiers
4463: 
4464: Tier I – Basic Poisons (Levels 1–6)
4465: | Name | Effect on Hit | On Critical Hit |
4466: |------|--------------|----------------|
4467: | Bloodleech | Target takes 1d4 bleed (2 rounds) | You heal for 1d4 HP |
4468: | Crippler's Draught | Target has –1 to attack rolls next turn | Target is slowed by 50% |
4469: | Mindburn Sap | DC 12 WIS save or –1 to spellcasting | Target is confused for 1 round |
4470: | Venombite Oil | DC 12 CON save or Poisoned (–1 to rolls) | Target has disadvantage on next save |
4471: 
4472: Tier II – Advanced Poisons (Levels 7–13)
4473: | Name | Effect on Hit | On Critical Hit |
4474: |------|--------------|----------------|
4475: | Ashblood Toxin | 2d6 poison + 1 fire damage/round (2 rounds) | Target cannot benefit from healing |
4476: | Silencer's Sting | Target cannot speak or cast verbal spells (1 round) | Target is disarmed and silenced (1 round) |
4477: | Shiver Oil | Target becomes Shaken (–2 morale) | Target loses one reaction until next turn |
4478: | Hexroot Resin | All saves –1 for 2 rounds | Target's spell resistance is halved (1 round) |
4479: 
4480: Tier III – Expert Poisons (Levels 14–20)
4481: | Name | Effect on Hit | On Critical Hit |
4482: |------|--------------|----------------|
4483: | Voidspine Extract | Target takes 1d6 necrotic/round (3 rounds) | Target suffers –2 CON for 2 rounds (no save) |
4484: | Gravepetal Paste | 50% chance to fall unconscious (1 round) | Target falls prone and takes 3d6 poison damage |
4485: | Neuroshock Salve | DEX and WIS saves at disadvantage (1 round) | Target can't use reactions or opportunity attacks this turn |
4486: | Wraithbane Venom | Turns off passive abilities for 1 round | Target becomes Ethereal (and useless) for 1 round |
4487: 
4488: Disable Effect Tags
4489: Poison effects are grouped into disable types:
4490: 
4491: | Tag | Description |
4492: |-----|-------------|
4493: | Bleed | 1d4 damage/round. Stops healing and regeneration. |
4494: | Poisoned | –1 to all rolls, stackable per level |
4495: | Shaken | –2 morale, fear-based synergy |
4496: | Silenced | Cannot cast spells or speak |
4497: | Disoriented | Confused or action-locked for 1 round |
4498: | Crippled | Movement halved, Dodge reduced |
4499: | Suppressed | Cannot use reactions or class abilities |
4500: 
4501: Crafting & Inventory
4502: Poison Kit: Required for preparing toxins
4503: Crafting uses herbs, monster glands, or extracts from bestiary drops
4504: 10 common recipes can be learned via quests, guilds, or faction stores
4505: Exotic poisons can be extracted from rare monsters (with Survival + Poisoncraft)
4506: 
4507: Playing a Thief
4508: 
4509: As a thief in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on positioning, preparation, and precision. Unlike pure damage dealers or tanks, you excel at controlling key targets through debilitating effects and eliminating high-value threats before they can act.
4510: 
4511: Combat Strategy
4512: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path:
4513: 
4514: Arcane Trickster Thieves should focus on disrupting enemy spellcasters, removing magical buffs, and using illusions to control the battlefield while maintaining stealth.
4515: 
4516: Shadowblade Thieves excel at pure assassination, using their superior mobility and critical hit potential to eliminate high-value targets before vanishing back into the shadows.
4517: 
4518: Blackguard Thieves dominate through pain and fear, applying multiple debilitating effects to control enemies while sustaining themselves through the suffering they inflict.
4519: 
4520: All thieves should remember that preparation is key, having the right poisons ready for specific encounters can make the difference between success and failure.
4521: 
4522: Reputation and Social Consequences
4523: Your choice of Hero path significantly affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
4524: 
4525: Arcane Trickster Thieves often find work as specialized agents for magical academies or as counter-intelligence operatives against hostile spellcasters. Their focus on disrupting harmful magic rather than killing makes them more acceptable in civilized society.
4526: 
4527: Shadowblade Thieves typically operate as neutral mercenaries, treasure hunters, or guild assassins. Their reputation depends largely on their choice of targets and employers rather than their methods.
4528: 
4529: Blackguard Thieves are feared for their cruelty and the suffering they inflict. They often serve as torturers, interrogators, or enforcers for tyrannical regimes or criminal organizations, and are rarely welcome in lawful or good-aligned communities.
4530: 
4531: Notable Thief NPCs in Aethel
4532: 
4533: Lyssa Nightwhisper
4534: A halfling Arcane Trickster who specializes in countering magical threats. She works as a consultant for the Imperial Mage Guild, helping to secure magical artifacts and neutralize rogue spellcasters. Her signature pocket illusions have saved countless lives by drawing fire away from her allies.
4535: 
4536: Vex Shadowstep
4537: A human Shadowblade who leads the elite infiltration team of the Silver Coin mercenary company. His ability to appear and disappear seemingly at will has earned him the nickname "The Ghost." He's known for completing impossible extractions and retrievals, though he refuses contracts that target children or innocents.
4538: 
4539: The Smiling Knife
4540: A Umbrin Blackguard whose real name has been forgotten. Operating in the criminal underworld of Port Nexus, he specializes in information extraction through carefully administered poisons and psychological terror. His victims are said to survive for days under his ministrations, revealing everything they know before finally succumbing to his exotic toxins.
4541: 
4542: Tinker
4543: 
4544: "The gnome tinker's goggles reflected the firelight as she made final adjustments to her mechanical drone. 'Stand back,' she warned with a mischievous grin, tossing a small metal sphere toward the approaching bandits. The device bounced once before erupting in a spectacular explosion of frost that crystallized three attackers mid-stride. As the remaining bandits charged, her drone whirred to life, launching into the air and firing a barrage of bolts while she readied her crossbow and another explosive from her bandolier. 'I've got six more where that came from,' she called out cheerfully. 'Who wants to see what the purple one does?'"
4545: 
4546: Tinkers are brilliant and reckless battlefield engineers who overwhelm enemies with explosives, gadgets, deployable devices, and mechanical minions. In the world of Aethel, these innovative creators combine precision ranged fire from shortbows or crossbows with the unpredictable power of engineered chaos.
4547: 
4548: Class Overview
4549: 
4550: As a tinker, you excel at controlling the battlefield through a combination of explosive devices, mechanical companions, and deployable gadgets. Your specialty lies in creating zones of control, supporting allies with technological enhancements, and unleashing devastating area effects that can turn the tide of battle in an instant.
4551: 
4552: In Aethel, tinkers might serve as military engineers, independent inventors, guild specialists, or eccentric researchers. Their ability to create and deploy advanced technology makes them both respected and sometimes feared, particularly in regions where such innovations are rare or misunderstood.
4553: 
4554: Core Mechanics
4555: 
4556: Mechanical Pet (Anacite Drone) – Command a customizable mechanical companion that assists you in combat with ranged attacks and special abilities.
4557: 
4558: Explosive Toolkit – Create and deploy various bombs with different elemental effects and tactical applications.
4559: 
4560: Deployable Devices – Place turrets, traps, and other gadgets on the battlefield to control space and support your allies.
4561: 
4562: Primary Attributes
4563: Main Attribute: Intelligence
4564: Secondary Attribute: Dexterity
4565: Hit Points: Medium (8 + Constitution modifier at 1st level, 1d8 per level thereafter)
4566: Armor Proficiency: Light armor (with Tinker-specific upgrades at higher levels)
4567: Weapon Proficiency: Shortbows, crossbows, and simple weapons
4568: Tool Proficiency: Tinker's tools, alchemist's supplies
4569: 
4570: Progression Path
4571: 
4572: Level 1-2 (Base Tinker Skills)
4573: Foundational skills before Arch class selection.
4574: 
4575: Passive Skills
4576: Mechanical Aptitude: Gain advantage on checks to understand, repair, or disable mechanical devices.
4577: Explosive Handling: Throw bombs as a bonus action without risk of misfire.
4578: Quick Fingers: Perform device activation and manipulation actions with increased speed.
4579: Technical Knowledge: Identify the properties and functions of mechanical or alchemical items on sight.
4580: Improvised Engineering: Create temporary tools or simple devices from available materials.
4581: 
4582: Active Skills
4583: Deploy Anacite Drone: Summon a mechanical companion that attacks with ranged bolts (1d6 damage + 25% chance to shock).
4584: Tinker Bomb I: Create and throw Fire or Cold bombs that deal area effect damage.
4585: Field Repair: Quickly fix a damaged mechanical device or construct.
4586: Analyze Weakness: Study a target to identify vulnerabilities to specific damage types.
4587: Jury-rig: Temporarily modify a device or weapon to perform a different function.
4588: 
4589: Level 3-9 (Arch Class: Mechanist)
4590: At level 3, tinkers specialize into the Mechanist arch class, enhancing their gadgetry and explosive capabilities.
4591: 
4592: Passive Skills
4593: Enhanced Drone Control: Your Anacite Drone gains improved targeting and responsiveness.
4594: Bomb Specialization: Your explosive devices deal additional damage or have enhanced effects.
4595: Pet Upgrade Slot: Modify your drone with an Offensive, Defensive, or Support module (level 5).
4596: Deployable Expertise: Your deployed devices have increased durability and effectiveness.
4597: Remote Detonation: Detonate your devices remotely for area effect damage or stunning (level 9).
4598: 
4599: Active Skills
4600: Deploy Sentry Turret: Place an automated turret that fires at enemies (level 6).
4601: Deploy Net Trap: Set a trap that roots enemies who trigger it (level 6).
4602: Overcharge: Temporarily boost the effectiveness of one of your devices.
4603: Recalibrate: Adjust your drone's targeting parameters to focus on a specific enemy type.
4604: Emergency Protocols: Your drone automatically intercepts an attack meant for you once per encounter.
4605: 
4606: Level 10+ (Hero Path Selection)
4607: At level 10, tinkers choose a Hero Path that reflects their philosophy and methods, further specializing their abilities.
4608: 
4609: Artificer (Hero Path)
4610: "We build to protect. And sometimes, to explode gloriously."
4611: 
4612: Passive Skills
4613: Calibrated Precision: Allies within 3 tiles gain +1 to hit.
4614: Safe Detonation: Your bombs deal no damage to allies.
4615: Protective Programming: Your drone prioritizes defending vulnerable allies.
4616: Efficient Power Cells: Your devices last 50% longer before needing recharging.
4617: 
4618: Active Skills
4619: Nano-Aegis Drone: Replace your combat drone with a healing/shield drone that protects allies.
4620: Repair Pulse: Heal all drones, turrets, and party deployables for 50% HP and grant +1 AC to nearby allies.
4621: Magnetic Wall: Deploy a barrier that pulls ranged attacks into it and provides cover.
4622: Restoration Field: Create an area that gradually repairs mechanical devices and heals organic allies.
4623: Overload Suppression: Temporarily disable enemy mechanical devices or constructs.
4624: 
4625: Techsmith (Hero Path)
4626: "Why build one turret when I can build two? Or four?"
4627: 
4628: Passive Skills
4629: Automated Field Kit: Carry an extra bomb and reduce deployable cooldowns by 1 round.
4630: Enhanced Targeting: Your turrets gain +1 to hit.
4631: Efficient Recycling: Recover components from destroyed devices to create new ones.
4632: Technical Mastery: Your devices have a 10% chance to function twice when activated.
4633: 
4634: Active Skills
4635: Advanced Turret Bay: Deploy two turrets simultaneously in either Sniper or Burst mode.
4636: Gadget Sync Protocol: When you throw a bomb, one turret may fire instantly at the same target.
4637: Snap Rebuild: Once per encounter, instantly redeploy any destroyed turret or pet with 50% HP.
4638: Reconfiguration: Transform one deployed device into another type.
4639: Field Fabrication: Create a temporary gadget or tool to address a specific situation.
4640: 
4641: Warmachine (Hero Path)
4642: "It's not mad science if it works. It's just mad."
4643: 
4644: Passive Skills
4645: Malfunction Field: Enemies near your devices must roll twice on spell attacks and take the lower result.
4646: Volatile Components: Your devices explode when destroyed, damaging nearby enemies.
4647: Unstable Enhancements: Your bombs have a 25% chance to deal double damage but may affect allies.
4648: Scrap Salvage: Create temporary minions from destroyed devices.
4649: 
4650: Active Skills
4651: Boomcore Drone: Upgrade your drone to use blast bolts that deal damage and knockback.
4652: Overpressure Bombs: Your bombs gain knockback or prone effects with enhanced elemental damage.
4653: Scrap Reaper: When a turret or drone dies, summon a Scrapling that chases and explodes on enemies.
4654: Volatile Injection: Temporarily boost a device's power at the cost of stability.
4655: Catastrophic Overload: Sacrifice a device for a massive explosion that damages all nearby creatures.
4656: 
4657: Mechanical Pet System
4658: 
4659: Your Anacite Drone is a key component of your arsenal, providing consistent support in combat and serving as a platform for various upgrades.
4660: 
4661: Base Drone Properties:
4662: Attack: Ranged 1d6 damage + 25% chance to shock
4663: HP: 5 + your Intelligence modifier + your level
4664: AC: 13 + your Intelligence modifier
4665: Movement: 30 feet, flying
4666: Actions: Attacks once per round using your bonus action to command
4667: 
4668: At level 5, you gain a Pet Upgrade Slot that allows you to modify your drone with one of the following modules:
4669: 
4670: Offensive Modules
4671: Burn Injector: Drone attacks apply a burning effect (1d4 fire damage for 2 rounds)
4672: Poison Vents: Drone attacks have a 25% chance to poison targets (-1 to all rolls)
4673: Concussive Bolt: Drone attacks have a 25% chance to stun targets for 1 round
4674: 
4675: Defensive Modules
4676: Target Taunter: Drone can draw aggression from enemies once per encounter
4677: Energy Shield: Drone projects a small shield that grants +2 AC to one adjacent ally
4678: Smoke Cloud: Drone can deploy a smoke screen once per encounter, obscuring vision
4679: 
4680: Support Modules
4681: Ammo Dispenser: Allies within 2 tiles gain +1 damage with ranged weapons
4682: Target Scanner: Drone marks one enemy per round, granting allies +1 to hit against it
4683: Tether Grappler: Drone can fire a grappling hook to pull allies to safety once per encounter
4684: 
4685: At level 13, you gain Dual Drone Protocols, allowing you to either deploy two basic drones or enhance a single drone with an additional upgrade slot.
4686: 
4687: Explosive Toolkit
4688: 
4689: As a tinker, you have access to various bombs that can turn the tide of battle through area effects and status conditions.
4690: 
4691: | Bomb | Effect |
4692: |------|--------|
4693: | Thermobaric Flask | 3x3 AoE fire damage + knockback (STR save) |
4694: | Cryo Charge | Roots enemies hit; 50% chance to reduce enemy DR |
4695: | Shock Orb | Disrupts spellcasting or machine targets for 1 round |
4696: | Glue Canister | Ground AoE - slows enemies 75%, applies Grease condition |
4697: | Overload Core | Drone self-destruct: 2d6 damage + stun (DEX save) |
4698: 
4699: You begin with access to basic Fire and Cold bombs at level 1, and gain additional bomb types as you advance. The number of bombs you can use per encounter increases from 1 to 3 based on your level.
4700: 
4701: At level 15, you gain the Danger Close ability, which prevents your bombs from harming you or your allies.
4702: 
4703: Deployable Devices
4704: 
4705: Starting at level 6, you gain the ability to place various devices on the battlefield to control space and support your strategy.
4706: 
4707: | Deployable | Function |
4708: |------------|----------|
4709: | Sentry Turret | Auto-fires for 1d6 damage per round, range 5 tiles |
4710: | Net Trap | Roots 1 enemy who walks over it |
4711: | Rocket Tower | Launches mini-bomb once per round for 3 rounds |
4712: | Decoy Dummy | Draws aggro for 1 round and explodes when hit |
4713: | Blast Wall | Blocks line of sight and provides cover (2 tiles wide) |
4714: 
4715: You begin with one deployable slot and gain additional slots as you level up. At level 20, you gain Mech Overdrive, allowing one device to become permanent for the duration of a battle.
4716: 
4717: Playing a Tinker
4718: 
4719: As a tinker in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on preparation, positioning, and the strategic deployment of your various gadgets. Unlike pure spellcasters or martial classes, you excel at controlling the battlefield through a combination of area effects, persistent devices, and your mechanical companion.
4720: 
4721: Combat Strategy
4722: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path:
4723: 
4724: Artificer Tinkers should focus on supporting allies with shields and healing while using their devices to create safe zones and control enemy movement.
4725: 
4726: Techsmith Tinkers excel at deploying multiple devices in strategic locations, creating overlapping fields of fire and control effects that can lock down entire sections of the battlefield.
4727: 
4728: Warmachine Tinkers dominate through aggressive deployment of explosive devices and damaging effects, sacrificing precision for raw destructive power.
4729: 
4730: All tinkers should remember that positioning is crucial, your devices and bombs are most effective when placed to control chokepoints or to create zones that funnel enemies into disadvantageous positions.
4731: 
4732: Reputation and Social Consequences
4733: Your choice of Hero path significantly affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
4734: 
4735: Artificer Tinkers are generally welcomed in civilized areas, their protective innovations making them valuable allies to communities and military organizations alike. Many serve as guild masters or advisors to nobility.
4736: 
4737: Techsmith Tinkers often maintain workshops in urban centers, selling their inventions and services to those who can afford them. Their neutral stance on the application of their technology makes them acceptable in most regions.
4738: 
4739: Warmachine Tinkers are viewed with suspicion or outright hostility in peaceful regions, their destructive creations making them unwelcome except in times of war. Many find employment with mercenary companies or operate from remote laboratories.
4740: 
4741: Notable Tinker NPCs in Aethel
4742: 
4743: Fizwick Cogsprocket
4744: A gnome Artificer who serves as the Royal Engineer for the Imperial Court. His defensive innovations have saved the capital city from multiple siege attempts, and his shield drones are standard equipment for the palace guard. He's known for his elaborate safety protocols and his insistence that "proper engineering shouldn't hurt anyone, unless they really deserve it."
4745: 
4746: Mekka Geargrind
4747: A goblin Techsmith who operates a famous workshop in the trade city of Port Nexus. Her turret designs are sought after by merchants and ship captains for protection against pirates, and her deployable traps have revolutionized mining safety. She's constantly surrounded by a swarm of tiny helper drones that fetch tools and materials as she works.
4748: 
4749: The Iron Tinkerer
4750: A human Warmachine whose real name has been forgotten. Operating from a mobile workshop built into a massive steam-powered wagon, he sells his destructive inventions to the highest bidder. Military commanders seek his expertise before major battles, though few trust him enough to let him near their encampments afterward. His Boomcore Drones have been known to level small fortifications with a single coordinated strike.
4751: 
4752: Warrior
4753: 
4754: "The orc's muscles bulged unnaturally as the serum coursed through his veins, his skin hardening to a stone-like texture. With a primal roar that shook the very ground, he charged forward, his massive axe cleaving through the enemy ranks as if they were made of parchment. Those who survived the initial onslaught fled in terror as his form continued to mutate, growing more monstrous with each passing moment."
4755: 
4756: Warriors are physical combatants who enhance their natural abilities through controlled mutation and alchemical augmentation. In the world of Aethel, these battlefield titans serve as front-line fighters whose bodies become living weapons through the use of specialized serums that trigger temporary but powerful transformations.
4757: 
4758: Class Overview
4759: 
4760: As a warrior, you harness the power of controlled mutation to enhance your combat abilities, using specialized serums to trigger transformations that grant you superhuman strength, resilience, and destructive potential. Your specialized training allows you to manage these mutations, channeling their power while maintaining control over their more dangerous aspects. Beyond your combat abilities, you excel at intimidation, survival in harsh conditions, and pushing physical limits beyond what seems possible.
4761: 
4762: In Aethel, warriors might serve as elite shock troops, champions in gladiatorial arenas, mercenaries for dangerous expeditions, or guardians of settlements threatened by monsters or hostile forces. Their distinctive appearance, often bearing visible signs of their mutations even when not in combat, makes them instantly recognizable and often feared.
4763: 
4764: Core Mechanics
4765: 
4766: Mutation Slots – Warriors have access to mutation slots (initially 1 at Level 1, up to 4 at Level 20) that can be filled with different mutation types. These mutations can be swapped during rests, allowing for strategic customization based on anticipated challenges.
4767: 
4768: Serum Engine – Warriors use specialized serums to trigger and fuel their mutations. Managing serum consumption and timing activation for maximum effect is key to mastering the class.
4769: 
4770: Brutality Threshold – As warriors take and deal damage, they build toward a Brutality Threshold that, once crossed, enhances their mutations and unlocks more powerful abilities.
4771: 
4772: Primary Attributes
4773: Main Attribute: Strength or Dexterity (player builds for power or agility)
4774: Secondary Attributes: Constitution
4775: Hit Points: High (12 + Constitution modifier at 1st level, 1d12 per level thereafter)
4776: Armor Proficiency: Medium and heavy armor
4777: Weapon Proficiency: All martial weapons; bonuses when wielding mutation-tagged gear
4778: Tool Proficiency: None
4779: 
4780: Progression Path
4781: 
4782: Level 1-2 (Base Warrior Skills)
4783: Foundational skills before Arch class selection.
4784: 
4785: Class Progression
4786: | Level | Feature |
4787: |-------|---------|
4788: | 1 | Mutation Slot I (choose 1), Brutality Threshold (BT) tracker |
4789: | 2 | Mutagen Injection (1/rest: +2 STR or +2 AC for 3 rounds) |
4790: | 3 | Promote to Arch Class: Juggernaut |
4791: | 5 | Mutation Slot II unlocked |
4792: | 6 | Bone Hardened: DR +1 while not staggered |
4793: | 9 | Serum Overclock: Inject 2 serums at once |
4794: | 10 | Unlock Hero Path |
4795: | 13 | Mutation Slot III unlocked |
4796: | 15 | Brutal Adaptation – Gain passive bonus from a mutation |
4797: | 17 | Reflexive Mutation – Once per round, mutate reactively |
4798: | 20 | Apex Form – Gain a permanent 4th mutation & unleash once/encounter ultimate form |
4799: 
4800: Passive Skills
4801: Mutation Adaptation: Your body has been conditioned to accept mutations with reduced side effects.
4802: Serum Tolerance: You can consume serums without the normal toxicity effects that would affect others.
4803: Combat Instinct: +5% initiative and +10% resistance to being surprised.
4804: Physical Conditioning: +10% carrying capacity and advantage on physical endurance checks.
4805: Brutality Sense: You can sense when an opponent is below 25% health, marking them as vulnerable.
4806: 
4807: Active Skills
4808: Serum Injection: Activate a prepared mutation serum as a bonus action, triggering its effects for 3 rounds.
4809: Brutal Strike: A powerful attack that deals additional damage based on your Strength and has a chance to stagger the target.
4810: Mutation Surge: Temporarily enhance an active mutation, increasing its effects for 1 round but adding strain to your body.
4811: Intimidating Presence: Use your mutated appearance to frighten enemies, potentially causing them to flee or surrender.
4812: Survival Instinct: Enter a heightened state of awareness, gaining bonuses to perception and reaction time for a short duration.
4813: 
4814: Level 3-9 (Arch Class: Juggernaut)
4815: At level 3, warriors specialize into the Juggernaut arch class, refining their mutation control and developing more powerful transformation techniques.
4816: 
4817: Passive Skills
4818: Mutation Efficiency: May have 2 active mutations at once (Lv 3)
4819: Brutal Fortitude: Gain +1 DR for each BT stack (max +3)
4820: Overpressure Response: Once per round, when struck by a critical hit, gain 1 free mutation activation
4821: Crushing Advance: Once per long rest, move in a straight line up to 20 ft, knocking aside all enemies (STR save or fall prone, take 2d6)
4822: Pain Conversion: Convert 10% of damage taken into temporary Strength bonus.
4823: Adaptive Tissue: Gain resistance to the last damage type that critically hit you until your next rest.
4824: Brutality Momentum: Killing a marked vulnerable target instantly readies your next Brutal Strike.
4825: 
4826: Active Skills
4827: Mutation Overload: Push your mutations beyond safe limits, gaining significantly enhanced effects for 2 rounds followed by exhaustion.
4828: Serum Cocktail: Combine two different serums for a hybrid effect, though with increased strain on your body.
4829: Brutality Unleashed: Once your Brutality Threshold is crossed, unleash a devastating area attack that damages all enemies within range.
4830: Survival Adaptation: Rapidly adapt your body to environmental hazards, gaining temporary resistance to environmental effects.
4831: Intimidating Transformation: Undergo a particularly terrifying mutation that can cause enemies to become frightened or even paralyzed with fear.
4832: 
4833: Level 10+ (Hero Path Selection)
4834: At level 10, warriors choose a Hero Path that reflects their philosophy and methods, further specializing their mutation abilities.
4835: 
4836: Wardborn (Hero Path)
4837: The self-mutated protector ,  armored in flesh and tenacity.
4838: 
4839: "I bled first. You don't get to bleed last."
4840: 
4841: Role Focus
4842: Frontline protector
4843: Uses mutations to defend others
4844: High HP and DR scaling
4845: 
4846: Passive Skills
4847: Guardian Tissue: When an adjacent ally would take damage, you can absorb 30% of it as a reaction.
4848: Protective Mutations: Your mutations generate a 5-foot aura that grants allies +10% damage resistance.
4849: Serum Sharing: You can safely inject allies with diluted versions of your serums, granting them 50% of the benefits.
4850: Controlled Transformation: Your mutations appear less monstrous and can be partially concealed when necessary.
4851: Final Form – Aegis Incarnate: Transform into a living shield for 3 rounds, becoming immovable and redirecting 50% of all damage dealt to allies within 15 feet to yourself.
4852: 
4853: Active Skills
4854: Bulwark Mutation Tree: Unlocks mutations like:
4855: Spinal Plating (+2 DR vs AoE)
4856: Adrenal Overwatch (gain reaction attack when ally hit)
4857: Living Cover (adjacent allies gain +2 AC)
4858: Mutated Guard: If you end your turn within 2 tiles of an ally, grant them +1 Save and DR
4859: Guardian Pulse: 1/rest, redirect an attack from an adjacent ally to yourself and immediately counterstrike
4860: Warding Mutation: Develop protective growths that can absorb damage directed at allies.
4861: Stabilizing Injection: Use your serum knowledge to create a stabilizing agent that can heal and remove negative status effects.
4862: 
4863: Ironskin (Hero Path)
4864: The evolution of pure brutality ,  a mutation-hardened warbeast.
4865: 
4866: "I'm not tougher than you. I'm just built to survive what you can't."
4867: 
4868: Role Focus
4869: Solo tank/brawler
4870: Maximizes passive mutation stacking
4871: Excels at breaking formations and terrain
4872: 
4873: Passive Skills
4874: Environmental Adaptation: Your body automatically begins adapting to extreme environments after 1 minute of exposure.
4875: Regenerative Mutation: Heal 5% of your maximum health each round while at least one mutation is active.
4876: Serum Recycling: 20% chance to not consume a serum when activating a mutation.
4877: Damage Memory: After taking a specific damage type, gain increasing resistance to it with each subsequent hit.
4878: Final Form – Adaptive Perfection: For 3 rounds, gain immunity to the damage types and environmental effects you've been exposed to during the current day.
4879: 
4880: Active Skills
4881: Gore Mutation Tree: Unlocks mutations like:
4882: Gore Maw (bite attack, 1d8 + bleed)
4883: Reinforced Joints (immune to prone)
4884: Shard Skin (1d4 damage to melee attackers)
4885: Stack Overflow: Can hold up to 4 BT stacks instead of 3
4886: Auto-Rupture: If you reach max BT, release a shockwave: all adjacent enemies must STR save or fall prone
4887: Adaptive Mutation: Rapidly evolve your body to counter a specific threat or environmental hazard.
4888: Cellular Reconstruction: Enter a brief stasis where your body rapidly heals and purges toxins or diseases.
4889: 
4890: Dreadspawn (Hero Path)
4891: A cursed evolution ,  a warrior who mutates into something far beyond mortal flesh.
4892: 
4893: "There is no coming back from this. That's the point."
4894: 
4895: Role Focus
4896: Mutation corruption
4897: Applies fear, bleed, and chaos
4898: The most dangerous and unstable path
4899: 
4900: Passive Skills
4901: Horrific Appearance: Enemies who see you activate a mutation must make a fear save or be frightened for 1 round.
4902: Infectious Mutation: Your attacks have a 10% chance to cause minor, painful mutations in your targets.
4903: Serum Toxicity: Your serums become poisonous to others; your blood deals acid damage to those who wound you.
4904: Pain Empowerment: Gain +5% damage for each 10% of health you are missing.
4905: Final Form – Abomination Ascendant: Transform into a nightmarish creature for 3 rounds, gaining terrifying new attacks and abilities while causing fear in a 30-foot radius.
4906: 
4907: Active Skills
4908: Aberrant Mutation Tree: Unlocks:
4909: Whip-Tendon Limbs (reach + pull effect)
4910: Soul Spike Carapace (crit = +1 BT and fear within 2 tiles)
4911: Hemophage Core (heal on kill; absorb 2d6 HP)
4912: Terror Radius: Enemies within 3 tiles at start of turn must save or suffer –1 morale and –1 AC
4913: Apex Aberration (1/day): You transform for 2 rounds:
4914: Gain +4 STR
4915: Gain immunity to spells of level 3 or lower
4916: All attacks cause fear on hit
4917: Horrific Mutation: Undergo a particularly disturbing transformation designed to terrify and demoralize enemies.
4918: Toxic Secretion: Develop glands that secrete powerful toxins, allowing you to poison enemies with your attacks.

5048: 
5049: Warriors use specialized serums to trigger and maintain their mutations. These serums come in several varieties:
5050: 
5051: | Serum Type | Primary Effect | Secondary Effect | Duration | Side Effect |
5052: |------------|----------------|------------------|----------|-------------|
5053: | Overdrive | +2 STR/DEX | +10% Speed | 3 rounds | -10% Defense |
5054: | Stonecore | +2 AC | Immunity to knockback | 3 rounds | -15% Speed |

5058: | Berserker Brew | +40% Damage | Fear Immunity | 3 rounds | Temporary Madness |
5059: 
5060: Brutality Threshold
5061: 
5062: As warriors take and deal damage, they build toward a Brutality Threshold that enhances their capabilities:
5063: 
5064: | Threshold Level | Requirement | Benefit |
5065: |-----------------|-------------|---------|
5066: | Awakened | Deal 25% of max HP as damage | Mutations cost 10% less to activate |
5067: | Primed | Deal 50% of max HP as damage | Active mutations gain +25% effectiveness |
5068: | Unleashed | Deal 75% of max HP as damage | Unlock Brutality Unleashed ability |
5069: | Transcendent | Deal 100% of max HP as damage | All mutations active simultaneously for 1 round |
5070: 
5071: Playing a Warrior
5072: 
5073: As a warrior in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on mutation management, serum timing, and building toward your Brutality Threshold. Unlike other classes that might focus on weapons or spells, you excel at turning your own body into a living weapon through controlled transformation.
5074: 
5075: Combat Strategy
5076: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path:
5077: 
5078: Wardborn Warriors should focus on positioning to protect vulnerable allies, using their mutations to absorb damage and create safe zones within the battlefield.
5079: 
5080: Ironskin Warriors excel at adaptation and survival, choosing mutations that counter specific threats and using their regenerative capabilities to outlast enemies in prolonged engagements.
5081: 
5082: Dreadspawn Warriors thrive on intimidation and psychological warfare, using their horrific transformations to demoralize enemies before tearing them apart with monstrous attacks.
5083: 
5084: All warriors should remember that serum management is crucial, timing your injections for maximum effect and choosing the right mutations for each situation can dramatically increase your effectiveness in combat.
5085: 
5086: Reputation and Social Consequences
5087: Your choice of Hero path significantly affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
5088: 
5089: Wardborn Warriors are often respected, if somewhat feared, for their willingness to use their mutations to protect others rather than for personal gain or destruction.
5090: 
5091: Ironskin Warriors typically maintain neutral reputations, their focus on survival and adaptation making them valuable allies in dangerous situations but sometimes unpredictable.
5092: 
5093: Dreadspawn Warriors are feared and often reviled, their embrace of monstrous, terrifying mutations making them unwelcome in most civilized areas except as mercenaries for particularly dangerous tasks.
5094: 
5095: Notable Warrior NPCs in Aethel
5096: 
5097: Commander Thorne Ironhide
5098: A human Wardborn warrior who leads the defense of the northern frontier against monstrous incursions. His ability to develop stone-like skin and protective bone structures has saved countless soldiers under his command, earning him the nickname "The Living Wall."
5099: 
5100: Vex Adaptus
5101: A Drakari Ironskin warrior who works as a guide through the most dangerous and inhospitable regions of Aethel. His body can adapt to virtually any environment, from the frozen wastes to volcanic calderas, making him invaluable to explorers and researchers venturing into uncharted territories.
5102: 
5103: The Nightmare of Blackmire
5104: An orc Dreadspawn warrior whose true name has been forgotten, replaced by the terrifying moniker given by those unfortunate enough to encounter him in the swamps of Blackmire. His horrific mutations and predatory hunting style have made him a legend used to frighten children, though the reality of his existence is far more terrifying than any story.
5105: 
5106: Wizard
5107: 
5108: "The human's eyes glowed with an ethereal blue light as he traced complex sigils in the air. The air around him crackled with arcane energy as he consulted his spellbook, finding the exact formula needed. With a final gesture and a word of power, he completed the spell matrix, and reality itself bent to his will as a shimmering portal opened before the astonished onlookers."
5109: 
5110: Wizards are masters of arcane theory and spell matrices, methodically studying the fundamental laws of magic to bend reality to their will. In the world of Aethel, these scholarly practitioners represent the pinnacle of magical research and innovation, approaching spellcasting as a science to be mastered through rigorous study, experimentation, and precise formula manipulation.
5111: 
5112: Class Overview
5113: 
5114: As a wizard, you harness arcane power through meticulous study and the precise application of magical theory. Unlike the raw elemental destruction of mages or the intuitive spellcasting of bards, your magic comes from understanding the underlying principles of reality and manipulating them through carefully constructed spell matrices. Your spellbook is your most prized possession, containing the accumulated knowledge that forms the foundation of your power.
5115: 
5116: In Aethel, wizards might serve as royal advisors, heads of arcane academies, researchers of ancient magical phenomena, or solitary scholars pursuing theoretical breakthroughs. Their methodical approach to magic makes them both respected and sometimes feared, as their power comes not from innate gifts or divine favor, but from the relentless pursuit of knowledge that can reshape reality itself.
5117: 
5118: Core Mechanics
5119: 
5120: Spell Matrix System – Wizards prepare spells by constructing complex arcane matrices in their spellbooks. These matrices can be modified and optimized through study, allowing for customization of spell effects. Managing your prepared matrices and adapting them to different situations is key to maximizing a wizard's versatility.
5121: 
5122: Primary Attributes
5123: Main Attribute: Intelligence
5124: Secondary Attributes: Constitution (for resilience under pressure)
5125: Hit Points: Low (6 + Constitution modifier at 1st level, 1d6 per level thereafter)
5126: Armor Proficiency: None (Arcane Armor feat unlocks Light armor casting)
5127: Weapon Proficiency: Arcane Staff, Wand, Spellblade
5128: Tool Proficiency: Arcana tools, scribe's supplies
5129: 
5130: Spellcasting – Spell Matrix System
5131: 
5132: Prepared Matrix
5133: You prepare spells each day from your spellbook
5134: You cast only from your prepared matrix
5135: You can prepare duplicates, and swap 1 spell mid-day (10-minute ritual)
5136: 
5137: Daily Preparation
5138: INT modifier + Wizard Level spells
5139:

5148: | 11 | Tier 6 | |
5149: | 13 | Tier 7 | |
