5153: 
5154: Spellcasting Traits
5155: Casting Resource: Resonance Points (RP); spell tier determines RP cost.
5156: Focus: Not Used
5157: Requirements: Must use a free hand or arcane focus (staff, wand, ring)
5158: Arcane Armor (feat): Allows casting in Light armor
5159: 
5160: Arcane School Specialization (Choose at Level 1)
5161: Gain +1 DC and +1 to hit for your chosen school
5162: Learn one exclusive bonus spell from that school
5163: Gain a passive benefit (see below)
5164: 
5165: | School | Passive Benefit |
5166: |--------|----------------|
5167: | Necromancy | Heal 1 HP on spell kill |
5168: | Evocation | Exclude 1 target from friendly AoE |
5169: | Divination | Reroll 1 failed save per long rest |
5170: | Illusion | +2 to maintain concentration |
5171: | Enchantment | +1 to control duration on failed save |
5172: | Conjuration | Summons gain +1 HP per Wizard level |
5173: | Transmutation | Gain +5 movement when casting spells |
5174: 
5175: Progression Path
5176: 
5177: Level 1-2 (Base Wizard Skills)
5178: Foundational skills before Arch class selection.
5179: 
5180: Class Progression
5181: | Level | Feature |
5182: |-------|---------|
5183: | 1 | Arcane School, Spell Matrix, Spellbook (6 spells) |
5184: | 2 | Counterspell Reaction, Ritual Casting |
5185: | 3 | Promote to Arch Class: Arcanist |
5186: | 5 | Bonus Feat: School Mastery |
5187: | 6 | Arcane Ward (resistance to chosen school's element) |
5188: | 9 | Dual Cast (cast 2 Tier 1–2 spells in a round) |
5189: | 10 | Unlock Hero Path |
5190: | 13 | Overchannel (cast a spell 1 tier higher once/day) |
5191: | 15 | Arcane Feedback (after successfully countering a spell, recover 1 RP once per round) |
5192: | 17 | Tethered Cast (bonus action spell link 1/round) |
5193: | 20 | Grand Reservoir (1/day spell from any school, any tier) |
5194: 
5195: Passive Skills
5196: Arcane Recovery: Once per day during a short rest, recover RP equal to half your Wizard level (rounded up), up to your normal maximum.
5197: Spell Scribing: Copy spells into your spellbook at half the normal cost and time.
5198: Arcane Insight: Identify magical effects and items with greater accuracy than non-wizards.
5199: Matrix Efficiency: Reduce the preparation time for spells by 25%.
5200: Scholarly Mind: Gain advantage on Intelligence checks related to arcane knowledge.
5201: 
5202: Active Skills
5203: Arcane Bolt: Launch a bolt of pure arcane energy that deals force damage to a single target.
5204: Minor Conjuration: Create a temporary, small inanimate object that lasts for 1 hour.
5205: Arcane Shield: Create a barrier of magical force that provides temporary protection.
5206: Analyze Magic: Study a magical effect or item to gain insight into its properties and function.
5207: Spellbook Study: Spend time studying your spellbook to prepare a different selection of spells.
5208: 
5209: Level 3-9 (Arch Class: Arcanist)
5210: At level 3, wizards specialize into the Arcanist arch class, refining their understanding of spell matrices and developing more powerful magical techniques.
5211: 
5212: Passive Skills
5213: Floating Preparation: Prepare 1 extra spell from any school each day
5214: Sigil Rewrite: Once/day, instantly swap a spell you know for another of the same tier
5215: Spell Tether: Maintain 2 self-buff spells without concentration (1/rest)
5216: Matrix Mastery: Modify spell matrices to change minor aspects of prepared spells.
5217: Arcane Tradition: Choose a school of magic to specialize in, gaining bonuses to spells of that school.
5218: Spell Efficiency: Reduce the Resonance Point cost of frequently cast spells by 10%.
5219: Improved Concentration: Advantage on concentration checks when taking damage.
5220: 
5221: Active Skills
5222: Counterspell: Disrupt an enemy's spellcasting, potentially negating their spell entirely.
5223: Arcane Lock: Create a magical barrier that prevents entry or access to an object or area.
5224: Dispel Magic: End magical effects in an area or on a target.
5225: Dimensional Pocket: Create a small extradimensional space to store items.
5226: Arcane Sight: See magical auras and effects for a short duration.
5227: 
5228: Level 10+ (Hero Path Selection)
5229: At level 10, wizards choose a Hero Path that reflects their philosophy and methods, further specializing their arcane abilities.
5230: 
5231: Spellbinder (Hero Path)
5232: Master of arcane lockdown and anti-magic warfare.
5233: 
5234: "Their silence is louder than my spells."
5235: 
5236: Role Focus
5237: Spell disruption
5238: Magical suppression
5239: Arcane dueling
5240: 
5241: Passive Skills
5242: Sigil Authority: +1 to all counterspell rolls, +2 DC vs targets currently affected by crowd control
5243: Spell Preservation: Your spellbook cannot be destroyed by non-artifact means.
5244: Knowledge Shield: When you cast a protective spell, allies gain +2 to saving throws.
5245: Matrix Sharing: Grant an ally temporary access to one of your prepared spells.
5246: Arcane Memory: Recall and cast any spell you've ever prepared once per long rest.
5247: 
5248: Active Skills
5249: Seal Magic: Prevent a target from casting spells of a specific school (WIS save, 3 rounds)
5250: Runic Snare: Glyph trap explodes in a 2x2 root + disarm on trigger
5251: Counterpulse: Countering a spell silences the caster for 1 round
5252: Spell Absorption: Absorb a hostile spell, gaining temporary hit points equal to the spell's level.
5253: Knowledge Transfer: Temporarily grant an ally knowledge of a skill or language you possess.
5254: 
5255: Final Theorem
5256: Arcane Sanctuary: Create a demiplane of safety for 1 hour where harmful magic cannot function (1/week).
5257: 
5258: Elementalist (Hero Path)
5259: Embodiment of elemental devastation and chain reactions.
5260: 
5261: "Fire doesn't think. Lightning doesn't pause."
5262: 
5263: Role Focus
5264: Raw elemental damage
5265: AoE and environment control
5266: Elemental synergy combos
5267: 
5268: Passive Skills
5269: Core Sync: Spells of your chosen element cost 1 fewer RP at Tier 1–2 (minimum 1 RP); you may change your elemental affinity once per long rest
5270: Matrix Innovation: Combine aspects of two different spells into one casting.
5271: Spell Adaptation: Change a spell's damage type or effect type once per short rest.
5272: Efficient Casting: 20% chance to refund the RP spent on a spell immediately after it resolves.
5273: Arcane Experimentation: Gain advantage on checks to create new spells or magical items.
5274: 
5275: Active Skills
5276: Elemental Affinity: Choose 1 element (Fire, Ice, Lightning, Earth); all spells of that element gain +2 DC, +1 damage tier
5277: Combo Surge: Casting two different elements in sequence triggers a combo effect
5278: Fire + Ice = Burn + Root
5279: Lightning + Earth = Paralyze + Knockdown
5280: Cycling Ward: Rotate resistance to your cast element (25% reduction)
5281: Spell Modification: Alter a prepared spell on the fly, changing its range, duration, or area of effect.
5282: Arcane Fusion: Combine two spells into a single, more powerful effect.
5283: 
5284: Final Theorem
5285: Reality Edit: Temporarily change one aspect of reality in a small area (1/week).
5286: 
5287: Necromancer (Hero Path)
5288: Summoner of the dead and pactbound with infernal patrons.
5289: 
5290: "Their corpses are mine. Their souls, negotiable."
5291: 
5292: Role Focus
5293: Undead summoning
5294: Battlefield saturation
5295: Pact magic with infernal forces
5296: 
5297: Passive Skills
5298: Flesh Ascendant: All undead gain +2 HP per Wizard level, if 2+ undead are active, gain +1 AC and Save
5299: Mind Matrix: Your enchantment spells are harder to resist (+2 to save DC).
5300: Will Domination: Creatures that fail saves against your spells by 5+ are stunned for 1 round.
5301: Arcane Leech: Gain temporary hit points when a creature fails a save against your spells.
5302: Binding Sigil: Mark a creature, giving you advantage on spell attacks against it.
5303: 
5304: Active Skills
5305: Corpse Harvest: Raise skeletons/goblins/zombies from slain humanoids
5306: Ghoul Lash: Minions within 1 tile of each other gain bonus attacks
5307: Infernal Pact (Quest-based unlock): Grants Gravebrand: 1d6 necrotic per round, heals caster, overrides corpse resistance
5308: Mental Binding: Force a creature to obey a simple command for a short duration.
5309: Arcane Domination: Take control of a creature's actions for 1 minute.
5310: 
5311: Auto-Raise Mechanic
5312: When a minion slays a humanoid, automatically raise that corpse into the same minion type (up to cap)
5313: Works only on humanoid races unless corrupted by pact
5314: 
5315: Final Theorem
5316: Mind Prison: Trap a creature's mind in an illusory reality while controlling its body (1/week).
5317: 
5318: Undead Pet System
5319: Necromancers can maintain up to 3 elite undead companions:
5320: 
5321: | Pet Name | Role | Abilities |
5322: |----------|------|-----------|
5323: | Boneflayer | Ranged caster | Casts Bone Shards, Fear Pulse, AoE DoT |
5324: | Dreadblade | Melee disruptor | Crits apply Weakened, interrupts casting |
5325: | Wraithbound | Ethereal assassin | Invisible unless attacking, 20% stun chance |
5326: 
5327: Spell Matrix System
5328: 
5329: The Wizard's Spell Matrix System is a unique approach to spellcasting that emphasizes preparation, modification, and optimization of magical formulas.
5330: 
5331: Matrix Components
5332: Each spell matrix consists of several components:
5333: Core Formula: The basic spell effect
5334: Power Nodes: Determine spell level and potency
5335: Stability Anchors: Affect duration and concentration requirements
5336: Range Modifiers: Control distance and area of effect
5337: Elemental Attunement: Optional component that can add elemental properties
5338: 
5339: Matrix Preparation
5340: Wizards prepare spells by constructing matrices in their spellbooks during a long rest. The number of matrices you can maintain simultaneously is determined by your Intelligence modifier and wizard level.
5341: 
5342: Matrix Modification
5343: As you advance, you gain the ability to modify matrices in various ways:
5344: Quick Substitution: Swap one component for another without rebuilding the entire matrix
5345: Component Enhancement: Strengthen specific components at the cost of additional resources
5346: Matrix Linking: Connect multiple matrices for combination effects
5347: Stability Reinforcement: Make matrices more resistant to disruption
5348: 
5349: Spellbook Importance
5350: Your spellbook is the physical repository of your spell matrices. Unlike other spellcasters, losing your spellbook is a significant setback, though not insurmountable. You can recreate matrices from memory, but the process is time-consuming and potentially imperfect.
5351: 
5352: Playing a Wizard
5353: 
5354: As a wizard in Decennium Descent, your effectiveness depends on preparation, adaptability, and strategic spell selection. Unlike more spontaneous spellcasters, you excel at having the right magical solution for a wide variety of situations, provided you've had time to prepare.
5355: 
5356: Combat Strategy
5357: Your approach to combat will vary based on your chosen Hero path:
5358: 
5359: Spellbinder Wizards should focus on controlling the battlefield through magical suppression, using their counterspell abilities to shut down enemy casters and their protective spells to safeguard allies.
5360: 
5361: Elementalist Wizards excel at adapting to changing situations, modifying their elemental focus to exploit enemy weaknesses and combining elements for devastating combo effects.
5362: 
5363: Necromancer Wizards dominate the battlefield through undead minions and dark pacts, focusing on battlefield control through summoned creatures and debilitating effects.
5364: 
5365: All wizards should remember that positioning is crucial, your low health and armor make you vulnerable to direct attacks, so maintaining distance from enemies while still having line of sight to your targets is essential.
5366: 
5367: Reputation and Social Consequences
5368: Your choice of Hero path affects how you're perceived in the world of Aethel:
5369: 
5370: Spellbinder Wizards are generally respected in civilized areas, their dedication to magical control and suppression making them valuable allies against rogue spellcasters and magical threats.
5371: 
5372: Elementalist Wizards often face a mixture of respect and wariness, their mastery of elemental forces viewed as both impressive and potentially dangerous.
5373: 
5374: Necromancer Wizards are feared in most regions, their manipulation of the dead and pacts with infernal entities considered taboo. They often operate from the shadows or in regions where such practices are tolerated.
5375: 
5376: Notable Wizard NPCs in Aethel
5377: 
5378: Archmage Thorne Vellum
5379: A human Spellbinder who serves as the head of the Grand Library of Aethel. His collection of preserved spellbooks spans centuries, and he has dedicated his life to ensuring that magical knowledge is not lost to time or catastrophe. His protective matrices are said to be impenetrable, and many seek his guidance in matters of ancient magic.
5380: 
5381: Magister Elara Spellweaver
5382: An elf Elementalist who has pioneered numerous innovations in elemental combination magic. Her experimental approach has resulted in both spectacular breakthroughs and occasional disasters, but her contributions to magical theory are undeniable. She travels between arcane academies, sharing her discoveries and challenging traditional approaches to spellcasting.
5383: 
5384: Lord Malachai the Deathbinder
5385: A Umbrin Necromancer who operates from a hidden tower in the borderlands. Those who seek his aid in matters of life and death pay dearly for his services, often with fragments of their own vitality or soul. Rumors suggest he maintains a collection of elite undead servants whose former identities are known only to him, and that he has made pacts with entities beyond mortal comprehension.
5386: 


Complete Chapter 6 multiclass prestige and feat context
7154: Chapter 6: Making Your Character Your Own
7155: In the world of Aethel, heroes rarely follow a single, predetermined path. As you adventure across the realm, you'll discover new abilities, forge unexpected alliances, and perhaps even unlock legendary powers that transcend conventional training. This chapter explores how to customize and evolve your character beyond the basic framework of a single class.
7156: Multiclassing
7157: Multiclassing allows you to gain levels in multiple classes, representing the diversification of your character's talents and interests. Perhaps your Warrior has discovered a talent for arcane magic, or your Thief has begun to hear the call of nature. Multiclassing lets you combine abilities from different classes to create a character that is uniquely yours.
7158: Prerequisites
7159: Not every character is suited for every class. To qualify for a new class, you must meet the ability score prerequisites for both your current class and the new one, as shown in the Multiclassing Prerequisites table.
7160: Multiclassing Prerequisites
7161: Class
7162: Minimum Ability Scores
7163: Bard
7164: Charisma 13
7165: Cleric
7166: Wisdom 13
7167: Crimson Agent
7168: Dexterity 13, Constitution 13
7169: Dragoon
7170: Strength 13, Dexterity 13
7171: Druid
7172: Wisdom 13
7173: Mage
7174: Intelligence 13
7175: Mirrorsage
7176: Intelligence 13, Charisma 13
7177: Monk
7178: Dexterity 13, Wisdom 13
7179: Oracle
7180: Wisdom 13, Charisma 13
7181: Paladin
7182: Strength 13, Charisma 13
7183: Ranger
7184: Dexterity 13, Wisdom 13
7185: Shaman
7186: Wisdom 13, Constitution 13
7187: Soldier
7188: Strength 13, Constitution 13
7189: Thief
7190: Dexterity 13
7191: Tinker
7192: Intelligence 13, Dexterity 13
7193: Warrior
7194: Strength 13, Constitution 13
7195: Wizard
7196: Intelligence 13
7197: Experience and Levels
7198: The experience point cost to gain a level is always based on your total character level, as shown in the Character Advancement table in Chapter 1, not your level in a particular class. When you gain a level, you may choose whether to increase your level in your current class or to gain a level in a new class.
7199: Hit Points and Hit Dice
7200: You gain the hit points from your new class as described for levels after 1st. You gain the 1st-level hit points for a class only when you are a 1st-level character.
7201: You add together the Hit Dice granted by all your classes to form your pool of Hit Dice. If the Hit Dice are the same die type, you can simply pool them together. If they're different, track them separately.
7202: Proficiency Bonus
7203: Your proficiency bonus is always based on your total character level, as shown in the Character Advancement table, not your level in a particular class.
7204: Proficiencies
7205: When you gain a level in a class other than your first, you gain only some of that class's starting proficiencies, as shown in the Multiclassing Proficiencies table.
7206: Multiclassing Proficiencies
7207: Class
7208: Proficiencies Gained
7209: Bard
7210: Light armor, one skill of your choice, one musical instrument
7211: Cleric
7212: Light armor, medium armor, shields
7213: Crimson Agent
7214: Light armor, one skill from the class's skill list
7215: Dragoon
7216: Medium armor, shields, martial weapons
7217: Druid
7218: Light armor, medium armor, shields (druids will not wear armor or use shields made of metal)
7219: Mage
7220: One skill from the class's skill list
7221: Mirrorsage
7222: One skill from the class's skill list
7223: Monk
7224: Simple weapons, shortswords
7225: Oracle
7226: Light armor, one skill from the class's skill list
7227: Paladin
7228: Light armor, medium armor, shields, simple weapons, martial weapons
7229: Ranger
7230: Light armor, medium armor, shields, simple weapons, martial weapons, one skill from the class's skill list
7231: Shaman
7232: Light armor, shields, one skill from the class's skill list
7233: Soldier
7234: Light armor, medium armor, heavy armor, shields, simple weapons, martial weapons
7235: Thief
7236: Light armor, one skill from the class's skill list, thieves' tools
7237: Tinker
7238: Light armor, one skill from the class's skill list, tinker's tools
7239: Warrior
7240: Light armor, medium armor, shields, simple weapons, martial weapons
7241: Wizard
7242: One skill from the class's skill list
7243: Class Features
7244: When you gain a new level in a class, you get its features for that level. A few features, however, have additional rules when you're multiclassing.
7245: Channel Divinity, Extra Attack, and Unarmored Defense
7246: If you already have the Channel Divinity, Extra Attack, or Unarmored Defense features from another class, you don't gain the feature again. Instead, each time you reach a level that grants the feature, you gain the benefits specified for your new class.
7247: Spellcasting
7248: Weave casters use Resonance Points (RP), not multiclass spell slots. Spells known and prepared remain determined for each class individually, as if you were a single-classed member of that class.
7249: Resonance Multiclassing (Approved Canon)
7250: When a hero has two or more Weave-casting classes, maximum RP equals the full pool of the highest caster-tier class, using that class's level, plus 25% of one secondary Weave caster's maximum pool, rounded up. Tertiary and later Weave-casting classes add no further RP. For example, a Wizard 10 / Cleric 6 has the Wizard's 10th-level pool plus ceil(25% of the Cleric's 6th-level pool). Only a class feature or other rule that explicitly changes maximum RP can modify this merged total; reputation and alignment do not alter intrinsic RP capacity. Oracle is not a Weave RP caster. An Oracle uses Revelation Charges (RC) for Mystery Revelations and never contributes an RP pool to this calculation.
7251: 
7252: 
7253: Prestige Classes
7254: While multiclassing allows you to diversify your character's abilities, prestige classes represent specialized training or extraordinary circumstances that transform your character in more profound ways. Unlike core classes, prestige classes cannot be taken at character creation, they must be unlocked through gameplay, representing your character's growth and unique experiences in the world of Aethel.
7255: Unlocking Prestige Classes
7256: There are two primary methods to unlock a prestige class:
7257: Prerequisite Path
7258: This traditional path requires you to meet specific prerequisites:
7259: Reach a minimum level in one or more core classes
7260: Acquire specific feats that demonstrate aptitude
7261: Master relevant skills
7262: Complete a special requirement unique to the prestige class
7263: Discovery Path
7264: This alternative path focuses on narrative discovery:
7265: Encounter a specific event, location, or NPC
7266: Complete a series of thematic challenges or quests
7267: Undergo a transformation or ritual
7268: Demonstrate aptitude through roleplay and character choices
7269: Prestige Class List
7270: The following prestige classes represent specialized paths that characters may discover during their adventures in Aethel. Each offers unique abilities and playstyles that build upon the foundation of the core classes.
7271: Battlemage - Melee-arcane hybrid who weaves spells and swordplay
7272: Beast Master - Animal companion specialist with deep primal bonds
7273: Blademaster - Weapon perfectionist with supernatural martial prowess
7274: Bloodbound - Vampiric predator who feeds on blood to empower abilities
7275: Berserker - Chaos-fueled brute who thrives in the thick of battle
7276: Chronomancer - Manipulator of time who banks and reuses actions
7277: Echomancer - Mystic who mirrors the powers of enemies and monsters
7278: Forge Master - Legendary blacksmith who imbues weapons with mythic properties
7279: Giant Killer - Specialized hunter trained to bring down colossal foes
7280: Gravetouched - Cursed soul balanced between life and death
7281: Hexblade - Weapon-bound curse channeler who weaves doom through strikes
7282: Mooncursed - Lycanthropic warrior whose form varies by curse type
7283: Sapper - Demolition expert and trapmaster who controls the battlefield
7284: Soulrender - Necrotic engine of agony who harvests souls for power
7285: Storm Lord - Tempest-fueled channeler of lightning and wind
7286: Verdant Animist - Summoner of living traps and wild overgrowth
7287: Warlock - Pact-bound caster who channels dark forces from beyond
7288: Wyrmslayer - Hunter forged in fire and scaled blood, bane of dragons
7289: The following pages detail each prestige class, including their unique abilities, unlock requirements, and role in the world of Aethel.
7290: Decennium Descent – Prestige Classes (Classes 1–6)
7291: Each prestige class includes:
7292: Class Overview
7293: Requirements & Unlock Conditions
7294: Signature Mechanics
7295: Capstone Ability
7296: Combat Role
7297: Character Vignette (2–3 paragraphs)
7298: 1. Battlemage
7299: Overview: A melee-arcane hybrid who builds momentum through physical combat to unleash devastating spells.
7300: 
7301: Requirements:
7302: Base Class: Warrior or Mage (Lv 10+)
7303: Feats: Power Attack, Combat Casting, Arcane Momentum
7304: Skills: Spellcraft, Concentration, Weapon Mastery
7305: Alignment: Any non-Lawful Good
7306: 
7307: Unlock: Slay a spellcasting enemy with a melee weapon while concentrating on an active spell.
7308: 
7309: Signature Mechanics:
7310: Battle Flow: Gain 1 stack of Momentum per melee hit (max 5)
7311: Momentum Surge: Spend Momentum to quick-cast or empower spellstrikes
7312: Spellshock Guard: Gain magic resistance after casting in melee
7313: Arcane Grudge: Bonus damage vs enemy casters who injured you
7314: 
7315: Capstone – Arcane Detonation: Consume all Momentum to unleash an AoE elemental blast that knocks back enemies and restores RP equal to the cost of one spell you can cast, up to Tier 5.
7316: 
7317: Combat Role: Frontline disruptor and caster killer. Excels in hybrid burst pressure.
7318: 
7319: Character Story: *The Ember Blade
7320: Tyrellus Fane, once a court mage, found no joy in scrolls or lectures. When invaders stormed his sanctum, desperation forced him to lift a fallen knight’s blade. With a roar and a spell, he became fire and steel. Now he stalks battlefields as the Ember Blade, arcane energy humming through his strikes, seeking the thrill that only war provides. Those who mistake him for a mere battler are consumed in flame and fury.
7321: 2. Beastmaster
7322: Overview: A primal commander who bonds with multiple beasts and leads them in coordinated battle.
7323: 
7324: Requirements:
7325: Base Class: Ranger or Druid (Lv 10+)
7326: Feats: Companion Training, Survivalist, Beast Lore
7327: Skills: Survival, Animal Empathy
7328: 
7329: Unlock: Tame and command three different CR-appropriate beasts during a wilderness trial quest.
7330: 
7331: Signature Mechanics:
7332: Primal Pack: Control up to 3 active companions that scale with level
7333: Shared Instinct: Beast allies benefit from your feat bonuses
7334: Pack Coordination: Use a bonus action to command multiple companions
7335: 
7336: Capstone – Apex Alpha: Once per long rest, your companions act twice per round and inherit your weapon feats temporarily.
7337: 
7338: Combat Role: Flexible frontline or skirmish leader. Best in mid-sized parties or wilderness zones.
7339: 
7340: Character Story: *Of Claws and Kin
7341: Seraya Wanderclaw walks alone, yet never without company. Wolves flank her. An eagle circles above. A bear watches from the shadow of a pine. Raised by the wild after her tribe fell, she learned not to command beasts, but to earn their trust. In battle, they strike as one ,  claw, beak, fang. Where she steps, the forest fights back.
7342: 3. Blademaster
7343: Overview: A weapon perfectionist who executes seamless, deadly strikes through honed martial flow.
7344: 
7345: Requirements:
7346: Base Class: Warrior or Thief (Lv 10+)
7347: Feats: Weapon Focus, Weapon Mastery, Combat Reflexes
7348: Skills: Acrobatics, Weapon Mastery
7349: 
7350: Unlock: Defeat a master duelist without taking more than one hit.
7351: 
7352: Signature Mechanics:
7353: Blade Echo: Every 3rd strike triggers a free maneuver
7354: Parry Counter: Successful dodge grants a retaliatory strike
7355: Precision Flow: Critical hits increase maneuver DC and chance to chain
7356: 
7357: Capstone – Perfect Form: For one round per short rest, all attacks auto-hit if an enemy has already been struck this turn.
7358: 
7359: Combat Role: Single-target DPS and duelist. Excels in agile combat and enemy pressure.
7360: 
7361: Character Story: *The Silver Cut
7362: Kane the Quiet never shouts, never snarls. His blade does it for him. A master of the Thousand Strokes style, he sees the world in openings. One breath, one kill. Some say his sword whispers to him, others that he moves faster than thought. None who faced him have lived long enough to explain.
7363: 4. Bloodbound
7364: Overview: A vampiric predator who feeds on blood to empower attacks, summon thralls, and cheat death.
7365: 
7366: Requirements:
7367: Base Class: Crimson Agent, Thief, or Gravetouched (Lv 10+)
7368: Feats: Hemocraft, Dark Affinity, Life Drinker
7369: Skills: Stealth, Hemocraft, Arcana
7370: 
7371: Unlock: Survive a vampiric infection through a dark relic or story-driven event and accept the curse.
7372: 
7373: Signature Mechanics:
7374: Bloodletting Strikes: Apply Bleed stacks; consume them to heal or deal bonus damage
7375: Crimson Domination: Charm or control a humanoid thrall temporarily
7376: Sanguine Regeneration: Passive healing based on kills and crits
7377: Bloodwalk: Teleport to bleeding enemies in line of sight
7378: Mistform: Once per encounter, avoid death by becoming mist and retreating
7379: 
7380: Capstone – Throne of Night: Transform into a Master Vampire for 1 minute: lifesteal, domination, fear aura, and resistance to nonmagical damage.
7381: 
7382: Combat Role: Sustained fighter with mobility and lifesteal. Dominates small skirmishes.
7383: 
7384: Character Story: *The Crimson Waltz
7385: Lira Vey once danced for kings. Her voice seduced nobles; her smile masked poison. But it was her final performance that earned her immortality. The vampire lord saw her elegance and claimed her. Now she glides through battlefields as effortlessly as she did across marble halls, draining blood with every step. She is as feared for her kiss as for her claws.
7386: 5. Berserker
7387: Overview: A chaos-fueled brute who thrives in the thick of battle, dealing AoE damage and overwhelming force.
7388: 
7389: Requirements:
7390: Base Class: Warrior or Shaman (Lv 10+)

/**
 * Hero paths that deliberately violate the normal Dissonance safety cutoff.
 * They remain tied to their parent class mechanic; this is not a generic caster option.
 */
export const DARK_RESONANCE_PATHS = {
  Lich: {
    baseClass: "Mage",
    mechanic: "Soul Furnace",
    principle: "Force the Weave through accumulated Dissonance, accepting direct magical trauma rather than losing access to ranked casting.",
  },
  Necromancer: {
    baseClass: "Wizard",
    mechanic: "Grave Conductor",
    principle: "Use death, corpses, and bound undead as resonators while forcing spells through Dissonance that would stop an ordinary caster.",
  },
};

export function isDarkResonancePath(heroPath) {
  return Boolean(heroPath && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(DARK_RESONANCE_PATHS, heroPath));
}

export function getClassCoreMechanic(className) {
  return CLASS_CORE_MECHANICS[className] || null;
}

export default CLASS_CORE_MECHANICS;

FILE docs/audit/2026-10-08/director_main_vs_review.diff.txt
--- ReviewBible DM-01

+++ main DM-01

@@ -69,7 +69,7 @@

 The Cycle. Once a decade, the Shardspire activates and the Decennium's hundred floors accept mortal expeditions again. Between Cycles, the dungeon is sealed; only rumor, old maps, and the last Cycle's survivors carry knowledge forward. A Director should treat each new Cycle as a chance to reshuffle: a Gate Guardian's domain may have shifted in emphasis, a faction that dominated the last Cycle may have fallen from favor, a haven that was safe may now be a ruin. Name the Cycle after whoever or whatever most shaped it — "the Cycle of the Broken Warded Gate," "the Third Choralith Cycle" — and let players feel the weight of history stacking up.
 The Opening — a calendar, not just a countdown. One hundred days is brief enough to feel desperate and long enough to develop a shape. Treat it in three movements: the rush (hours 0–480, roughly days 1–20, when expeditions race for early floors and fresh rumors), the grind (hours 480–1200, roughly days 21–50, when serious companies push depth and the surface absorbs the first loot and casualties), and the reckoning (hours 1200–2400, roughly days 51–100, everything after the Horn). Mark visible beats on the calendar: a haven running short of supplies, a market reacting to a windfall, or a rival Expedition returning in triumph or ruin.
-The Horn — the point of no return. At hour 1,200, day 50, the Horn sounds at the exact midpoint of the Opening. After it, known extraction routes become less reliable, rescue contracts thin out, and NPC allies begin making final decisions about whether to push deeper or turn upward. The Horn does not seal the dungeon by itself, but it ends the illusion that every Expedition can still recover from one more mistake.
+The Horn — the point of no return. At hour 1200, day 50, the Horn sounds at the exact midpoint of the Opening. After it, known extraction routes become less reliable, rescue contracts thin out, and NPC allies begin making final decisions about whether to push deeper or turn upward. The Horn does not seal the dungeon by itself, but it ends the illusion that every Expedition can still recover from one more mistake.
 The seal. At hour 2400, day 100, the Decennium seals. Anyone still inside is not automatically killed; survivors may be trapped until the next Cycle, ten years later. Telegraph the closing repeatedly. A seal that arrives without warning is not tension; it is an ambush by the rules.
-Director canon for the tabletop game is a 2,400-hour Opening (100 days) with the Horn at hour 1,200 (day 50). Do not compress the Opening to 240/120 — that figure was a typo. Use smaller local clocks for patrol resets, torch life, collapsing rooms, or a single delve, but give those clocks distinct names so they are never confused with the Horn.
+Director canon for the tabletop game is a 2400-hour Opening (100 days) with the Horn at hour 1200 (day 50). Do not compress the Opening to 240/120 — that figure was a typo. Use smaller local clocks for patrol resets, torch life, collapsing rooms, or a single delve, but give those clocks distinct names so they are never confused with the Horn.
 Chapter 4 — Expeditions at the Table
 This is the Player Rulebook's own structure, not a Director invention — treat it as binding, and use this chapter to actually run it, because most tables under-use it.
@@ -282,7 +282,7 @@

 Session Zero for Decennium Descent
 Session zero must do more than approve characters. It establishes how the Expedition functions as an organization and how the table handles pressure, secrecy, loss, and conflict.
-1. State the campaign scope. Tell the players whether you expect a one-shot, a first-push campaign, a full 2,400-hour Opening, a legacy campaign, or another agreed form.
+1. State the campaign scope. Tell the players whether you expect a one-shot, a first-push campaign, a full 2400-hour Opening, a legacy campaign, or another agreed form.

That joint-creation story is the complete public explanation at campaign start. @@ -916,18 +916,4 @@

 Design villains and mysteries that rhyme with the machine, without stating it. The best original antagonists in this setting want dissonance kept alive for their own reasons that happen to serve the Pact's actual function, whether they know it or not. A temple zealot who provokes unnecessary wars "to test the faithful," a merchant who profits specifically from prolonged Expedition rivalries, a Custodian-descended noble who suppresses evidence that would calm two warring factions — none of these need to know about the Heartcell to function as excellent, thematically resonant content.
 A closing design checklist for new content.
-The Matrix as an Invisible Design Language
-The Matrix of Oblivion should shape what players experience long before they can name it. When designing a floor, faction, relic, social conflict, or campaign arc, decide which link in the living chain is under pressure. Do not label the link at the table; make the party live through it.
-Possibility. Present multiple plausible futures, routes, interpretations, or alliances. A room with one correct answer tests knowledge; a room with several survivable answers tests possibility.
-Choice. Make at least one meaningful option exclude another. If every reward can be collected with enough time, the machine has produced activity but not will.
-Difference. Put identities, factions, copies, ideologies, or memories into contact without flattening them into interchangeable pieces. Difference is the first thing Unity tries to dissolve.
-Conflict. Conflict can be combat, law, love, debt, doctrine, inheritance, or incompatible needs. Do not reduce it to violence; preserve the fact that two real wants cannot both fully win.
-Motion. Stagnation must have a price. Clocks, migrating hazards, rival expeditions, shifting routes, and political reactions keep a solved situation from becoming permanent stillness.
-Time. Deadlines convert preference into commitment. The 2,400-hour Opening and the Horn at hour 1,200 are the setting’s largest expression of this principle, but smaller clocks should appear everywhere.
-History. Let previous choices alter current options. Scars on a floor, inherited debts, dead NPCs, rebuilt havens, and rival claims prove that the world remembers action.
-Memory. Make information preservable, corruptible, tradable, stealable, and sometimes costly to keep. A forgotten truth is not just missing lore; it can be a mechanical loss of identity.
-Identity. Ask who a character, faction, or community becomes after adapting. Copies, masks, oaths, mutations, titles, and reputation all matter because sameness is closer to the zero-state than contradiction.
-Will. Put characters in situations where refusing, sacrificing, persisting, or walking away says something that no die roll can say for them. Will is choice maintained under cost.
-Life. The final question is not whether a creature has hit points; it is whether continued existence still contains distinct desire, memory, relationship, and unfinished possibility. Endgame choices should make the party defend—or redefine—that proposition.
-When several links appear in one scenario, the content will feel unmistakably like Decennium Descent even if the Heartcell is never mentioned. This is the preferred method: the players should experience the equation before they ever discover it.
 Does it introduce a real choice, not just a fight?
 Does it create at least one lasting consequence either way?

 Chapter 40 — Multi-Cycle Play and Legacy
@@ -1762,20 +1748,8 @@

 War as Dissonance
 The Divine Pact’s deeper function does not mean every war is secretly ordered by Heaven or Hell. Most conflict is mortal, sincere, and destructive. The setting’s horror is that the machinery benefits from difference and struggle regardless of who began it. Keep responsibility with the characters and institutions that choose violence. Cosmic function is not moral absolution.
-False Resonance and Unity
-Unity is the Director term for a counterfeit form of harmony that appears to resolve suffering by making difference disappear. It can feel compassionate, efficient, peaceful, equal, or inevitable. That appearance is why it is dangerous. True resonance permits distinct voices to remain distinct while relating to one another; false resonance rewards systems in which every choice converges toward the same answer, every institution toward the same dependency, and every identity toward a quieter copy of the whole.
-False resonance attacks the Matrix chain from the middle outward. It reduces Difference, which removes meaningful Conflict; without conflict, Motion and consequential History thin; memory becomes interchangeable narrative, Identity loses edges, Will becomes procedural consent, and life approaches the zero-state without recognizing it as death. A Unity movement therefore does not need to preach annihilation. It may sincerely promise that no one will ever have to disagree again.
-Do not make every peace movement, equality doctrine, bank, guild, or collectivist institution a Unity vector. That would flatten the setting into paranoia and erase the distinction the cosmology depends on. Unity appears when a system demands convergence—when peace requires sameness, equality requires the destruction of meaningful agency, wealth makes every participant dependent on one promise, or safety requires every future choice to be surrendered.
-The Goblin Question
-Goblins are metaphysically more susceptible to Unity than most mortal peoples. This is a vulnerability, not a moral alignment and not proof of guilt. Most goblins never know the term Unity. Many live ordinary lives untouched by it. Some recognize its patterns and become among its fiercest opponents. The susceptibility matters because goblin cultures are exceptionally good at building adaptive networks of obligation, salvage, credit, promises, and mutual reliance—the exact social machinery false resonance can exploit when it turns from relationship into totalizing dependency.
-Goblin financiers are major backers of Decennial expeditions and helped create the Aethel banking conglomerate. They finance equipment, buy future salvage claims, insure expeditions, fund mercenary companies, and extend war credit. None of those activities is inherently corrupt. The Director danger is that a sufficiently captured institution can spread false resonance through perfectly ordinary transactions: a promise that removes alternatives, a debt that converts identity into obligation, a peace fund that quietly requires every faction to surrender the capacity to disagree, or a prosperity scheme whose beneficiaries can no longer exist outside it.
-The ancient anti-goblin crusade must remain morally difficult. Do not present it as automatically enlightened, and do not present it as obviously irrational persecution. The crusaders may have correctly identified goblin institutions as major vectors of an existential infection and may have concluded that eradication was the only available containment method. At the same time, susceptibility was never universal culpability: uninvolved families, resistant communities, reformers, and active enemies of Unity were swept into the same category. The campaign question is not “were the crusaders secretly right about every goblin?” They were not. The harder question is whether a civilization can face a real species-skewed existential risk without turning risk into collective guilt.
-For play, give goblin NPCs positions on this history. A banker may deny the old charges, a crusader-descended archivist may carry evidence of genuine capture, a goblin reformer may hate both the financiers and the crusaders, and a Unity cultist may weaponize the memory of persecution to make legitimate scrutiny socially impossible. Never let one NPC stand in for the entire people.
-Moloch and the Coinfather Pattern
-Moloch did not invent goblin civilization, commerce, or ambition. He recognized a vulnerability and learned to exploit it. His Coinfather pattern turns value into surveillance, obligation into identity, and prosperity into a chain whose participants gradually lose the practical ability to choose anything outside the system. Some goblin financiers have knowingly served Moloch. Far more have reproduced pieces of the pattern without understanding its origin, because a profitable institution can preserve a metaphysical design long after its founders forget why it was built.
-Use Moloch sparingly and at high leverage. Most questionable loans are just loans; most wars are mortal wars. Evidence of the Coinfather pattern should require repeated structural clues—identical contract geometry across unrelated institutions, debt instruments that behave like ritual objects, promises of peace or equality whose language erases alternatives, or financial networks that continue making the same choices after every individual decision-maker has been replaced.
 Pact Intervention
 Direct divine or infernal intervention should be rare, formal, and constrained. Agents prefer doctrine, contracts, omens, legal rulings, chosen champions, and faction pressure. When a being tied to the Pact acts openly, show the cost of acting without breaking the agreement. This preserves the setting’s bureaucratic liturgy and prevents cosmology from becoming arbitrary omnipotence.
 Folk Religion
-The Fifteenfold Pantheon and Wheel of Testimony may shape law, ritual, class identity, and culture. Treat them as mortal religious frameworks containing real experiences and incomplete interpretations. NPCs can be devout without being secretly foolish. Their rites may work because divine powers answer, because the Weave responds, because institutions preserve effective practices, or because several explanations overlap.
+The Fifteenfold Pantheon and Great Wheel may shape law, ritual, class identity, and culture. Treat them as mortal religious frameworks containing real experiences and incomplete interpretations. NPCs can be devout without being secretly foolish. Their rites may work because divine powers answer, because the Weave responds, because institutions preserve effective practices, or because several explanations overlap.
 Chapter 43 — Downtime, Craft, and Research Chains
 The Player Rulebook supplies the foundations of harvesting and crafting. The Director’s task is to turn those procedures into campaign choices. A chain should begin with a question or desired capability, require access and components, consume Opening time or inter-adventure time, and produce both an answer and a consequence.

FILE DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/CHANGELOG_THEME_PROPAGATION_2026-07-31.md
# Theme Propagation & Investor Repository Pass — 2026-07-31

This pass applies the current Decennium Descent canon across the product and turns the working repository into a compact investor/development package.

## 1. Core canon propagated

- Preserved the public Crucible tradition: Calan'thir and Asmodeus genuinely built and governed the known Crucible.
- Preserved the deeper Director truth: the Heartcell predates the Crucible and was created by the Silent Ones / Old Ones.
- Added the Matrix of Oblivion as an explicit Director design grammar: Possibility → Choice → Difference → Conflict → Motion → Time → History → Memory → Identity → Will → Life.
- Added Director guidance that players should experience the Matrix before they can name it.
- Added Unity / false resonance as counterfeit harmony that destroys meaningful distinction rather than ordinary peace itself.
- Added explicit guardrails against treating ordinary peace, equality, banks, guilds, or collective institutions as automatic Unity vectors.

## 2. Goblin canon integrated at the correct information layer

### Player layer
- Reframed goblins as survivors, brokers, builders, financiers, insurers, expedition backers, and institutional actors.
- Added goblin involvement in the Aethel banking conglomerate and Decennial expedition finance.
- Added the ancient anti-goblin crusade as disputed history with incompatible surviving interpretations.
- Preserved goblin internal diversity, reformers, dissidents, and distrust of concentrated obligation.

### Director layer
- Added goblins' elevated metaphysical susceptibility to Unity.
- Explicitly separated susceptibility from morality, guilt, or universal complicity.
- Added Moloch's Coinfather pattern: value → surveillance; obligation → identity; prosperity → dependency.
- Preserved the difficult crusade question: crusaders may have recognized a genuine existential vector without making collective extermination morally or factually equivalent to universal goblin culpability.

## 3. Player Rulebook aligned to the setting

- Added **Peoples of the Opening**, tying all fourteen peoples to the Decennial cycle rather than presenting them as generic fantasy cultures detached from the central premise.
- Standardized current underground terminology around **Umbrin** and the **Murk**.
- Removed the orphan Fighter chapter and made the **17 selectable base-class** roster authoritative.
- Added **Hero Paths Are Philosophies, Not Moral Verdicts**.
- Removed Good / Neutral / Evil labels from Hero Path headers while retaining path identity and consequences.
- Standardized the Player Rulebook on **Director** instead of mixing DM, GM, and Game Master language.
- Replaced remaining named legacy spells and external-rulebook references with Aethel-neutral language.
- Reframed player cosmography as mortal interpretation rather than omniscient objective truth.
- Renamed major inherited planar labels in player-facing material, including **Wheel of Testimony**, **Verdant Reflection**, **Veiled Expanse**, and the **Nine Infernal Courts** family of terminology.
- Cleaned several obvious inherited proper names from racial naming examples.

## 4. Resonance architecture consolidated

- Weave casters now consistently use **Resonance Points** where the current rules call for them.
- Oracle remains a deliberate exception using **Revelation Charges**.
- Legacy spell-slot wording was removed from relevant class features, recovery rules, prestige/capstone language, and multiclass explanations.
- Mirrorsage storage terminology remains distinct where a "slot" is a literal storage mechanic rather than a casting resource.

## 5. Director book expanded

Added or strengthened:

- **The Matrix as an Invisible Design Language**
- **False Resonance and Unity**
- **The Goblin Question**
- **Moloch and the Coinfather Pattern**
- public-vs-secret cosmology boundaries
- conflict/war-as-dissonance guardrails that deny cosmic function as moral absolution
- Wheel of Testimony language for mortal planar models

## 6. Bestiary naming and publication-status cleanup

- Propagated Aethel naming across canonical Excel sources, generated JSON, Bestiary, and Codex.
- Removed targeted high-recognition legacy identifiers and half-reskinned family/rank names.
- Finalized Aethel rank naming including:
  - Hush-Broker Scriptor / Chainmaw / Manyclaw / Mirefin / Spearbound / Dreadwing / Plaguehand / Longarm
  - Order-Drone Unit / Pair / Triad / Tetrad / Quintet / Covenant Engine
  - Spiral Breach Gnawer / Brood-Sage / Ripper / Colossus / Augur
- Updated the defensive scrub script so repeated runs do not reintroduce doubled prefixes.
- Verified the two bestiary workbooks contain no targeted retired rank names, doubled prefixes, or spreadsheet formula-error strings.
- Added `docs/development/BESTIARY_PUBLICATION_STATUS.md`.
- Corrected the Bestiary and Codex so they no longer imply that compatibility/prototype creature shells are already final commercial publication copy.

**Important:** the bestiary pipeline is intentionally still a development scaffold. Many ordinary creature concepts/stat shells need a full independent authorship/provenance/publication pass. The investor repository now says this explicitly instead of overstating completion.

## 7. Repository cleanup

The extracted working repository was reduced from roughly **919 MB** to roughly **14 MB** while retaining the current books, canon, mechanics, structured source data, app source, maintained tooling, and curated playtest evidence.

Removed from the distributable package:

- `node_modules/`
- `.next/`, `out/`, generated local builds
- approximately 163 MB of third-party D&D/Pathfinder reference PDFs
- private/local backups and pre-edit Word files
- redundant modular Word documents already compiled into current books
- superseded Director drafts
- completed one-off migration/patch scripts
- raw temporary analysis output
- stale generated fragments and obsolete content generators
- Python cache folders

The remaining repository contains **224 files** before ZIP packaging.

- Production Next.js build could not complete in the packaging sandbox because the package mirror returned 404 while Next.js attempted to retrieve its Linux SWC binary.

- Maintained `scripts/` / `tools/` reference audit: **0 missing referenced paths**.
- Bestiary source ingest: **708 Excel rows / 707 live JSON rows / 1 hidden row skipped**.
- Targeted Player/Director/Cosmology/Bestiary/Codex terminology scan completed after edits.

## 10. Document render QA

Latest edited books were rendered through the DOCX QA workflow. Complete PDFs were produced for the Player Rulebook, Director's Crucible, Chronicle, Cosmology, Bestiary, and Codex. Large Player/Codex PNG rasterization exceeded the sandbox time limit after the PDFs had already completed; targeted pages from the completed PDFs were then rendered and visually inspected.

Inspected sections included:

No clipping or overlap was found in the inspected edited sections.

## 11. Protected open canon questions

This pass does **not** invent answers to intentionally unresolved canon, including:

- the exact relationship of the Level 97 Old God encounter to the Silent Ones beyond current locks;
- the precise threshold at which peace/stillness becomes existentially dangerous;
- any still-open authorship detail the current canon briefs explicitly preserve as unresolved.

Those remain development questions rather than accidental contradictions.

## 12. Magic & ability canon reconciliation

A second rules pass reconciled the complete spell/ability layer without flattening class identity.

- Rebuilt the spell source of truth around **848 unique named spells** and **936 school/rank placements**.
- Preserved intentional shared-spell overlap: **619 spells** currently have different acquisition levels across classes. A spell exists once; class training determines school/thread access and when its Resonance Rank becomes learnable.
- Locked all **17 base-class core mechanics** as protected design contracts. Resonance/Dissonance wraps those engines rather than replacing Crescendo, Conviction, Blood Gauge, Momentum, Shapeshifting, Nuke Chains, Ki, Echo Slots, Revelation Charges, Track Target, Totems, Palisades, Sneak Attack/Poison, Deployables, Mutation Slots, Spell Matrix, or Paladin Oaths.
- Established **Resonance Points** as magical capacity and **Dissonance/Refrain** as the universal anti-spam law of ordinary Weave casting. Immediate repetition increases Refrain strain, including repeated cantrips.
- Removed alignment and faction reputation from intrinsic spell pricing/capacity. Reputation may unlock teachers, focuses, sanctuaries, components, research, and explicit boons; it does not rewrite the Weave's physics.
- Preserved Oracle as a separate Revelation caster. **Revelation Charges do not automatically recover on rests**; Mystery Prophecy Triggers and explicit Revelation features control RC income.
- Reconciled Druid Shapeshifting as a protected non-spell resource with its own uses/day and CR progression. Shapeshifting has no default RP activation cost and creates no passive RP regeneration.
- Added the explicit dark-path exception: **Lich** retains the Mage/Nuke Chain identity and **Necromancer** retains the Wizard/Spell Matrix identity, but both may deliberately force ranked magic through Discordant/Fractured states via **Traumatic Casting**, taking direct metaphysical backlash rather than receiving an "evil discount."
- Removed remaining player-facing spell-slot/mana residue from the reconciled class/feat/resource text; compatibility IDs may remain internally where required for saved data.
- Current structured ability inventory validates **334 feats, 10 Paladin Oaths, 10 Oracle Mysteries, and 10 Oracle Curses** alongside the 17 protected class engines.
- Added reproducible validation commands: `npm run validate:magic`, `npm run validate:abilities`, and the combined `npm run validate:rules`.
- Final spell/class validation at packaging: **PASS — 848 spells / 936 placements / 619 varied acquisition cases / 17 base classes**.
